The script is in the client's precompiled lua pack (not in the client source); under CN the client also counts listings in status 1, the server only the ones on sale (CN is not a target). The fee field of the request is read and not used: before, the server paid whatever it said, so 0 listed for free and a number below 0 gave merets. The fee is stored at once, and goes back when the listing could not be stored.
  - Chosen here: a request whose fee differs from the server's is charged the server's fee without a notice (an unchanged client always sends the same number).
- **Design market price range:** the price of a new listing and of one put on sale again is kept inside the register dialog's range: the constants UGCShopSellMinPrice and UGCShopSellMaxPrice, a design item's own range from the design table where it is above 0, and a music score's lowest price from the client script calcMarketSellPriceMinMaxMusicScore (15; 100 under LOCALE=CN; the two were the wrong way round before). A music score cannot be put on sale: the server lists only items with a design template, and the client's score market (feature UGCMarketMusicScore) is off in KR. Before, putting a listing on sale again took any price, also one below 0, which paid the buyer.
- **Design market refusals:** only a design whose look was made by the player's account can be put on sale (the dialog offers no other). Only a listing on sale can be bought: one taken off sale, past its sale time or with a price below 0 gets the purchase failure answer; before, any listing id could be bought. A purchase (design or premium) makes the item before it takes the price, and a design sale that cannot be stored gives the merets back; before, a product whose item could not be made was paid for and the handler threw.
- **Design market cancel and refund:** the client's meret market has no refund request at all (the modes it sends are 11 to 15, 18 to 20, 22 to 25, 27, 29, 30, 32, 33, 101 to 104 and 107). The cancel of the bill dialog sends nothing. The sales window sends 15 ("stop the sale", after a confirm box) only for a listing on sale and 14 ("delete") only for one that is not on sale; for one on sale it shows a notice instead. The server now keeps to that: 15 for a listing that is not on sale and 14 for one on sale do nothing (before, 14 deleted a listing on sale and 15 wrote the expired state again). Neither gives the register fee back, as the client shows none.
- **Change attributes:** the ForceFill sub-type is 8 and the debug remake sub-type is 7.
- **Change attributes sale (SaleRemake):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the rate out of 10000, value2 the item class: 0 all, 1 equip types 13 to 17, 2 types 12 and 18 to 21, 3 pets) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the rate, the class as a byte. The client keeps a rate per class, takes the higher of the item's class and class 0 (`gamerule.cpp`, GetRemakeSaleRate) and lowers the ingredient counts of the staged item's reply itself (`itemremakescrolldata.cpp:397`: the rate held to 10000 x IngredientFeeReduceLimit, then (count * (1 - rate / 10000) + 0.000001) in doubles cut to a whole number, at least 1), so that reply keeps the full counts and the change consumes the lowered ones (`EventSaleRules.RemakeIngredientCount`). KR has 23 rows of rate 3000 or 5000, all class 0, some in a part of the day only, all ended (the last 2021-09-09). Of several running events of one class the client keeps the highest rate (`gamerule.h:85`, Recompute), and so does the server. The limit is 0.5 when the constants lack IngredientFeeReduceLimit, as in the client (the KR table has 0.5). The client takes the higher of this rate and the character's fee attribute, ReduceRemakeOptionFee (169) for an item and ReducePetRemakeOptionFee (173) for a pet, as a rate out of 10000 (`itemremakescrolldata.cpp:361` and `:379`), and so does the server (`EventSaleRules.RemakeDiscount`). The item dialog shows the full counts with the sale line and its rate under them and uses the lowered counts only to test whether the player can pay (`itemremakeoptiondialog.cpp:776` and `:836`); the pet tab shows the lowered counts (`tabcontentspetremakeoption.cpp:645`).
- **Special attribute hot time (SAHotTime):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the special attribute ids, value2 their values, comma lists of one length; value4 the text of the HUD icon's tooltip) and the event list sends it: the event's id and the four values as strings. The client (`gameevent.cpp:664`) keeps a value of a rate attribute (valueType 2 in `table/specialabilitystringtable.xml`) as value x 1000 cut to a whole number and any other as the whole number, sums the running events per attribute (`gamerule.cpp:2896`) and adds the sums to the character's special attribute totals (`CEquipment::GetAbilityTotal`, `equipment.cpp:740`); the upper HUD shows an icon with the tooltip while one runs (`uiupperhuddialog.cpp:876`). The server adds the same sums to every player's totals while the events run (`StatsManager.AddHotTime`). KR has 83 rows, all ended; the last with values is 13100006 (attributes 1, 59 and 61, 1 each).
  - Chosen here: the rate attributes come from the played client's table, and without a client pack every attribute counts as a rate (the 12 the KR rows name are rates); a row whose lists differ in length, name an unknown attribute or add nothing is not stored; the PC room variant of the event is not sent; the totals follow an event's start and end (dates, days of the week, part of the day) within a second.
- **Dungeon clear bonus (DungeonBonusReward):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<ms2><v dungeonID groupType mesoRate expRate itemRolls customDropBoxIds/></ms2>`, the root's first element as in `gameevent.cpp:996`; groupType is the group's name) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the room, the group, the two rates, the rolls and the box ids. The client keeps the running events per room and per group and merges them (rolls and rates summed, boxes joined, `gameeventcontext.cpp:786`), and shows only the items of the event boxes, marked as event items, in the room window's reward preview (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:976`); the rates and the rolls are for the server. A clear with a reward count left now applies the merged bonus of the running events that name the room or its group (`EventSaleRules.DungeonBonus`): exp and mesos plus `(amount * rate + 0.001)` cut to a whole number (the client's `ApplyExpRate` and `ApplyMesoRate`), the room's clear boxes rolled once more per roll, each event box rolled once, and the record's event flag set. KR has 128 rows, all ended (the last stored ones 2022-07-07; the 15 rows to 2023-02-22 are under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out): 87 with itemRolls 1 (82 by room, 5 by group), 40 with expRate 9, 1 with both rates and a roll at 1; none names a box.
  - Chosen here: a rate adds to the room's amount (a rate of 1 doubles it, as the row with all three at 1 reads); an event that names both the room and its group counts once; at most 10 extra rolls (`MaxDungeonBonusRolls`); a row that names no room or group, or adds nothing, is not stored. The result dialog shows the event line (see Dungeon clear result).
- **Item collection (CollectItemGroup):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<ms2><info name/><result itemID count grade/><condition itemID count grade/>...</ms2>`, `gameevent.cpp:6474`) and the event list sends it: the event's id twice, the name, begin and end, the condition items and the result. The client's window lists up to 3 running events that have 2 to 5 conditions, ticks a condition the player has (the count of that id and rank in the bag or, for a count of 1, the id worn; `uicollectitemgroupeventdialog.cpp:292`) and sends EventReward 19 with the event id from its button. The server now handles 19: the running event of that id, not received yet (user value 2200 of the event), every condition held by the client's test; it gives the result (bag, else mail) and sets the user value to 1, which the window reads as received. The value is stored with the reward instead of at logout, and it follows the event's end when a later replay ingest moves the event, so the result is given once per run. An item row without a grade is read as rank 1, the client's default. The client has no reply case for 19 and refreshes on the user value, so a refusal sends nothing. KR has 22 rows, all ended (the last stored one 2022-04-14; the two rows to 2023-03-09 are under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out), badge upgrades from a lower badge plus tokens.
  - Chosen here: the condition items stay with the player. The client only tests that they are there, counts a worn piece and shows no cost; nothing in it says what the live server took. The claim is kept for the account (the client's user value list does not say whose it is), so one set of items pays an account once, whichever character holds it; a set that can be traded can still pay another account. Rows without a result, with an item without an id or a count, or outside 2 to 5 conditions are not stored (the window shows none of them).
- **Skill damage:** sub-types 7 and 8 write the client's fields; dot damage (sub-type 3) writes the amount as a long.
- **Npc damage to players:** an npc (not a pet) hitting a player now uses a factor of 2.85 instead of 4 in front of Rate x physical or magical attack x resistance / defense; players and pets keep 4. The factor was fitted to KMS2 hits of ordinary field mobs on players: 8 npc and player groups from 4 client builds (player levels 15 to 50, npcs 0 to 10 levels below), where the largest hit was 0.667 to 0.728 of what the factor 4 gives; within each npc the ratio did not move with Rate or the player's defense, and it follows the player's resistance as the formula has it, (1500 - resistance) / 1500: in KMS2 one npc skill on players of magical resistance 185, 255, 460 and 530 hits for 0.947, 0.790 and 0.736 of the first (the formula: 0.947, 0.791, 0.738), and with that term the constant stays between 2.78 and 2.89 for player resistances from 21 to 376. So only the constant was off. Chosen here: one constant 2.85 (4 x 0.71); 3 x 0.95 (the papRate for one player in the server table npcStatFactorByPlayerCount) fits these field hits equally well, but open fields name no factor id, so the table is applied on its own only where a room or instance field names one (next bullet); player hits on npcs were not measured and keep 4; some dungeon and boss npcs hit for much less in KMS2 than their stats give and are not covered by this constant. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc hit spread:** an npc's (not a pet's) hit is now spread evenly between 0.96 and 1 of the amount the formula gives; before, every hit of one npc skill on the same player was the same number. In KMS2, hits of one npc skill on a player whose defense and resistances stay the same vary: in the 7 such groups of 9 to 27 hits with a largest hit of 300 or more (98 hits), the smallest hit is 0.962 to 0.975 of the largest and the hits fill that span evenly. The 2.85 above was fitted to the largest hit, so the spread goes down from it. The same sessions agree with two parts that are unchanged: a critical npc hit is 1.5 of a normal one (36 groups, 41 critical hits at 1.46 to 1.53 of the largest normal hit, bar two on amounts under 25; the npcs' critical damage stat is 500), and at npc critical rate 62 against critical evasion 50 the critical share is 42 of 1,054 hits (4.0 %; the formula gives 4.9 %). The client only shows the amount it is sent, so the span is measured, not read. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Player hit rate:** the chance that an attack is not evaded is (accuracy - 10) / (evasion + 0.8 x accuracy) x 2, now plus up to 0.05 from the attacker's luck (0.05 x luck / (luck + 50)). The code subtracted that luck term and added the same term of the target's dexterity, but divided both as whole numbers, so both were always 0; the luck term now counts and is added, the dexterity term is left out (unmeasured; this changes nothing). No npc has a luck stat (0 of the 8,183 npc rows), so npc hits on players are as before. Fitted to KMS2 player hits on npcs (accuracy 82, luck 18 to 26, 8,682 hits): the misses at evasion 82, 86, 87, 91, 97 and 103 are 0.7 %, 2.6 %, 3.1 %, 5.2 %, 11.5 % and 13.0 %; the rule now gives 1.1 %, 3.7 %, 3.9 %, 6.7 %, 10.1 % and 13.3 %, without the luck it gave 2.4 %, 5.0 %, 5.6 %, 8.0 %, 11.4 % and 14.6 %. The size of the luck term is chosen here: it is the old term with its sign turned, and at low evasion KMS2 still misses a little less than it gives. Not settled: at evasion 97 the rate without the luck (11.4 %) is nearer to KMS2 (11.5 %) than the new one (10.1 %); the other five points are nearer with it. The critical rate needed no change: 736 of 10,126 KMS2 player hits on npcs are critical where the rule gives 761, and a critical hit is 1.25 to 1.27 times a normal one at critical damage 250.
- **Npc stats by player count:** the server table npcStatFactorByPlayerCount is ingested (rows whose feature is off and the file's empty rows are skipped). An npc spawned in a dungeon room with a playerCountFactorID, or in an instance field with an npcStatFactorID, gets the row for (factor id, npc class, player count): health, physical and magical attack, defense and critical rate each become stat x rate + value, cut to a whole number. No row for the id or the npc's class leaves the npc as it is, and a count above the factor's largest playerCount takes that largest row. The recorded hits agree: npcs in rooms with factor 1000 (papRate 0.95 for one player) hit for 0.95 of the field mobs' ratio, and those of instance field 1100001 (factor 2001, papRate 0.1) for 0.1 of it. The client only reads the two ids (dungeonroom.cpp, fieldwardata.cpp) and nothing in it says how the count is picked. Chosen here: the player count is the room's size when it was made (the party's members, 1 solo) or the players in the field at spawn, whichever is larger, since a room's npcs spawn before anyone enters; it is taken once, so npcs already spawned keep their stats when someone joins or leaves; a count with no row of its own takes the nearest lower row, or the lowest row when it is below all of them (factor 5001 starts at 4 players); the room's id wins over the instance field's, because a room id is set per dungeon room (one map can serve rooms of several difficulties) while the instance id is per map, and no room in the data has a field whose instance field names an id; player pets are never scaled, and neither is an npc a player summons with an item; npcs summoned by other npcs are scaled like the room's own. The npc damage factor 2.85 is unchanged. Needs a re-ingest (a server without the table refuses to start in Release, like every server table). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **AI select useNpcProb:** a select node with useNpcProb="1" now picks its children by the npc's own skill probs (npc xml `<skill probs>`, now ingested next to the ids and levels; needs a re-ingest), child i by the prob of skill i + 1, a child past the npc's skills never. Before, the node's prob list was used, which the parser fills with a single 100 when the attribute is absent, so such a select always took its first child: the 38 AI files with useNpcProb (AI_Default among them) (one branch per skill index under the top select, 2 or 3 of them) only ever used skill 1. Chosen here: the child-to-skill mapping is by position, as the branches are laid out in those files (two files, BossDungeon/BeetleBigYellow/AI_Default.xml and AI_DefaultNew_AttackAfterRunaway.xml, also name other skill indexes inside a branch); an npc with no probs (data ingested before this) keeps the old behaviour. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Skill autoTargeting turn:** an npc cast of a skill whose motion faces the target now turns to the target by the client's autoTargeting check (`PCBase::vfunc_101`, `FUN_141679800`): the target must be within a cylinder around the caster, radius autoTargetingMaxDistance (a plain ground distance) and height autoTargetingMaxHeight (150 when 0), and within a cone of autoTargetingMaxDegree degrees, the whole width, around the facing on the ground plane (dot >= cos(degree / 2 in radians)); all three must hold. Before, the degree went into Cos as radians, the squared distance was compared to MaxDistance, the height check took a ground axis, and the distance and height checks were ORed. The npc skills' blocks are almost all the default 210 / 0 / 0, so their turn changes little. Chosen here: a radius of 0, for which the client takes the skill's attack range, sets no limit; the cylinder is centred on the caster in both height cases and the target is taken as a point (the client centres it only for a set height and tests the target's collision volume). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc standby facing:** a standby node with faceTarget="1" (AI_Default has one after each skill) now turns the npc to its battle target when the standby starts; before, the standby ignored the attribute and the next skill started on the old heading. Skills whose motion does not face the target (such as a lunge that moves the caster forward) then went past the player: one test field mob hit on 13 of 95 casts. In recorded KMS fights most field-mob casts near the player point at the player (about 80 to 90 of 100) and most engaged casts hit. Chosen here: only the value 1 turns the npc (3 and absent do not); the turn is the plain direction to the target, on the same spot the facing is kept. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Npc cast facing:** an npc's skill use now carries the npc's facing as its rotation, and the npc turns to its target (or to the AI's face position) before the cast is sent instead of after it. The client takes a cast's facing from the rotation's z (`CSceneStateGame::OnSkillUse`); KMS2 sends an npc cast with a zero direction and the rotation (0, 0, facing) (all of 4,155 npc casts), and a boss's cast at a player points at that player within 15 degrees in 290 of 381 first motions that face the target. Before, the rotation of every npc cast was zero unless the npc itself faced zero (the test for a missing rotation looked at the caster's instead of the cast's), and the control entry sent with the cast still had the old heading. Splash and cube skills an npc cast places take their rotation from the cast as well. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **AI skill node without faceTarget:** a skill node that writes no faceTarget (19722 of the 32397 skill nodes; the rest write 0, 1 or 3) now turns the npc to its target at the cast, as faceTarget="1" does; a written faceTarget="0" still leaves the facing to the motion's own faceTarget and the autoTargeting check. The parser reads both as 0, so the ingest tells them apart from the AI file (`AiSkillFace`; needs a re-ingest). KMS2 sends show the difference: a boss whose skill nodes write no faceTarget and whose motions have faceTarget="0" casts facing its target every time, turning right round between two casts 1.4 s apart, while one whose nodes write faceTarget="0" keeps its facing. Before, such bosses (most boss AI files) never turned at a cast. Standby nodes without the attribute (648) are unchanged: not known.
- **Guide objects:** the skill guide has no int before the entry count; a ball is created on field entry through the home action packet, not the guide object create, which refuses a ball.
- **Guild:**
  - Server-to-client sub-types 52 to 89 are shifted up by 2 to the client's numbers.
  - Experience, funds and member contribution use the client's sub-types (53 and 97).
- **Server list:** the error writes the string the client reads first.
- **Reconnect (0x05):** after a lost link the client can connect to the game server again and, in place of ResponseKey, send a reconnect request with its account id, character id, attempt, packet sequence, machine id and the context the server gave it (`CNetworkService::OnRequestKey`, `CReconnectionManagerCli::SendReconnectRequest`, reconnectionmanagercli.cpp:245). It does so only after the server turned reconnection on (Gvg group 43, the first option 1) and sent a context of mode 1 (Reconnect command 0); this server sends neither, so the client does not reconnect and shows the usual disconnect box. The request had no handler; it is now read and refused with Reconnect command 3, on which the client drops its reconnect state and, in the game scene, quits to the login scene with its own message (`OnReconnectPacket`). Nothing in the request proves the account, and a real resume would need the lost session kept with the packets the client has not had acknowledged (the client drops its copies on Pong), which the server does not keep.
- **Heartbeat and lost sessions:** the World server asks every session for a heartbeat each 30 s; the client only answers it (`CNetworkService::OnHeartbeatRequest`) and keeps no time limit of its own. A late answer no longer ends the session (the Release build cut any answer later than 2 s, so a client that stood still for 3 s with a heartbeat on the way was dropped): a held-up client is still there. A session ends when nothing at all has arrived from its client for three heartbeat rounds, 90 s (chosen here), checked when the next heartbeat is due, so the cut comes 90 to 120 s after the client's last packet and only after two unanswered heartbeats (a client that stands still for 100 s is cut only when a round falls into its last 10 s). That session's socket is reset, not closed in order: the client shows its lost-connection notice, or starts its reconnect, only when the socket closes with an error (`CGameSocket::OnClose`); after an orderly close it shows nothing and stays on the field with nothing reaching the server. `Session.Cut` resets; `Session.Disconnect` still closes in order, for a client that was sent its last packet and leaves by itself.
- **A World server that comes back:** a Game server now checks its World link every 5 s and registers again when the World no longer knows its channel: it asks for its own channel id (`AddChannelRequest.channel`, with an instance id so a second process cannot take a held id) and then reports each online session with the player update it sends at login, so the World's channel and player lists are whole again; a report that fails because the World is not there is tried again, and one the World refuses for a single session is logged and skipped. Before this a restarted World handed the next Game server the same id and knew none of the players until each changed map. Chosen here: parties are not kept across a World restart, so a member whose party the World no longer has gets the party-disbanded answer the client already handles; group chats, party search entries, dungeon matches and other state the World holds in memory are gone as before. No database change.
- **Which close a session gets:** every close that the client did not ask for and was not told of by a last packet is now a reset (`Session.Cut`): a socket error or a failed send, an exception on the session's threads, a wrong client version, the World server's disconnect request (the account logs in elsewhere, a GM ends the session), an equip or badge request that leaves the session in a bad state, a login that fails without an answer, and the older login session of an account that logs in again. The orderly close stays for a session that was sent a last packet that makes the client leave (the move to another server, channel or map instance, a disconnect notice) and for what the client asks for itself (quit, back to the server list). The first close decides: a reset that comes while an orderly close drains (the session's own threads end with one) no longer turns it into a reset. Login sessions get the same 90 s silence check as channel sessions, in the login server's heartbeat round. The silence check also needs two heartbeats that went unanswered since the client's last packet (chosen here), so that a session is not cut for the time the rounds themselves stood still (a World server restart, a round that was held up); rounds closer together than half an interval count as one. An error packet that the client shows without leaving (a failed channel change, move to the home or another map, return to the character list, login key or game entry) and the logout command are followed by a reset that waits 0.5 s after the send queue is drained (`Session.CutAfterSend`, the wait chosen here), so the client reads the error and then learns that the connection is gone; they were closed in order, which left the client on a dead socket.
  - Chosen here: command 3 rather than a failure code of command 2, so no code is made up; the request is handled on the game server only, where the client sends it; the server does not close the link itself (the client does on command 3) and does not act on the ids it read; reconnection stays off (no Gvg group 43, no context).
- **Field portal:** sub-type 3 writes the portal id before the two vectors.
- **Cube:**
  - The architect score, build budget and build permission packets (sub-types 28 and 56 to 59) write the client's fields.
  - The profile update writes four ints before the name and a trailing byte; plot extension writes only the error byte.
  - The vote confirm (sub-type 25) writes the error byte and, on success, the character id and value; sub-type 53 writes the four longs the client reads.
  - A refused edit is answered in the edit's own sub-type, as KMS2 sends it: place (10), remove (12) and replace (15) write the error byte and the object id twice (10 bytes), a refused lift (17) the error byte and the object id (6 bytes). The server answered every refusal through sub-type 2, which KMS2 does not use for these (186 refused edits and 20 refused lifts in KMS2 sessions, none in sub-type 2). A place on a taken cell is refused with error 2 (s_ugcmap_create_on_non_empty_area) instead of s_ugcmap_cant_create_on_place, and a replace by the item already on the cell with error 53 (s_ugcmap_cant_replace_same_cube), which the server carried out (68 such refusals, no such replace carried out). A cube that cannot be paid is refused the same way (error 96 or 97).
    - The 10-byte place answer with byte 1 is no refusal: it is the notice of a bought cube, sent between the storage packets and the place itself (KMS2 sends FurnishingStorage 3, 5, 2, this notice, FurnishingStorage 4, FurnishingInventory 1, then the place). The server sends the same packets in that order. Its FurnishingStorage 5 named the item id where the client reads the storage item's uid (`CSceneStateGame::OnFurnishingStorage`, scenestategame.cpp:2736), so the client found no item and skipped its gain notice; it now names the uid, as KMS2 does.
    - Not done: a refused rotate keeps sub-type 2: KMS2 sessions hold no refused rotate.
  - A build helper gets sub-type 55 after sub-type 53, as KMS2 sends it: the owner's interior level (a short) and the list of (trophy id, grade) pairs that cube items ask for and the owner has not cleared, in key order. In a home it may build in, the client locks a cube by these instead of the helper's own level and trophies (`MyPC::FUN_1415302e0`, mypc.cpp:4437; the tooltip, astooltip.cpp:2292); without the packet the owner's level is 0 there. The level is the home's decoration level, the value sub-type 39 gives the owner's own client.
  - A lifted weapon cube (a rock, a sign) is away until its respawn time: a second lift of it before then is refused (`s_ugcmap_no_cube_to_lift`), and a player who enters the field meanwhile gets sub-type 27 with each such cube's cell and the tick it comes back at, which hides it on that client as the lift itself (sub-type 17) does for the players present (scenestategame.cpp:10195). Before, the server kept no such state: a late player saw the cube and could lift it again at once.
    - Chosen here: sub-type 27 is sent only when a cube is away. KMS2 also sends it with a count of 0 (9 of 10), which changes nothing in the client.
  - The forfeit confirm (sub-type 7) carries a reason: 0x72 to an owner who gives the plot up, as KMS2 sends it, and 0x75 to an online owner whose plot ran out (the World's expiry job tells every channel; the owner is answered on whichever channel they are on, whether or not it has the plot's map loaded). The client shows its "already expired" notice only for 0x75 and the forfeit notice for anything else (scenestategame.cpp:9959); the server sent 0 on both paths, so an expired plot was announced as given up.
  - Not changed: the remove-helper answer (sub-type 58) ends in KMS2 with the helper's name after the account id; the client reads the error byte and the id only.
  - Not sent, the played client has no reader: FunctionCube sub-type 8 and HomeBlock sub-type 0 (its reader takes 2 to 6; KMS2 sessions with sub-type 0 as their second or third packet never hold a sub-type 4 list, so they are of another build). EnterUgcMap 0, 2, 5 and 7 to 11 are message boxes for a refused entry and stay unsent (the server sends 3, 4 and 6): KMS2 sends each once or twice, and the packets do not name their cause.
- **Home maids:** a furnishing with a maid id (`<maid maidID>` in its item row) places the maid npc of that row of `table/maidproperty.xml` at the cube when the home loads and when the cube is placed, and removes it with the cube and when the plot is forfeited or expires. Only a friendly npc is placed, without its AI (it stands on the cube); the npc is spawned and removed on the field thread. The field sends the maid (FieldMaid, CMaid::ReadFromPacket) before the npc, and the npc's FieldAddNpc carries the maid uid (the cube uid), by which the client finds its maid. A new maid starts at affinity level 1 and mood 0 and is hired for `PeriodOfMaidEmployment` days (30). The client keys a maid by the uid it knows the maid furnishing by: CMaid's id (FieldMaid and UserMaid, by which CFieldMaidManager and CUserMaidManager hold it), the npc's maid uid of FieldAddNpc, and the uid the maid dialog's requests send (Maid 0x62, the npc's maid uid) are all that one uid, and the maid item tooltip looks the maid up by the item's uid. A placed furnishing is known by its entry of the placed list (FurnishingInventory, the cube uid), a stored one by its furnishing storage item uid. So the maid row (`maid` table, no longer tied to the cube table: migration `MaidKeyedByFurnishing`) is keyed by the cube uid while the furnishing is placed and moves to the storage item when the cube is picked up (UserMaid remove of the cube uid, add under the item uid), and back to the new cube uid when that item is placed again: the maid keeps its mood, affinity, contract and profile, and the stored item's tooltip shows it. Each maid item keeps a storage entry of its own (maid items do not stack; one may take an emptied entry of the same item). Deleting a stored maid item from the storage deletes its maid; so do forfeiting or buying a plot and dropping a cube from a saved plot, which delete cubes without returning them to storage. Maid rows written before this migration are all keyed by placed cubes and stay as they are. The npc keeps the cube's position and rotation when it was placed; turning or moving the cube does not move it. The owner gets the list of their maids at login (UserMaid; the maid status window, the tooltip and the craft windows read it), a new maid when it is hired and its removal with the cube, and each change of a maid (mood, affinity, contract, profile) both as UserMaid update and again as FieldMaid to the field. The maid dialog saves the profile (Maid mode 4) for the owner only and only while the maid has no name, as the client's dialog allows; a banned word in any of its 13 texts drops the save. Its debug buttons (modes 5-10: affinity level, affinity exp, reset, mood up and down, contract end earlier or later) are taken from a player with the Debug admin permission only; the mood step of 10 is the server's (the client sends none). The maid's talk script works through the script functions: `maidMoodUp` adds mood (kept in 0..100), `maidAffinityUp` adds affinity exp (each level needs the exp of its `table/maidexp.xml` row; the top level is the lower of `MaidAffinityMax` and that table's row count, where the exp stays 0), and `maidPay` takes the salary of `table/maidsalary.xml` (mesos for salary type 0, merets otherwise; nothing happens when the player cannot pay) and extends the contract by `PeriodOfMaidEmployment` days from its end, or from now when it has ended. These run for the maid's owner only, and the script condition `maid_auth` passes only for the owner. Crafting is on the next line. Not done yet: gifts to a maid (the client has no request for them); the conditions `maid_expired`, `maid_ready_to_pay`, `maid_affinity_grade` and the mood, affinity and days-before-expiry times (the ingest reads `maid_expired` and `maid_ready_to_pay` as true when the attribute is absent, and the two times a CMaid keeps at +0x38 and +0x40 are not known, so the server sends the hiring time for both); the counted id list and the byte at +0x4c of CMaid (sent as empty and 1). Needs a re-ingest (the maid table with salaries and affinity exp, and the items' maid ids).
- **Maid crafting (MaidCraftItem 0x68/0xA9):** a maid of the owner's home crafts one recipe at a time. The ingest reads `table/maidrecipe.xml` and `table/maidrecipegroup.xml` straight from the XML. A maid's recipes are the group whose id is its maid id (the craft dialog looks the group up by the maid id), each with the affinity level it needs. A recipe names its ingredients (an item id, or an item tag), its WorkbenchType, a lead time per mood grade, the maid exp and mood it gives and its product (item, count, rarity). Requests: 4 craft (recipe id, maid uid), 6 receive (maid uid), 10 cancel (maid uid). They run on the field thread of the maid's field, only after the maid is found still placed there and owned by the requester. A craft needs a maid that crafts nothing, a level the recipe group asks for and a running contract (else result 0x12). With WorkbenchType > 0 (28-30) the owner must have a cube with that icon code on one of their plots of the field (CubeMapInformationService); no recipe costs mesos. The ingredients are counted as the client counts them: by id, at the item's rank as rarity unless it is equipment, or by tag at any rarity. Each count is cut by the ReduceMaidRecipe rate, to at least 1. The ingredients are taken and the lead time of the maid's mood grade is set (result 1, record add); short items or no workbench answer 8. A receive after the ready time (start + lead time; 7 without a craft, 0xd before it) gives the product, raises the maid's affinity exp by the recipe's exp at the MaidExp rate and its mood by the recipe's mood. The answers are record remove (3), result 2 and the reward page (mode 11: item, count, rarity, exp, then bonus, jackpot and merat, sent as 0). A cancel answers 4 and gives nothing back. The owner gets the records of all their maids at login (mode 0). A maid picked up or placed again keeps its craft under its new uid; deleting the stored maid item deletes it. The craft is kept in the maid row (migration `MaidCraft`). Chosen here: the record's first long (read nowhere by the client) and its last int (the gauge's total) are the maid uid and the lead time. A recipe with an ingredient that is no known item tag is dropped at ingest, and a group table whose two lists differ in length on any row is dropped whole (the client fails the whole table then). Locked and expired items do not count as ingredients. The mood is added on receive, not at start. The product's socket data is not used. A full bag answers nothing; the craft waits until there is room (the client has no message for it). A maid's home can be open in several channels, each with its own copy of the maid. So craft, receive and cancel write the craft as one update that tests the stored craft (recipe and start), and only act when that update changed the row. Otherwise the request gets the stored craft back (its record, or its removal; a receive also gets its result from it, never result 2 since it gave no product), so a second receive of one craft gives no product and a second craft takes no ingredients. A receive or cancel on a copy without a craft reads the stored craft first, as another channel may have started one since the home was loaded. The general maid save (mood, affinity, contract, profile) no longer writes the craft. A craft is stored before its ingredients are taken; a receive checks room for the whole product (what the stacks do not take, at the slot maximum), clears the craft before it adds the product and stores it again only if the bag takes none of it. A recipe whose product item the server does not have is never started. An ingredient with a count below 1 is left out at ingest (it asks for nothing), and the product count is at least 1. Not done: request 5 (the dialog's debug craft), 7 (receive at once for merets; the cost is computed by client Lua), 8, and 9 with the jackpot (result 3, fireworks), whose rates come from `Table/Server/MaidGradeInfo.xml`, a server table not shipped with the client. Needs a re-ingest (the maid tables and the items' iconCode and rank) and `dotnet ef database update`.
- **Fishing:** the simulation result writes four fixed ints per fish.
- **Item socket:** unequipping a gem writes the gem's uid at the end.
- **Right-click equip of an outfit:** with the second outfit tab feature on, a right click on an outfit item asks for the second outfit tab (kind 2), and for the first only while the skin-tab key is held (`FUN_1414056b0`, client/ms2/invenslot.cpp:602); the client does not check the tab's period. The server refused kind 2 when the tab had no running period (or for hair and face), with no answer, so a right click did nothing unless the character window's outfit tab took a drag. The item's own type now picks gear or outfit for every kind (the server's own equip request and auto-equip send 0), and kind 2 goes to the second tab only when it takes the slot, else to the first (the client itself sends 1 for 2 when the feature is off). A refused equip still sends nothing: the client holds no pending state for it (`Unk_Inventory::RequestEquipItem` sends and returns), and it has already shown its own messages for what it checks.
- **Region spawn positions:** a region spawn's mobs took the ground-cube spawn tiles within 250 of its point, and once those ran out (a field floored by a mesh has one or two) the rest were put on a tile already taken, so 4 to 7 mobs stood on one spot; the random pick also swapped with the wrong slot and could pick a tile twice while others were free. Each mob now takes a tile of its own while there are tiles, and a mob past them goes to a random navmesh point within 250 of a tile (the tile itself when none is found).
- **Region spawns on a mesh floor:** a region spawn whose point stands on a mesh's floor (a road, a bridge, a roof) or a moving platform has no spawn tile in reach, since tiles are made only over ground cubes: 162 spawns in 63 fields (Kerning Interchange alone 8). The field logged "Failed to find spawns" as an error on every entry and used the bare point, in the air above the floor. Such a spawn now takes the walkable navmesh spots under its point and the 8 block neighbours (at most half a block aside and one block up or down, so none past a ledge), which gives 147 of the 162 one to nine spots; the 15 left (points with nothing under them) keep the bare point and log a warning. Not changed: 1,058 rows of the map spawn table name a spawn point the field's own data does not have (707 with a population; the played client's field file is the same), and those are still skipped.
- **Region spawn respawns, one mob per death:** a dead mob of a region spawn now comes back alone, at its death + the NPC's `Dead.Time` + the spawn's `Cooldown`, whatever the rest of its group does; the group is full on field entry and never goes over its `Population`. KMS2 field play shows this: cooldown 13 brought a mob back 18.0 s after its death (58 respawns, most 17.9-18.7 s, with 0 to 3 of a group of 4 still alive), cooldown 20 after 25.0 s (14) and cooldown 30 after 35.1 s (2), with `Dead.Time` 5. Before, the whole group came back at once: `Cooldown` after the death that emptied it (13 s, 5 s early) or twice `Cooldown` after the first death while some were alive (26 s, 8 s late). Chosen here: the pet roll is made each time the group is full again, about once per refill as before; a spawn that fails (an NPC the played client lacks) is tried again one `Cooldown` later. Only region spawns (the map's spawn table) change; spawn points, trigger and AI-summoned NPCs, which have no region spawn, keep their own rules. A debug build brought mobs back in half these times; it now uses the same times as a release build, so a live check on a debug server shows the real timing.
- **Animation sequence names ignore case:** trigger scripts name a sequence in another case than the model at times (`set_npc_emotion_sequence` "ChatUP_A" for a model whose sequence is "ChatUp_A"), and the exact-case lookup dropped the emotion with an "Invalid sequence" error. A model's sequences are now keyed without regard to case when loaded, and the npc plays the sequence under the model's own name.
- **Club:** a member rename writes the club id before the names; a member update writes only the member profile; leaving writes only the club id.
- **Club leader leave is stored:** when the leader of a club of three or more leaves, the next member leads (KMS2 sends UpdateLeader, then the leave notice, and no disband), and the club row now stores that member. The database is written first: if the write fails, the member, the leader and the other channels stay as they were and the leaver gets s_club_err_unknown. Before, only the member row went, so the stored leader was no member and the club failed to load after a restart (GetClub picks the leader among the members). Checked against the client (`CClubClientManager` @0x140b16a70 onward, client/ms2/clubcli.cpp) and KMS2: the create, staged reply, decline (Reply 76 then Fail 77), disband (207, from two members), leave, join, rename and buff layouts match. Not sent and not needed: modes 12 (id and name, the client reads nothing more) and 25 (a member removed by name with notice 0x351, see below); modes 4 and 28 are sent now (next bullet); the staged reply code 75 that KMS2 sent twice has no meaning in the client's table and is not used.
- **Club staged reply result and deleted members:** Club 4 (`FUN_140b16a70`: club id, result byte; 0 drops the club from the client, any other result prints that club error) now answers a member's own staged invite reply: a decline gets result 0, so the decliner's window of the staged club goes at once (before, nothing came until the World's disband); an accept gets nothing (Club 1 follows when all have accepted); a reply the World refuses gets its error code in mode 4 instead of the error notice 0x1d (the client has mode 4 for this reply; the choice of mode 4 over 0x1d is made here, KMS2 was not seen refusing a reply). The replier no longer gets the staged reply notice that names itself; the other members still get it. Club 28 (`FUN_140b14110`: club id, name; the member goes from the list with no notice) is sent when a member's character is deleted: the Login server asks the World first (`ClubRequest.delete_character`), which takes the character out of each established club, hands a club of three or more on to the next member when the character led it, and disbands a club of two and every staged club the character is in (a staged club could never reach all its members' accepts, as with a declined invite); the other members get 28 and not the leave notice 18. No club state stops the deletion: a club that does not load, fails to change or fails its database delete is logged and dropped from the World's memory, so it loads again from the database as the deletion leaves it. That partial result is safe: the character's member rows go with the character row and a club it still leads goes with it, so the worst case is a club handed on or left before a later failure, a lost membership with no rows out of step. A disband now deletes the club rows before it tells the channels, so a failed delete leaves the club whole everywhere. A club stored with a leader who is no member (saved before the leader's leave fix) loads with its longest member as the leader instead of failing, so its members can log in and delete characters again. Before, the World was never told: the member rows went with the character row and a deleted leader's club rows went with it, while the World and the channels still listed the member. When the World cannot be reached or answers an error (club state no longer gives one), the character is not deleted (CharacterList delete answer s_char_err_destroy). Club 25 (`FUN_140b13dc0`: name only, notice 0x351) stays unsent: it would be a member removed by someone else, and the client has no kick command, while a member who leaves gets 18. No database change.
- **Character info (another player's info window):** the guild line writes the player's guild rank name after the guild name, which the client shows together (`UIPCInfoDialog::vfunc_103`, uipcinfodialog.cpp:1341, string `s_char_info_guild_name`); it was empty. The home line's two ints (`CPCData` m_n408, m_n40c, commonfiles/pcdata.cpp:138) are the home's architect scores, all time then this month: the window shows them as "{m_n40c} / {m_n408}" (`vfunc_102`, :1328, `s_char_info_home_commend`), in the "this month / all time" order of the home info window (`UIHomeInfoDialog`, uihomeinfodialog.cpp:410); both were 0. A player online on another channel now gets the window too: the World server passes the request to that channel, which writes the packet as for its own players. A player who is offline, still entering the game, or on a channel that does not answer gets the "not found" answer (`s_char_info_err_not_found`).
  - Chosen here (the client does not settle it): no info for an offline player. The client asks only by character id and reads the same packet either way, so an offline answer would need stats and equipment read from the database without the player's buffs; the server keeps the "not found" answer instead.
- **Shop:** a shop item writes the extra flag the client reads.
- **Shop sell price by the client's rule:** the price a shop pays (and the black market preview shows) is the client's `CItemData::GetSellPrice` (commonfiles/itemdata.cpp:1508): the item's price of the rarity; for equipment (property type 1) it is multiplied in float by the `multicontrol.xml` `equipmentSellPrice` factor of the rarity and cut to a whole number, and equipment without a skin type, with a price of 0 and a level limit of 57 or more takes the constant `SellPrice<Rarity>Max` first. The factors are those of LOCALE's own table (`table/<locale>/multicontrol.xml` of the played client's pack, else the data folder's client pack, else the server pack): the NA table has 0.333 for rarity 1 to 3, the KR table has no row, so under KR equipment sells at the full price, as in KMS2. Before, every item with a level limit of 57 or more sold at a fixed NA price and every other weapon or armor of rarity 1 to 3 at a third, under any locale. The factors are stored with the constants table; needs a re-ingest, until then no factor applies. Chosen here: the server data has two price lists and the price is `priceCustom` where it is above 0, else `price`, which gives the prices the client's own item data lists.
- **Shop open flag:** the shop info writes the shop's `DisplayProbability` as the flag the client reads into its probability-info field (`UIShopDialog::ReadShopInfo`, client/ms2/uishopdialog.cpp:1579); it was always 0. KMS2 sends 1 for such a shop.
- **Refused sales are answered:** a sale the client's shop dialog blocks (`UIShopDialog::CanSellItem`, uishopdialog.cpp:2076: the item data forbids the shop sale, the item is locked or still unlocking, or it has a gemstone in a socket) is refused by the server too and answered with shop error 5 and the sell command's number, as KMS2 sends; the client shows its cannot-sell notice (codes 4, 5 and 6 share it). Before, only the item data's flag was checked and the refusal sent nothing.
- **Shop restock while the shop is cached:** a shop on a restock schedule whose time passed while the player stayed online is made anew at the next opening, as it already was after a login; it kept the old list and time. Chosen here: a shop with a fixed restock time in its data keeps its list.
- **Shop payment in other currencies:** a purchase paid in a token currency takes the price of the bought quantity (it took the unit amount whatever the quantity), a refusal for lack of it sends error 11 with the currency kind (the client's lack notice of that kind; chosen here: the payment type's number), and a purchase in game merets takes game merets (it took merets).
- **Guild shop checks:** the guild products are checked as the client's shop dialog checks them (uishopdialog.cpp:3562-3711). A guild trophy product needs 7 days in the guild (code 20) and then enough trophies (16). A guild npc product needs the guild's npc of that type at the product's level; the client shows `s_err_lack_guild_npc_level` itself, so no shop code is sent. A product of currency 7 is paid from the guild fund (the enum value was named GuildCoin) by the guild master only (26). The World server takes the fund under the guild's lock and saves it before it answers (27 when the fund is short); every member then gets the new fund. Before this, currency 7 always failed and the npc and 7-day gates were a TODO.
- **Shop answer order:** the shop's answers go out in the order KMS2 sends them. A buy (14 KMS2 buys): the payment, the bag add, Shop 2 with the bought count for a product with a stock (it went before the bag add), then Shop 4. A sale (1): the bag removal, the buy-back entry (Shop 7), then the mesos (the mesos went first). A buy back (1): Shop 8, the bag add, then the mesos (the mesos went first); a buy back the wallet cannot pay is refused before the entry is taken. The packets themselves were equal, and so are the instant restock and the system shop open and close (9 KMS2 requests).
- **Shop id of a buy:** the client writes its window's shop id after the quantity (`UIShopDialog::SendPurchase`, uishopdialog.cpp:2846-2851) and the server did not read it. It is read now. Chosen here (KMS2 shows no such case): a buy naming another shop than the open one is dropped.
- **Shop products sold in packs:** a product with a sell unit over 1 gave `quantity` items while Shop 4 said unit x quantity. The client buys packs: its product item holds the pack size (`CNpcShopProductCli::Read`, npcshopproductbase.cpp:163 and :188), the quantity it sends is a number of packs (the most it offers is the item count divided by the pack size, :453), the price it shows is per pack (uishopdialog.cpp:3151) and it divides the bought count the server sends by the pack size (`SetBuyItemCount`, :222). So a buy of `quantity` now costs `quantity` prices, uses `quantity` of the stock and puts quantity x unit items in the bag, as Shop 2 and Shop 4 already said. A count over the int range is refused.
- **Bag sort sends the merged stacks:** where a sort merges stacks, KMS2 removes the emptied stack (ItemInventory 1) and updates the grown one (ItemInventory 2) before it resets and resends the tab (1 KMS2 sort); the server sent only the reset and the tab. Chosen here: with several merges, every removal goes before the updates.
- **Character list:** the name change result (CharacterList 7) is sent as KMS2 sends it: the result, the character and its new name; the client reads only the result.
- **Character rename:** two ways, as the client has them.
  - The name change item in game: KMS2 takes the item out of the bag, answers CharacterList 7, then sends the member's old and new name to the guild (Guild 12) and to each club (Club 27); it sends nothing for the friend list, the party, or the names kept in mails and market listings, and those are left as they are. The server sends the same in that order. The name is now written to the database with the rename, before the item is used up: it was held in memory until the logout save, so a create could take the same name meanwhile. A name that another character holds by the time of the write is refused with the client's taken text and the item stays.
  - The forced rename on the character list (CharacterManagement 5: the character and the name, no item, so it costs nothing). The client opens its name input for a character whose name is too long for the region (more than 12, Hangul counting twice): by itself when the list ended with the flag (CharacterList 4) and it starts with that character, or when the server answers the select with LoginToGame 49 (`CNetworkService::OnLoginToGame`). The server sets the flag only when a character of the account has such a name, answers the select of such a character with LoginToGame 49 and does not let it enter, and accepts the rename only for such a character of the account. The name passes the same checks as a create (blank, characters, length, banned word, taken; the write refuses a name taken since the check). The list takes no input while the name input is open, and after the request it stays so until the list is sent again or CharacterList 7 arrives (`UICharacterListDialog::vfunc_79`: it gives the input back, then code 1 puts the new name on the row, 2 shows the taken text, 6 the bad word text, any other the system text with the code). An accepted name is shown by sending the character list again, and the world tells the guild and club members online; a refused name is answered with CharacterList 7 and its code (2, 6, or 0 for a name that is not valid or a character that is not due), and the character stays due. The server answered a refusal with a message box only, so after its OK no row could be selected and Start and Escape did nothing until the client was started again. No server path stores a name that long (create and both renames refuse it), so the flag is 0 unless a row was written another way; KMS2 sends 1 in 7 of 8 lists, where the client's own test then decides.
  - A name that is too long and also has a character the region does not allow is due for the forced rename, as in the client (the server counted it as not due, so the client asked for a name and the server refused the rename). The rename by the name change item refreshes the character's modified time (the logout save logged a concurrency conflict after it) and stores the used item right after the name, not at the logout save: a crash before the logout save left the new name and gave the item back. A database error during a rename is answered as an error, not as a taken name.
- **Return to the character list:** the client answers the login server's MoveResult with ServerEnter (`CNetworkService::OnMoveResult`), and KMS2 lists the banners, servers and characters once, after it. Upstream listed them with the MoveResult and again on ServerEnter, so the character list was built twice; the list now goes out on ServerEnter only. The game entry sent the survival stats (Survival 23) twice; it is sent once.
  - Not done: KMS2 packets of the login and the game entry that are not sent, none with an effect the client shows without its feature: NpsInfo, BypassKey, SyncWorld, PremiumClub 0 on the login server, Gvg (the server's option groups: content shutdown, rates of 1.0 the client already defaults to, reconnect options), Pvp 12/22/23, MeretMarket 11/12/201, PcBangBonus 3, WorldTimeSync 0, Ugc 22.
- **Gender voucher (ItemUse ChangeGender):** upstream has no case for it, so the voucher did nothing. The client asks with a confirm box and sends RequestItemUse with an empty string; it refuses by itself while a worn item outside hair, face, face decoration and ear is limited to one gender (common string 755, `s_change_gender_err_equip_items`), and while the wedding state is not single or divorced (`ChangeGender` @0x140d22dd0). KMS2 answers with the voucher leaving the bag, ItemPutOff for the worn look items and a Notice 5 message box, after which the client leaves the game scene; the character list then shows the other gender with new items in those slots. The client shows the notice's text as it is after it leaves the scene (`CSceneStateGame::OnNotice` kind 5), so the fork sends `s_change_gender_result_success` (756 in this client's table). The KMS2 notice carried id 755 on build 2521; in this client 755 is the worn-items refusal, and sending it showed the refusal box after a change that went through. The fork repeats both client refusals with a message box.
  - Chosen here: each new item is the first default item (defaultitems, common list) limited to the new gender, else the first for both genders, and it keeps the colour of the item it replaces. Hair and face are always replaced; a face decoration or an ear only when one is worn. The skin colour stays. KMS2 gives other default items and another skin colour; its rule is not known. The change is one step under the item lock: the new items are stored and the voucher is consumed first, and if either fails the stored rows are removed and the voucher, the gender and the worn items stay (a message box with `s_change_gender_result_failed`); then the old look items are discarded, the gender flips, and the character, the equips and the bag are saved at once. The new items are not broadcast (KMS2 sends no put-on; the player leaves the scene). The server does not close the session itself after the notice, and equipped badges are not checked (the client's check walks the shown equipment only).
  - Chosen here: a message box with s_change_gender_result_failed, with the voucher kept, when no default hair or face exists.
- **User environment:** the gathering counts have no trailing int.
- **Mail expiry:** the client lists a mail only before its expiry time (CMailInfo::IsExpired), so a mail stored without one (system, wedding, event, market mails) gets the `MailExpiryDays` constant (30 days in the server and client tables) when it is created. The migration MailExpiryBackfill gives every mail stored with expiry 0 the migration time plus 30 days (not the send time plus 30 days, so an old mail with items does not run out at once).
- **Mail collect:** the client's collect answer (OnMail mode 10) names what was given: byte 1 and a slot (0-4) for an attachment, byte 0 for the meso. Each given attachment is sent with its own slot and the meso with the byte 0 form; the closing packet (mode 11) carries the mail's read time, and the client takes a time of 0 as not collected. Chosen here: collecting an unread mail marks it read first, so the closing packet always has a time. A collect is refused with code 21 once the mail is expired, and nothing is given unless every attachment fits the bag (existing stacks count).
- **Mail unread count and bulk requests:** the unread notice counts the mails without a read time that are not expired, so it goes down as mails are read. The notice's last int is the client's unread alarm, which only highlights the mail menu while it is above 0. KMS2 sends it lower than the unread count: with one unread system mail (kind 106) the notice is 1 and 1, and when a guild mail (kind 2) arrives it is 2 and 1. Chosen here (that one step is the only sample): the alarm counts the unread mails of kinds 100 to 112, the kinds the client highlights by itself once it has the list, so a player's or guild mail raises the count and not the highlight; it was the unread count. Chosen here: read-all and collect-all pass over a mail that is already read or already collected and go on with the rest; any other error ends the request and is shown.
- **Temporary item mail notice (Mail 15):** KMS2 sends Mail 15 right before the unread count (Mail 14) when a mail with a temporary item comes in during play; the client (OnMail mode 0xf) shows a notice and a chat line telling the player to take the item before it runs out. The server had the writer and never sent it. It is now sent once, before the count, for a mail that arrives after login with an attached item whose end time is still ahead.
  - Chosen here: not sent for the mails already in the box at login (KMS2 showed it only during play), and one notice for several such mails found in one fetch.
- **Mail errors name the request:** the client's error answer (OnMail mode 0x14, scenestategame.cpp:11662) reads the request's command and then the error. KMS2 answers a refused collect with `14 0b 14`; the server wrote 0 for the command in every mail error. Send, read, collect, read-all and collect-all errors now carry their command. Collecting an unread mail that holds mesos or merets (collect-all does it for every unread mail) threw in storage, because the read and the collect times were stored through two instances of the row in one request; the row is stored now. KMS2 mail requests seen: load 11, read 13, collect 3, delete 1 (28 in 11 sessions); the load, read, collect and delete answers are equal.
- **Mail between blocked players, mail delete:** the client has a text for a mail to a player the sender blocked (OnMail error 27) and for one to a player who blocked the sender (28); the server sent such mails. Both are refused now with their code. Deleting a mail removed only the mail's row: the items not yet taken stayed in storage, owned by the mail id. They now go with the mail (their rows lose the owner, and the World server deletes unowned items when it starts). A mail with a locked item not yet taken is not deleted, as the client refuses it too (`UIMailListDialog::HasLockedAttachment`). Not changed: Mail 12 (buy an ad mail, sent only for ad mails of kind 200, which the server never makes) is still only read. A collect is serialized by the item lock and reads the mail again from storage, so a second click gives nothing; a character is in one channel at a time.
- **Mail, global portal, premium club:** results the client reads no fields of (mail sent, portal closed, premium club sub-types 3 and 4) write only the sub-type.
- **Global portal announce and enter:** the client's dialog reads exactly three captions after the popup text and the sound (`UIGlobalPortalDialog`, uiglobalportaldialog.cpp); the server wrote one per field of the time event row, so it now writes three (empty where the row has fewer; the six rows in use have three). The enter request's button index was used unchecked against the row's fields on the World server; an index outside them is now ignored. A dead player's enter request is answered with the dialog's mode 3 (s_massive_event_error_cant_enter_dead). Mode 4 (level limit) is not sent: which level it means is not known.
- **Global portal at field enter and its schedule:** KMS2 sends the announce again to a player who logs in or enters a field while the portal is open (it stays open for a minute). The server's send at field enter tested the World answer for an error instead of for none, so it never went out; it now does, in the same field types as the announce at the opening. A portal row ran for a roll of 0..99 up to and including its probability (one in 100 too many, and a row of 0 ran), a row whose StartTime was still ahead was never scheduled, and the end time kept for the opening added milliseconds to seconds; rows and world bosses now share one rule for the first run and the roll. The six rows in use have probability 100 and started in 2015, so nothing changes for them. The send at field enter is tested on the session (open portal: sent in the four field types; none open, another field type, a World error or an unknown row: not sent). Home is one of the four: KMS2 sends the announce to players in their home (25 times in the sessions). When the portal closes, the World server now forgets it before it tells the channels, so a field enter during the close no longer gets the announce after the close (tested with two channels: each hears the close with the portal already gone from the World lookup).
- **Chat stickers:** adding a sticker group writes a group count of 1, not the item stack size.
- **Chat sticker refusals:** using a sticker (in the messenger or a group chat) that is unknown, whose group is not held or whose time has run out is answered with result 0, the client's print failure notice; before it got no answer. Marking a favourite needs the same test the client makes (a sticker it may use); a request for another sticker gets no answer, as the result codes have no notice for it. A sticker item used on a held group whose time has already run out now counts from now; before it counted from the old expiry, so the new time could already be over. Chosen here: a timed item adds its time to what is left of a timed group, an item without time makes the group permanent, and any item on a permanent group is refused with result 2; KMS2 sessions hold only permanent groups, so the client's notice texts are the only source.
- **Chat line channel and whisper portrait:** the int after the GM flag of a chat line (UserChat) is the sender's channel; the client prints it in front of world and super chat lines (`FUN_14183f310`, texttohtml.cpp). KMS2 always sends it non-zero in world, channel, super and system notice lines. World, channel and super lines carry the sender's channel, and so does the system notice of an item announcement (KMS2: always non-zero there). In a normal line KMS2 sends the channel when the sender's field is no instance and 0 in every instanced field (home, dungeon, instanced map); its guild and received whisper lines fit the same rule, so normal, party, guild and received whisper lines now carry the sender's channel outside an instance and 0 inside one. Chosen here: the party line follows that rule too (every KMS2 party line seen was sent in an instance and has 0), the sender's own copy of a whisper keeps 0 (KMS2 sends 0 in nearly all), and club lines keep 0 (not compared). The string of a received whisper is the sender's profile picture, which the whisper notice draws as the portrait (`UIWhisperNotifyDialog::AddWhisper`, uiwhispernotifydialog.cpp:245); it was the sender's name. The sender's own copy of a whisper has account id 0, as KMS2 sends it.
- **Micro game chat line (type 23):** the client's rock-paper-scissors game (statemicrogame.cpp) says its lines as chat type 23, and KMS2 gives each back to the field with type 23 and channel 0. The server had no case for the type and dropped the line; it now goes to the sender's field.
- **Whisper across channels, the whisper block and the guild megaphone:** the World server handed a whisper to the channel of the sender, so a whisper to a player on another channel was lost (or reached nobody); it now looks the recipient up and goes to the recipient's channel, and a recipient who is offline or no longer on that channel answers the sender with s_err_cannot_find_user. The sender's own copy also has character id 0 (KMS2: account and character 0 in all 8 own copies seen). A client whose whisper block option is on answers a received whisper with chat type 6 naming the whisperer and no text (`UIMessengerBrowser`, uimessenger.cpp:2829); the server ignored it. It now goes back to the whisperer as the KMS2 line: the refuser's ids and name, common string 0 (the client drops a line with empty text before it looks at the type and prints string 0x8cd with the name), channel of the refuser's line. Only a whisper the refuser received is answered, once. The guild megaphone (type 18) was dropped; the client sends it only with the rank right 0x800 (SendAlert), the server checks the same right and gives the line to the guild as type 18 with channel 0 (KMS2). Channel chat now sends its item links before the line, as the other chat types do. The int of a super chat line is the selected item's id (KMS2: 20801012, 20801001, 20800003, 20800033), which the client looks up as item data for the icon, size and colour (`FUN_14183eba0`); it was the item's first function parameter (the icon name). Wedding chat, the OX quiz line, chat penalties and the chat limits: see the next bullet.
- **Whisper to an unknown name:** a whisper to a name no character has asked the World for character id 0, which the World refuses (InvalidArgument), and the Game server logged an error line for each such whisper. The player info lookup now answers id 0 as not found without asking the World (a Debug line at most), and the sender gets s_whisper_err_target as before.
- **Group chat item links:** the group chat window keeps its own linked items (`UIGroupMessengerBrowser::ReadItemLinks`, uigroupmessenger.cpp:1764), which give a link in a line its rarity colour, its tooltip (`getTooltip`) and the item preview, and fills them only from GroupChat 0xb (`CGroupChatManager` 0xb: chat id, sender name, which must be a member of that chat, then the count and per item the uid, id, rarity and the item block, as in MessengerBrowser). The server never sent 0xb, so every item link in a group chat line was white and showed nothing. A line now carries the uids of its linked items that the sender holds (as other chat lines do), and each member gets 0xb with those items just before the line (mode 10); a line without links gets no 0xb. No database change.
- **Group chat member picture:** GroupChat 0xc (`CGroupChatManager` 0xc, groupchatmanager.cpp:750: chat id, member name, picture path) sets a member's profile picture in the group chat window; the server never sent it, so a member's new picture showed only after the chat was opened again. A member's picture change now reaches every member of each group chat with that member, the member's own session included (its own window shows the new picture too); an update that keeps the picture sends nothing. No database change.
- **Wedding megaphone, OX quiz line, chat penalties and the length limit:** the wedding megaphone (chat type 25, a WeddingChat item selected in the super chat tab, which lists those items too: `GetWeddingChatItems`, uisidepopuptalk_2.cpp:211) was dropped. The client reads after the line's end flag a reservation block (`FUN_1407a5960`: marriage id, package id, hall id, ceremony time, groom, bride; the groom is the hall's reserver and the bride the partner, as in the invitation mail, whichever of the two sends) and turns it into a link that takes the reader to the hall (texttohtml.cpp:589); the line's int is the item id, as in super chat. The server now takes the item and sends the line with the sender's reservation to every channel. Chosen here: without a reserved hall the sender gets s_err_wedding_chat_cannot_use_no_reservation, once the hall is held or its entry window is past s_err_wedding_chat_cannot_use_complete, and the item stays; super chat with a wedding megaphone selected (or the other way round) is refused with s_err_invalid_item. Type 22 is the home OX quiz line, which only the server sends (texttohtml.cpp:575); a client line of type 22 goes nowhere, and the event itself is not done (see HomeAction 10). Chat penalties: the client has no check of its own; the constants table has the values (FastChat_CheckTime 2000 ms and CheckCount 5, SameChat_CheckTime 3000 and CheckCount 5, SameChat_RestrictTime 10000, FastChat_RestrictTime 30000, RestrictChat_AddRestrictTime 10000, AccumWarning_AddRestrictTime 60000, RestrictWarning_ReleaseTime 10000) and the texts s_chat_restrict_* name the restricted lines (normal, whisper, world and channel chat) and take the seconds to wait. Chosen here: the line that reaches a count is refused and starts the restriction, a line while restricted adds RestrictChat_AddRestrictTime, and a restriction within RestrictWarning_ReleaseTime after the last one ended is the AccumWarning one. The restriction is kept on the game session, so a channel change or a relog clears it. Length: the client's input box takes MaxChatLength characters (50, 100 under NA; `SetMaxChars`, uimessenger.cpp:775); a longer line, counted without the html of item links, is dropped. The lines the client builds itself are not held to that cap: the party and chaos DPS reports (party lines, `onNotice`, uipartydialog.cpp:1342), the mini game lines (type 23) and the whisper block answer. Chosen here: those kinds (and every party line, which the server cannot tell from a report) are dropped past 2,000 characters with their html; the longest the client builds, the DPS report of a full party, is about 650 with the English strings. Level: no chat level value is in the client or the tables, and the client's send has no level check, so there is no level limit.
- **Wedding proposal ring:** the server refused a proposal unless the target also held the proposal ring (11600482), and the accept took a ring from each (the one who accepted needed one too). In the client only the proposer needs the ring: `WeddingManager::CheckPropose` (weddingmanager.cpp:1035) asks for the proposer's own ring before it sends Wedding 3, and the answer box (`OnProposeAnswer`, Wedding 4 with the answer and the proposer's id) checks no item. The server now checks only the proposer's ring at Wedding 3 and, on the accept, uses up one ring of the proposer; a proposer whose ring is gone by then gets error 0x1f (the notice that names the ring), the one who accepts gets the same error, and no marriage is made. Not done: the client's gender rule (feature 0x105, which the server has no name for) and error 0x20 (too far apart), for which the client has no check.
- **Strings:** server message codes are mapped to the client's string ids by name.
- **Item tags:** the client's tag table (CItemDataTable::InitItemTags) has no DungeonRewardTypeA to D, so from 10 on its numbers are 4 lower than the server enum (Onix 97, ChaosOnix 98, CrystalPiece 96), and PetEXP is 216. The four names are in the 2019 NA client; this client is the same for both locales, so one map by name (ItemTagMap) is used under KR and NA. Every packet that sends a tag number goes through it: ItemEnchant 5 (stage item), LimitBreak stage item, ChangeAttributes 4, ItemMerge select and ItemExchange set target. The server reads tags from data by name, so nothing else changes. Tags without a client number are sent as 0. Those are only DungeonRewardTypeA to D (NA-only names): a data row that names one would show as an empty ingredient slot (tag 0 with item id 0); the KMS data names none of them.
- **Data:** 62 constants missing from the KMS constants file get the old built-in values as defaults; the file parser is updated for current XML.
- **Client constants of another client version:** a price the player confirms in a box is the client's own constants row, and the ingest takes those rows (prices, the two home call cooltimes, the box multi-open cap) from the `Xml.m2d` in `MS2_DATA_FOLDER`. When the client that is played is another version, set `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` to that client's `Xml.m2d` and run the ingest again: its row then wins where it has one, and a constant it has no row for stays as before. Not set, nothing changes. Between the v1.3.2 data pack this fork is ingested with and the older KR client it was tried with, these rows differ (data pack, then played client): KR locale `BeautyStyleExpandSlotPrice` 980 and 330, `BlackMarketSellMaxPrice` 500000000 and 10000000000; NA locale `BlackMarketSellMaxPrice` 500000000 and 10000000000, `LimitMeratRevival` 1 and 0, `TradeFeePercent` 20 and 10. Until the ingest is run with the option, the server charges the data pack's amount while the box shows the played client's.
- **Furnishing prices of another client version:** the price, currency and buy flag of a cube are the client's own rows (`shop_Maid.xml`, then `shop_UGCAll.xml`). With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` the ingest lays that pack's rows over the server pack's, so the owner's purchase and a build helper's charge on the home budget take what the client shows; needs a re-ingest. Without it the server pack's rows stand, and a row with price 0 there is still free.
- **Cube price sale (SaleHousing):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the housing filters, value2 the money types, value3 the rates out of 10000, comma lists of one length) and the item's `<housing filter>`, and the event list sends it: the event's id, a count, then per row the filter and money type as shorts and the rate. The build palette and the cube tooltip show the lowered price (`gamerule.cpp:1985`: the first row with the item's filter and the price's money type, (int) (price * (1 - rate / 10000) + 0.5) in single floats), and the owner's purchase, a build helper's budget charge and the blueprint cost charge the same number (`EventSaleRules.HousingPrice`). Of two running events with the same filter and money type the lower event id counts, as the client keeps them (`gamerule.cpp:1988`). An item without a filter has 100, the client's default (`citemdata.h:255`). KR has 18 rows, all ended (the last stored one 2022-07-07: filters 100 and 200 in mesos and merets and 93 in mesos, rate 8000; a later row to 2023-03-09 with the same values is under feature 23_02, which the ingest leaves out). KR items have the filters 100, 93, 90, 91 and 99; none has 200. Chosen here: a rate is held to 0..10000, so a data error neither raises a price nor makes it negative.
- **Maps the played client does not have:** the server sends a map by id only (RequestFieldEnter) and the client looks the xblock up in its own field table. A pack built for an older client keeps a row for every map id in `table/fielddata.xml` but empties the rows of the maps that version lacks; sent to such a map the client loads nothing and hangs, and it hangs again at the next login because the character is saved there. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest marks every map whose row in that pack has no xblock name, and every map the pack has no row for (`NotInPlayedClient` in the map's property JSON, no new column). A marked map is closed on every way in the server picks: GM warp and goto, portals, taxi, quest and NPC moves, triggers, recall and party warps, channel moves. The player stays where they are and gets a chat line naming the map (the client has no text for this); the callers still send their usual move error. A character saved on a marked map arrives in its return field, else the default one, and a marked map is never used as a return field. Not set, nothing is marked and nothing changes; a pack without that table marks nothing. With the v1.3.2 data pack and the older KR client it was tried with, 13 maps are marked (10 with an empty row, 3 with no row) and 10 portals lead to them, 9 of those from maps that stay open. The mark takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Air taxi (Rotors):** the server now follows the client's rules for how a ride is paid (`CashCallManager` @0x140b030b0, client/ms2/cashcallmanager.cpp). A field counts as visited when it is in the character's visited-maps list, the one ServerEnter sends. The meso ride (mode 3) is refused to a field never visited, unless the character has premium club (free to any field). The client offers merets or advanced walkie-talkies there instead. The meso fare and the meret price (mode 4) are scaled by 1 - the character's air taxi fee reduce rate (special attribute 170), the same way the world map shows them; before, the full price was taken. A plain walkie-talkie (tag air_taxi) only reaches a visited field. An advanced one (air_taxi_advanced) takes 1 for a visited field and 3 for a field never visited, taken across stacks, as the client's s_err_cash_taxi_need_advanced_ticket says.
