Before, any walkie-talkie took 1 to any field. Mode 2, which the client never sends, moved the character for free; it is now refused and logged. Not done: on JP the client asks for 1 advanced walkie-talkie, not 3, and the server does not check the locale.
- **Ground taxi fare:** the server now prices a ride the way the taxi box does (`CTaxiManager::GetTaxiFare`, client/ms2/taximanager.cpp): the Lua taxi charge of the fewest portal hops from the current field to the station, found breadth-first over every portal of `fieldmetadata.xml` that has a target field (`CFieldMetaTable::GetPortalTargets`). As the client loads that list, a field it does not enable has no portals and a portal to such a field is dropped; the server counts a field as enabled when it has the map, and a portal back to its own field or to field 0 is skipped. Portals are one way, so the hops there and back can differ. Before, the server counted steps between adjacent world-map tiles, and 151 of the 2,556 ordered pairs of the 72 taxi stations got another count than the client (with the NA pack's file), so the server took another fare than the box showed; 2000064 to 2000257, for example, was 9 hops in the client and 11 on the server. The ingest reads the list from the played client's `fieldmetadata.xml` (`MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`, the copy the box reads), else from the pack's, into a new table, so it needs a re-ingest; the old world-map graph and its DB-only tests are gone. A station no portal path reaches is refused, where the client's fare would come out negative. Not done: the client keeps the first count it finds for a pair of fields in either direction for the whole session, so after asking B to A it shows that count for A to B too; the server always counts from the current field.
- **Taxi to a field without a navmesh:** a field whose xblock has no navmesh file cannot be created; a taxi ride there (ground, meso or meret air taxi) took the fare and then failed the move. The ride is now refused before the fare is taken, with the move error the failed field gives. The missing file itself is a data step: the navmesh set must be built by the ingest's `--run-navmesh` run under the locale the server runs, so its xblock names match the map data.
- **Dungeon missions the played client does not have:** at room entry the server sends the room's mission ids (DungeonMission 0x0100 mode 0, from `DungeonManager.FillMissions`, which keeps the ids of the ingested `dungeonmission.xml`). The client builds each id with `DungeonMissionCli::Create` (dungeonmissioncli.cpp:84) and asserts on null in `RoomStageDungeonCli::OnInitMissions` (roomstagedungeoncli.cpp:104, MS2_ASSERT_AT 0x68); it gets null when its own `table/dungeonmission.xml` has no row for the id (`DungeonMissionDataTable::ReadFromXml`: rows that pass `IsElementAvailable`, have a type and an id other than 0, the first such row of an id wins) or when the row's type has no registered mission class (16 classes, dungeonmissioncli.cpp:59-74). With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest keeps only the missions that pack's table builds by that rule, so they are never sent; not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: the played pack's rows are filtered with the server's `LOCALE` and features, as the other played-client tables are (the client uses its own locale, KR unless set, and its own feature settings). The NA data pack and the KMS2_DEV client differ here: 128 of the pack's 329 mission ids are not in that client's table. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Dungeon rooms closed on some days:** a room with `limitDayOfWeeks` can be entered only on the listed days. The client reads the list as day numbers with Sunday 1 (`DungeonRoom` `ReadDayBits`, commonfiles/dungeonroom.cpp:408) and tests the bit of the server time's day (`CMsTime::GetDayOfWeek` = tm_wday + 1); its room window shows the days, and its enter path (`UIDungeonRoomInfo::OnEnterSolo`, `OnEnterParty`, `RequestEntry`) sends the request on any day. `DungeonManager.CreateDungeonRoom` now refuses a room on another day with the client's own text, RoomDungeon error 12 `s_room_dungeon_canEnterDayOfWeeks` (`DungeonRecordManagerCli::OnError` 0xc). Before, the server ingested the days and never checked them. In the NA pack these are the five guild raid rooms 26001001-26005001 (no Thursday). Chosen here: the day is the server's local day, as the dungeon cooldowns use it; a party already in a room made on an open day can still enter it. Lobby buttons of the dungeon search window (RoomDungeon 4/12/13/14, no body) stay unhandled: the chaos raid and Lapenta groups have several lobby fields in the client's table and the packet names none.
- **Dungeon rooms open at set hours (DungeonEnterTimeLimit):** the ingest reads the event (value1 an xml list of `dungeonID` and `rangeTime` "hh:mm-hh:mm"; chosen here: read as hours and minutes of the day, since the client's own parse of the row gives 0:00 and it takes the ranges from the event list, so the official reading is not known; a row without value1 is skipped with a log line) and the room's `isLimitTime`, and the event list sends it: the event's id, a count, then per range the event's id again, the room and the start and end as seconds of the day. A room with `isLimitTime` is created only inside a range of a running event, both ends included, on the server's local time of day; otherwise the answer is DungeonRoom error 21 (`s_room_dungeon_NotAllowTime`), and with no range the client's room page already says the room cannot be entered. KR has one running row (room 20007001, 19:00-22:00) and no room that sets `isLimitTime`, so the rule refuses nothing with KR data.
- **Dungeon open periods (DungeonOpenPeriod):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the tag; a row without one is skipped, the client's event does nothing then) and the room's `openPeriodTag` and `openPeriodDate`, and the event list sends it as KMS2 does: the event's id, the tag, then the event's start and end as seconds (`GameEventDungeonOpenPeriod`, `gameevent.cpp:1738`); KMS2 sends such events at nearly every login. The client keeps the periods by tag and its room page shows a tagged room's dates, or that it is closed when no event of the tag runs (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:752`). A room with a date is not created before it (DungeonRoom error 28, `s_room_dungeon_is_not_open_period_date`), and only while the date is ahead the tag is not looked at, as on the room page; from the date on, and with no date, a room with a tag is created only while its tag is open (error 29, `s_room_dungeon_is_not_open_period`). The client keeps one period per tag: an event that starts while another of its tag is kept is dropped (`GameEventDungeonOpenPeriod::OnStart` logs the duplicate and clears its tag) and the tag goes when the kept event ends, so the room is then closed though the dropped event still runs; the server answers as that client, from the events it has held since the session began (those running at login in list order, later ones as they start). The dungeon match and party search ask the same check as the room request (day of the week, hours, open period): a closed room is left out of the rooms searched, and the channel does not make a matched room that has closed for its requester. Chosen here: the date is read in the form of the event times and compared on the server's local time; the room's tag is trimmed as the event's is; events that ended before the channel started are not known to it. KR: 21 rooms have a date, all passed, and none of them has a tag; one room (29006001) has a tag and no row of that tag, and the 4 rows of the type have ended, so that room stays closed, as the client already shows it.
- **Drops of items the played client does not have:** a drop or box result names items by id only, and the client finds an item's data in its own `itemdata/<nnn>.xml`, where an `<item>` counts only when one of its `<environment>` children passes the locale and feature test (`CItemDataTable::LoadItemDataFile`, commonfiles/itemdatatable.cpp:743). With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest leaves out every drop row that names an item that pack lacks: in the server's `individualItemDrop` (the table the box, gacha and monster drops use) and in the client tables `table/individualitemdrop*.xml` with their region copies (gacha, gear box, event, quest and the others). It also leaves out such items from the reward content item rows (`rewardcontentitemtable`; for example item 20302171 in row 118, levels 50-99, is in the KMS2 dev client's table but not in its item data). The log has one line per table with rows dropped and kept. A drop group left with no rows goes, and so does a box left with no groups: opening that box's item then fails with `s_err_cannot_open_multi_itembox_inventory_fail` before the box is used, as for any box id the table lacks, and a monster drop from it gives nothing. A reward level range left with no items stays and gives no items. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: a row with a second item (`itemID2`, the other gender's item) goes when either item is missing; the played pack's environments are filtered with the server's `LOCALE` and features, as the other played-client tables are; an empty box is refused rather than opened to nothing, so no box is used up for nothing. About 4,000 of the NA data pack's item ids are not in the KMS2 dev client's item data. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Original (KMS) drop table, opt-in:** the server pack's `Server.m2d` keeps the drop table it started from next to today's as `table/Server/individualItemDrop_Final_Backup.xml` (4,637 boxes, 45,512 rows; today's `individualItemDrop_Final.xml` has 5,363 boxes, 53,281 rows). With `MS2_ORIGINAL_DROP_TABLE=1` (or `true`) the ingest reads the backup for the server's `individualItemDrop` instead. Both files have one layout and are read with the same parser type, so rows keep the locale and feature filter of `LOCALE` and the played-client drop filter above (one log line names the file read with its box and row counts). After the filter: with `LOCALE` NA 32,121 rows from the backup against 32,659 from today's; with KR 39,874 against 38,684. The backup has no NA rows (its locale rows are KR and CN), so with NA only its rows without a locale are kept. Unset, empty, `0` or `false` reads today's table; any other value stops the ingest. Chosen here: an environment switch, default off, so an NA ingest is unchanged; only the drop table, because the server pack's option backups (`ItemOptionRandom_Backup.xml`, `itemOptionVariation_Backup.xml`) replace tables the ingest does not read (item options come from the client's `itemoption/` files in `Xml.m2d`); the parser has no field for the backup's box attribute `probVer` (on 67 boxes, all `1`), so it is not read, and a box's `feature` attribute is not read in either file. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Field drops as KMS2 sends them (FieldAddItem):** the u16 after the rarity is the drop kind, which the server sent as 0 for every drop. KMS2 sends 21 for an npc's death drop, 24 for a hit drop, 33 for an interact object's drop, 1005 for an item a player drops, 1062 for a trigger's drop, 3100 plus the mastery type for a gathering drop (3103 mining, 3104 gathering, 3105 breeding, 3106 farming) and 3200 for a pet's drop. Receivers: KMS2 sends death and hit drops, boss or not, as type 0 (owner only) with a character id: a death drop to each player who dealt damage, a hit drop to the hitter; in a party each member who dealt damage gets its own death drop and a member who did not gets none. The server sent boss drops and global hit drops as type 2 to everyone, and its party rule is replaced by this. Interact object drops now go to the user, with no owner object. Times: an unpicked drop goes after 90 s (was 120 s), a player's own drop after 60 s, and a trigger's drop after its spawn point's Lifetime (seconds; 90 s without one). A drop stays owner only until it goes; KMS2 shows no switch to free for all, so none is added. In the item stream an item without a colour now has colour index -1, as KMS2 sends it (was 0). The transfer flag is sent without Split (2): the client's rule (`CItemTransfer::ComputeRule`, commonfiles/itemsocketsystemutil.cpp:3097) knows only trade (4), binding (8) and the limited trade count (16), and KMS2 sends a tradable item as 4; the server still uses Split for its own split check. Not settled: corpse drops (sent as 24) and breakable or function cube drops (0) are not confirmed; the mesos of a boss's death drop are open.
- **Refused pickups are answered (FieldPickupItem 0x2D):** a pickup request the server refused for a drop that stays on the field (a bag with no room for the item, or a drop owned by another player) got no answer. The client marks a drop when it asks for it (`CDropItem::SendRequestItemPickup`, client/ms2/dropitem.cpp:936), and its auto loot and pet loot pass over a marked drop (client/ms2/mypc.cpp:4728, client/ms2/worldservice_1.cpp:6338), so a drop refused once was never asked for again, even after the player made room. KMS2 answers a refusal with picked 0, the drop's object id and mode 1 (55 packets in 7 sessions, 34 of them 0.16 to 0.64 s after the request for that drop; 52 of the 55 drops were picked up later), and the client clears the mark on mode 1 (`CSceneStateGame::OnFieldPickupItem` @0x141712420, client/ms2/scenestategame.cpp:2420). The server now sends that answer to the requester from the item and meso pickup handlers; a drop that is already gone gets none, as its removal was sent. The meso pickup handler now also refuses a meso drop owned by another player, as the item handler does.
  - Not settled: why KMS2 refused those 55 (the requests came 0.9 to 58 s after the drop appeared, 48 for meso drops), so the server adds no refusal reason of its own.
- **Item box announcements:** a drop table row with `isAnnounce` (1,532 rows in today's table, 1,855 in the backup; already ingested as `Announce`) now makes an item box open announce the item to every channel, the way the client prints `s_msg_item_open_item_announce_to_world` ("{0} has earned {2} from {1}."): a system notice line `3,<item uid>,3,<player name>:<box item id>`, which the client splits on `,` into source, uid, kind and args and, for kind 3, the args on `:` into exactly the name and the box item id (`FUN_140d2ab10`, `FUN_140d2b190`). The item is saved first and sent as a link, as the enchant announcement does. It covers every open of `ItemBoxManager` (item boxes, boxes with a key, select boxes, gacha, Lulu and pet boxes). Chosen here: the link is the inventory item that took the drop (the new row, or the first stack it merged into); an item that goes to the mail because the inventory is full, and a drop that is not an inventory item (currency, medals, furnishings), is not announced; mob, fishing and other drops with the flag are not announced, as the client text names a box item; a world server that does not answer only costs the announcement. Not tried live.
- **Select boxes give the picked row:** a select box (`SelectItemBox`, parameters "select count,individual drop box id") sends the rows the player ticked, as indexes joined by `,`, into the list the client's dialog shows (`UISelectItemBoxDialog::OnLoadComplete`, `ItemDropTable::GetBoxItems`): every group of the box in data order, each group's entries in order, an entry without item data left out, the other gender's entries left out in a smart gender group, and in a group with a smart drop rate the entries not recommended for the player's job (a beginner counts as a knight; an entry without recommended jobs is listed for all), an entry's second item as a row of its own; no level or drop count test. The server read the first parameter, the select count (1 on all 382 KR select boxes), as a group id and indexed group 1 only, so in a box like 320000044 (six smart gender groups, two with one item for each of 11 jobs) every row past the first gave nothing, a box without group 1 (320000095) was rolled instead, and the box was used up either way; a pick of several rows failed to parse. The server now builds the same list (`ItemDropManager.SelectBoxRows`), takes exactly the select count of distinct rows (`ItemBoxRules.ParseSelection`), and uses up the box only when every picked row is in the list. 138 of the 382 KR select boxes named a box the server data lacked; 40 now take theirs from the played client's table (next bullet), and the other 98 name a box with no KR row in either table, so they still give nothing and are not used up. An entry with a second item is two rows, and each gives only the item it shows; a random open of the same box gives both. Quest rewards hold no pick in this client: the complete reward list is shown and given whole for the player's job, and the quest complete, level-up guide and level-up package sends carry only the quest id. Not tried live.
- **Item box odds:** all box kinds (item box, box with key, gacha/capsule, pet box) roll one drop table, re-read against the table (4,170 boxes, 9,596 groups). Changed: a smart group whose rows carry only job weights (48 groups, weight 0 and properJobWeight 1) gave the first row of the job every time and now rolls among the job's rows by their job weight; a smart group with no weight of any kind (237 groups) gives one of the job's rows that the player can get, each as likely (before, the first); a job with no row of its own weight in a smart group gets nothing from it (before, the weightless roll fell on the first row, another job's item; the tables are built on this: KR box 320000497 has one group of one row per job, each row with a plain weight too, so a plain-weight roll would give every job's item; a box whose every group is for other jobs, as an old gear box opened by a later job, is used up for nothing: chosen here, what KMS2 does with such a box is not known); a smart group in which no row has a job weight (box 330002235 group 1, the accessory of box item 20301832) is rolled by the plain weight, as before; a row listed twice counts twice and a negative weight counts 0; an amount range with max below min gives min; a box whose fixed item cannot be made still rolls its table (before, the box and key were used up and the roll skipped); a box with a key takes the key and the box in one step, so a box that cannot be taken leaves the key and gives nothing (before, the key was taken and the items given). The smart drop rate itself is not a chance (groups of rate 1 with no weights, per-job groups of rate 100) and is only read as above or not above 0: chosen here, the data shows no other meaning. A group that gives more than one row can give the same row again: chosen here. Amount, rarity and count rolls take a seeded random in tests.
  - Chosen here (the client does not see odds): rows of a group without weights are each as likely; every smartDropRate above 0 (1 to 10000 in the table) is read as "job filtered", as before; picks in a group with a count above 1 can repeat.
- **Box used from the bag sends no ItemBox:** KMS2 answers an item box, select box or gacha box used with RequestItemUse with the inventory packets alone (the box first, then its items; a gacha box ends with the gacha script). `ItemBox` is only the answer to RequestItemBox, the multi-open dialog (item id, boxes opened, 2 = done or 4 = stopped by a full bag), and the client hands it to that dialog. The fork sent it after every item box and gacha box use; it no longer does. Lulu box uses still send it (KMS2 has no such use to compare).
- **Key boxes take nothing they cannot open:** a key box (OpenItemBoxLullu) takes its key, and its box or the box price in meso, only when all of them are there and the roll gave items; before, the key was taken first and lost when there was no box, or no meso with the pay box ticked. When an open of several boxes or capsules stops part way (key boxes, the simple key box, gacha capsules), the result window packet is still sent for those already opened; before, their items were given without it.
- **Bag expansion coupons are free:** an expansion coupon (`ExpandInven`, parameters "rows,tab name") took 390 Meret on top of the coupon, and was refused without them. The client asks before the use with its plain item-use box, which names no price (only the bag window's Meret button shows `InventoryExpandPrice1Row`), so the coupon is now the whole price. The client shows "expanded" for the `ItemUse` answer, so `ItemInventory` 10 (the same message) is sent for the Meret button only: Meret update, tab count, completion, as KMS2 sends. The tab is found by the client's tab names (`CItemTabNameTable`): the data's consumable tab coupon is `activeskill`, which the fork read as an unknown name and gave to the Misc tab; `lapenshard` was missing; a name the client does not know now expands nothing. The GM `expand` command takes no Meret either.
- **Level potion order:** KMS2 takes the potion out of the bag first and then sends the level up; the potion was taken last, after the quests and trophies. A potion that cannot be taken now changes nothing.
- **Level up and adventure level up order:** KMS2 follows a level up (after ExpUp) with the cleared death penalty (RevivalConfirm), LevelUp, the skill tree (Job 1, broadcast with the player's object id), the stat block and then the trophies; the trophies went before the death penalty and the stats, and the skill tree was not sent, so the skill window showed the old level's points until the next field. An adventure (prestige) level up is Prestige 2 (the level), Prestige 7 (the missions), the level's item and then the trophies; the trophies went first and the item before the level.
- **Level up below the death penalty level, hot bars, job change by quest:** the cleared death penalty of a level up (RevivalConfirm) is sent at every level, as KMS2 sends it; below the penalty's minimum level it was left out. After the stat block KMS2 sends the hot bars (KeyTable 7), now sent too. A job change at the end of a quest (the awakening, `changeJob` table) now counts the `job` condition and marks the player's job for the field's update, as the change at the job npc does.
- **Drop boxes from the played client's table:** the server pack's drop table (`table/Server/individualItemDrop_Final.xml`) holds 237 boxes with NA or CN rows only, so under LOCALE=KR the filter left them empty: among them 138 boxes of KR open items (`OpenItemBox`) gave nothing, and 36 boxes of 40 KR select items listed rows the server could not give. The played KR client's own copy (`table/individualitemdrop_final.xml`, which `ItemDropTable` reads and the select dialog lists from) has KR rows for them. At ingest, with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`, a box with no available row in the server table is taken from the client's copy (`ClientDropBoxes`); a box the server table has rows for keeps the server's rows. The client's rows have the item ids, counts, rank, enchant level, socket data and smart drop settings, but no weights (`weight`, `properJobWeight`, `imProperJobWeight`), drop counts, grades, announce, bind, quest or map conditions, minimum level or server drop. Where the server's NA group of the same id has the same items (each item id matched in order), those values come from the server row (261 groups). The other 395 groups keep the client's rows as they are: a plain group without weights gives one item at an equal chance and draws once, and a smart drop group gives the player's job item. No rate is made up. The ingest logs the count. 364 KR open boxes, 9 gacha boxes and 1 key box have no KR row in either table. Not tried live.
- **Enchant scroll grade weights and disableBreak:** the server pack's copy of the enchant scroll table (`table/Server/enchantScroll.xml`, 2,029 rows, 1,745 ids) has two attributes the client's copy (`table/enchantscroll.xml`, the one the parser reads) lacks: `successProp`, one weight per grade of the row's grade list, and `disableBreak` (3 rows). The ingest now reads both by scroll id (`ServerEnchantScroll`) into the scroll's `Rates` and `DisableBreak`. A random scroll (type 3, 5 ids) used to give each grade of its list with the same chance; it now rolls by the weights, e.g. 4970/3600/1000/500/90/40 for +6 to +11 and 517/9362/121 for +8 to +10. The scroll dialog packet sends the row's `disableBreak` byte, which the client reads after the scroll type (`UIEnchantScrollDialog` FUN_140f3b920) and shows as `s_enchantscroll_desc_disable_break`; it was always 0. With `LOCALE` KR, 1,621 scrolls are ingested, all with weights, 3 with `disableBreak`. Chosen here: the weights are relative (one random scroll's add up to 10200, not 10000); every other row has the single weight 10000, so those scrolls still always succeed and the dialog's rate field stays 10000; `isLockTrade` (30 rows) is not read: the client reads it into its scroll data (commonfiles/enchantscrolldata.cpp:85) but no client code uses it. Data ingested before this has no weights and keeps the even pick. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Enchant Crystal Fragment fee:** the enchant window's ingredient list (Ophelia and Peachy, `uiitemenchantdialog.cpp:1617`) shows the Crystal Fragments a player is short of as a meso cost, 1,000 mesos each (s_itemenchant_fee, in the KR string table), and the client sends the enchant without checking the ingredients. The server used to refuse it with "Not enough materials."; it now takes the fragments the player has and the fee for the rest. A player who cannot pay the fee, or is short of Onyx or Chaos Onyx, is still refused with that message.
- **Enchant stage order:** when an item is staged KMS2 sends the staged item (ItemEnchant) and then the smith's line (NpcTalk); the two were sent the other way round.
- **Enchant result order and repeat:** KMS2 sends the result (ItemEnchant success or failure) and then the trophy updates; the trophies went first. After a result the staged item's cost, rates and fodder list are computed again for its new grade: a second request without a new stage paid the first grade's cost and rolled its rate, and could use the first request's fodder and charges again. It now pays the current grade's cost, with no fodder or charges, or is refused when the materials are not held. The dialog's retry button (`UIItemEnchantDialog::OnRetry`) sends only talk event 4, which was ignored: the window kept the cost and the listed copies of the enchant before, and its Enchant button did nothing. As KMS2 does after each result, the same item is staged again on that event (the stage packet is the only one on which the client clears the listed copies). A request without the fodder its grade needs, or without the materials, is also answered with the client's "not enough materials" line (ItemEnchant error 3); before, only the smith's line was sent, which needs an open talk script.
- **Enchant transform (TransferEnchant 0x77):** the inventory menu's enchant transform window (feature EnchantTransform01, on in both locales' live settings) lists the gear-tab items the client's `CItemEnchantTransformTable::CanTransform` accepts: a row of `table/itemenchanttransform.xml` for the item, and the item not locked or waiting out its unlock time. It shows the row's meso fee and output items and sends only the item uid once the player has the fee; the answer is the empty TransferEnchant packet (0x104), after which the window offers a retry. The server repeats those checks, takes the item and the fee and gives the outputs (id, rarity, count). The row lookup now follows the client's key, `{0}-{1}-{2}-{3}-(4)` of item id, equip slot, enchant level, rarity and required level: the last part is the literal `(4)`, so the required level and the row's `inputLimitLevel` take no part, and a later row with the same key replaces an earlier one. The handler before this also compared the required level with `inputLimitLevel` and so could refuse an item the window offered. No row key repeats in the KR (1,257 rows) or NA (336) data.
  - Chosen here: what happens to the item and the outputs is not in the client (it reads no answer field); the item is taken and the outputs are added, as the window's fee and output list suggest. A request is refused, with no answer and no change, when the outputs do not each find a free slot in their tab (the item's own slot counts in its tab); the client does not check space. The client's content shutdown 0x54 only shows a notice and still sends; the server does not check it.
- **Item sockets from the socket table for every equipment slot, and from drop rows:** a new item got sockets only when it was an earring, necklace or ring; any other item, including the outfits whose item data names a socket row (`socketDataId`, e.g. 1002 or 9001-9008), got none. The client fills sockets for any equipment item (property type 1, `CItem::FUN_14052b420`, commonfiles/item.cpp) from the item's socket row: the row's `maxCount`, with `fixOpenCount` open (`CItemSocketTable::GetMaxSocketCount` / `GetInitialSocketCount`, commonfiles/itemsocketsystemutil.cpp:1448); without a socket row it asks `calcItemSocketMaxCount`. The server now does the same (`ItemStatsCalculator.CreateSockets`), and it passes the item's level limit and skin type to `calcItemSocketMaxCount` as the client does (it passed the option level factor and the skin flag). An outfit (non-zero `skinType`) has sockets in the client only while the `SkinSocket` feature is on (`IsSocketFeatureEnabled`, itemsocketsystemutil.cpp:1499; KR and CN in the pack's feature table), so the item ingest keeps an outfit's `socketDataId` only then (`ItemSocketRules.SocketId`). A drop box row's `socketDataID` (copied into the drop result by `ItemDropResult::Set`, commonfiles/itemdroptable.cpp:1023) now replaces the item's own socket row for the item made from that row: 23 of today's 49 such rows differ from the item's own, e.g. accessories without one get 1003 to 1005. With `LOCALE` KR, 5,845 outfits and 70 other equipment items keep a socket row; 172 pet items name one but get no sockets (not equipment). Chosen here: the drop table has no skin type, so a drop row's socket row is used on an outfit only when the outfit kept its own; when the row's id is not in the socket table, the item keeps the sockets it was made with; the client's tooltip counts the maximum from the item data's own row, so a drop-row item may show a different maximum line there while its socket rows come from the packet; items already stored keep their sockets. The drop row's `disableBreak` is still not used. Works on data ingested before this too; the `SkinSocket` gate on outfits takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Shop rows of items the played client does not have:** the NPC shop list sends each row's item, and the client reads it into an item object (`CNpcShopProductCli::ReadPacket`, client/ms2/npcshopproductbase.cpp:139) and reads its stream (`CItem::ReadFromStream`, commonfiles/item.cpp:2655) even when no item data was found for the id (item.cpp:2760), so opening such a shop crashes the client. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest leaves out the `shop_game` rows whose item the played pack lacks, with the same item test as the drops above; the log has one line with rows dropped and kept. In the NA data pack, 49 of 15,772 rows (before the feature filter) in 11 shops sell such an item; the meret custom and beauty coupon tables name none. The shop stays with its other rows. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: a row whose payment item (`paymentItemID`) the played pack lacks goes too (none in the NA pack today). Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Items the played client does not have are never created:** the client reads any item packet into an item object and dereferences its item data even when it found none for the id (`CItem::ReadFromStream`, commonfiles/item.cpp:2655, after the lookup at item.cpp:2760 failed), so an item it lacks crashes it when the server gives one (quest, mail, box, reward or any other path). With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the item ingest marks each item the played pack has no item data for (the same item test as the drops above; `ItemMetadataProperty.NotInPlayedClient`), and the server's item factories (`ItemDropManager.CreateItem` in the game server, the world server's item helper) return no item for a marked one, as for an id without item data. The log has one line with the marked and unmarked counts. Not set, or data ingested before the mark, nothing changes. Chosen here: the mark is checked where items are made, not at every caller; a caller that used something up before asking for the item behaves as it does for an id without item data. Items already stored are not sent either (next bullet). GM commands that build an item directly (the pet command) are not checked. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Stored items the played client does not have are not loaded:** an item the item mark above covers that is already in the game DB (from before the mark, or from another client's data) would crash the client the same way when its packet is sent. Every load of an item row (`GameStorage`: inventory, storage, equips, mail attachments, saved hairs, black market and the other lookups by item uid; UGC market listings; shop buyback) now treats a marked item the way it treats an id without item data: no item is made from the row and it is left out of what the server holds and sends. The row itself is not changed or deleted (saves update only the items the server holds, and mail deletion leaves attachment rows), so the item is back once the data no longer marks it. Not set, or data ingested before the mark, nothing changes. Chosen here: hidden, not deleted or converted; while hidden, its inventory slot counts as free, so a new item may take that slot, the same as for an id without item data today.
- **NPCs the played client does not have are never spawned:** the server sends an NPC by id, and the client looks its data up in its own `npcdata/<nnn>.xml`, where an `<npc>` counts only when its id is not 0 and one of its `<environment>` children passes the locale and feature test (`NpcDataTableCli::Load`, client/ms2/npcdatacli.cpp:311, with `CNpcData::Load`, commonfiles/npcdata.cpp:771). For any other id `FindNpcData` (npcdata.cpp:1047) finds nothing and `CSceneStateGame::OnFieldAddNpc` (client/ms2/scenestategame.cpp:3533) creates no NPC, so the server's NPC stood, fought and counted where the player saw nothing. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest marks each NPC that pack lacks (`NpcMetadataBasic.NotInPlayedClient`, one log line with the counts), and `FieldManager.SpawnNpc` returns no NPC for a marked one, as it does for any spawn that fails: spawn points, triggers, AI summons, mob spawns, the GM `npc` command (now an error) and `RequestNpcCreate` all go through it. About 300 of the NA data pack's NPC ids are not in the KMS2 dev client's NPC data. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: pets are not checked (their NPC comes from the pet item, which the item mark already covers); a trigger that waits on a marked NPC's death or count waits as it would for a spawn that failed; a mob spawn group spawns only its other NPCs. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **NPC stats from the played client's data:** an NPC's stat block (hp, ndd, pap, map and the rest of `<stat>`) is the one in the played client's own `npcdata/<nnn>.xml` when `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` is set and that client has the NPC, taken as it is with no hidden stat added. The environment is chosen as the client chooses it (the higher feature level, then the client's locale, then no locale; `CFeatureManager::SelectFeatureElement`, commonfiles/featuremanager.cpp:1049), and the client reads `<stat>` by the stat names and adds nothing (`CNpcData::LoadXml`, commonfiles/npcdata.cpp:1529). Before, the stats were the server pack's base plus the hidden adds (`hiddenhpadd`, `hiddenwapadd`, ...03, ...04) of the HiddenStatAdd01-04 features on for the ingest's locale: with LOCALE=NA that added the HiddenStatAdd03 values, so about 1,300 NPCs (mostly field mobs) had up to about 4 times the HP the KR client has, and mobs such as 24002101 hit with 145,044 pap where the KR data has 58,305. An NPC the played client does not have (it is never spawned), or an ingest without a played client, keeps the old path. Needs a re-ingest with LOCALE set to the played client's region.
  - Chosen here: the played client's block, not the hidden adds gated by the played region's features. The KR client knows only HiddenStatAdd01/02 (featuremanager.cpp:374) and adds neither; its data has them baked in already (24002101: pap 58,305 = 50,005 + `hiddenwapadd` 8,300 of the server pack, hp 167,074 = 37,728 + 129,346), and about 635 bosses carry KMS values that are neither base nor base + any add. Gating by the KR locale gives base only, which is wrong for both groups; only the client's own numbers are right for all of them.
- **Quests the played client does not have are never started or sent:** the client's quest list (Quest 0x16, `CQuestManager::OnQuest` @0x1416cbd90, client/ms2/questmanager_2.cpp) reads a quest's progress only when `CQuest::Init` (questmanager_1.cpp:127) found the quest in its own quest table, so one unknown quest in the list shifts the read of every quest after it. The client keeps a table quest only when its `feature` is enabled (`QuestDataTable::LoadQuestData`, commonfiles/questdata.cpp:1797; no locale test). With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the quest ingest marks each quest the played pack's `questdata/questdata.xml` lacks (`QuestMetadataBasic.NotInPlayedClient`), and the game server does not start a marked quest (also not with the GM bypass, and not as an auto-start at login), does not offer it in an NPC's quest list, and leaves it out of the quest list sent at login. A marked quest that is already stored gets no later packet either: condition progress (including the user-find action and the exploration auto-complete) skips it, and completing (also the GM and trigger bypasses), abandoning or tracking it is refused without a packet; the GM quest command names it as not in the played client's table. The log has one line with the marked and unmarked counts. Not set, or data ingested before the mark, nothing changes. Takes effect with the next ingest. Chosen here: a marked quest already stored for a character or account stays stored (not deleted) and is only not sent; the skip-chapter path may still store a marked quest as completed (no packet but the filtered quest list), so a later quest that requires it can start; the expire request answers with the ids it dropped (a quest in progress with an end time; see Alliance missions).
- **Quests started again (daily, weekly):** a completed quest is started again as the client allows it (`CQuestManagerCommon::vfunc_o10_21`, commonfiles/questdata.cpp:376), from the quest's basic `repeatable` and `usePeriod`: 0 never; 2 (daily) on any calendar day other than the one it was last started; 3 (weekly) from the start of the `usePeriod` weekday (sun to sat) after the last start; any other value at once. Times are the server's local time. The stored quest is reused: state started, new start time, fresh conditions, its completion count kept; the client takes the Start packet as a new quest. NPC talks offer such a quest again too. Both values are new parts of the quest table and read 0 (done once, as before) until the data ingest of this build is run. Guild quests (type 6) are left done once: the client adds a wait of a day or a week after a time the server does not keep. A `usePeriod` that is a duration rather than a day changes nothing here.
- **Quest helper "go to" (Quest 0x0C):** the quest window's go-to button moves the player to the quest's `gotoNpc` field only when the client shows it (`CQuestCommon::FUN_1407441f0`, commonfiles/pvpinfo.cpp:238): `gotoNpc enable` is set and `gotoField` is not 0, the quest is started with every condition at its goal, the player is not on `gotoField` already and not on a field where the quest's complete npc stands. Before, the server moved the player for any quest id it was sent, also one not started or not done yet. The fields of each npc come from the `<npc>` rows of the played client's `fieldmetadata.xml`, read into the same new table as the taxi portals (an npc the server has no data for is skipped, as in the client). Not done: the client drops an npc field outside the row's active time span; the server keeps every row.
- **Quest accept and complete answers:** the Quest sub-types the server sends and reads have the layouts of KMS2's packets (634 sessions compared by sub-type); the gaps were in the refusals and the order.
  - A refused accept (unknown quest, a quest held or completed that cannot start again, an npc that is not on the field, a remote quest of another map) is answered with the client's own text (Quest 0, s_quest_error_accept_fail). It had no answer, and the accept talk stayed without one.
  - A quest whose accept items do not fit in the bag is not started (Quest 0, s_quest_error_inventory_full). It was started and the items that did not fit were lost. The same holds for the other ways a quest starts: a quest scroll whose quest does not start is answered with the same text and stays in the bag (it was used up), the quests the client starts by itself and a returning user's quests answer a full bag with the text too. A forced accept (a map script's SetQuestAccept, a command) is never refused by the bag: the quest starts and the items that do not fit go by mail; a forced accept that still fails is logged.
  - A complete request for a quest that is not held, is already completed or has an open condition gives nothing and is answered with Quest 0 (s_quest_error_consume_fail); a dead player gets s_quest_error_fail_complete_by_dead. A second complete request for the same quest gives the reward once.
  - Complete order as KMS2 sends it: Quest 4 first, then the meso, the exp and the items. The meso went out before the quest's own packet. A full bag is answered with the bag's notice (ItemInventory error, code 13 `s_err_inventory`) and the quest stays in progress.
  - Quest talk at a held quest's npc: with an open condition, or at an npc that is not the quest's complete npc, the talk opens the quest's progress script (state 200 and up, as KMS2 sends it, with the close button); the complete script (300 and up) opens only at the complete npc with every condition met. The talk conditions (talk_in, dialogue) are counted before the script is picked, so a quest whose last condition is the talk itself opens its complete script in the same talk. They are counted for every talk request at an npc of the field, also one that opens no talk window.
  - The complete request's npc: the client writes the talk's npc object id, or 0 from the popup talk (`UIPopupNpcTalkDialog`). A named npc must be on the player's field and be the quest's complete npc; a request without one is taken only for a quest that is completed remotely or has no complete npc (the 10 of 149 KMS2 requests with 0 are 8 remote quests and 2 without a complete npc). A remote completion that names a field (`remoteComplete` `requireField`; 22 of the 499 remote quests of the KR data, all 8 of the KMS2 ones) is taken on that field only: the client offers the remote completion only while the player is in exactly that field (`CStoryQuest::FUN_14180fc80`, client/ms2/stringtable.cpp:334-338), so it sends it from there; the field was never checked. At the quest's npc the field does not matter. Quest 0x18 (the daily mission and level guide claims) names no field and is not checked. Anything else gives nothing and is answered with Quest 0 (s_quest_error_consume_fail). The id was not read against the field, so a quest could be completed from any map.
  - The complete request's reward choice: the client always writes 0 (it has no choice among a quest's rewards), so the value is read and not used.
  - Abandon (Quest 6, `UIQuestDialog::GiveUpQuest`; no KMS2 session has one): the answer is the same mode with the quest id. Abandoning a quest that was completed before and started again (a daily or weekly one after its reset) puts the row back as the completion left it: completed, with its count and that completion's start and end times, so the quest the client offers again can be accepted again in the same period. A restart overwrites both times, so the row keeps them aside until the quest is completed or abandoned (two new columns of the quest table, `LastStartTime` and `LastEndTime`, migration `QuestLastTimes`; neither is sent). A row restarted before the migration goes back with both times 0, which also reads as completed before the reset. Before, the row was deleted, which opened the quest's one-time rewards again.
  - Not done: Quest 0 with code 8 (KMS2 sent it once, with no request before it): `CQuestManager::ShowQuestErrorMessage` has no text for it and only ends the talk, so it is not sent. The accept items of an abandoned quest stay in the bag.
- **Quest condition spawner:** a kill of a mob of a region spawn (MS2RegionSpawn) now counts for the spawner condition, with the region spawn id as its code and the map as its target (the client registers the name, `RegisterConditionTypes`, commonfiles/conditionutil.cpp:530). Before, no kill counted, so quests such as 10001742 (level 10, kill 10 mobs of spawns 1 to 4 of one map) could not be finished. NPCs a trigger spawned have no region spawn and do not count.
- **Quest condition dungeon_clear_group:** a dungeon clear now also counts for dungeon_clear_group, with the room's groupType as its code (1 normal, 2 raid, 3 chaos raid; the same field the trigger dungeon_level reads). Before, it never counted, so quests such as 74000005 (clear 1 adventure dungeon, code 1) and 74000035 (clear 1 raid, code 2) could not be finished.
- **Quest condition enter_otherhouse:** entering a home field of another account now counts for enter_otherhouse (the quest rows have no codes or target). Before, it never counted, so the quests to visit another player's house (70300001 and others) could not be finished. Each real entry counts, from another map or another home; coming back into the same home from that home does not count again (a channel change or relog never lands in a home, as both go to the saved field). The client has no rule on distinct homes.
- **Quest condition quest_daily:** completing a daily quest (basic repeatable 2) now counts for quest_daily, with the quest id as the code (every quest the quest_daily rows list is a daily one, e.g. 30000359, 80020170). Before, it never counted, so those quests and achievements could not be finished. It reads the quest table's repeat value, which is 0 until the data ingest of this build is run.
- **Quest condition quest_guide:** completing a daily mission (quest type 3, the navigator's guide missions) now counts for quest_guide (the rows have no codes and read "complete a daily mission", quest 74000017, achievement 22300047). Before, it never counted.
- **Quest condition openItemBox:** each item box opened (any box function: item box, box with key, gacha, Lulu and pet boxes) now counts for openItemBox, with the box's item id as the code; a multi open counts each box. Before, it never counted, so quest 80020127 (open 200 Lucky Boxes, 20302745) could not be finished.
- **Quest condition item_exist with a map:** an item_exist row with a map target (quest drops of one map: 20001480 Triage, 20001790, 50100050 and others, lv31-60) now counts an item only when it is collected on that map; rows without a target count it anywhere, as before. Before, such rows never counted.
- **Condition npc_lasthit:** the player whose hit kills an NPC now counts npc_lasthit, with the NPC id as the code (141 achievement grades, field bosses such as Furious Baphomet 23000024 in 21231001). Before, it never counted.
- **Condition controller:** taking a placed furniture into use (sitting down, switching it on) now counts controller, with the furniture's item id as the code (111 achievement grades and 52 quests, such as Retro TV 50400002). Before, it never counted. Leaving the furniture does not count.
- **Condition field_mission with codes:** a completed field mission now counts with its quest id as the code, so the 16 achievement grades that list field missions (such as the binocular views 72000149-72000153, or the 71 missions 72000736-72000806) count only those. Before, the code was never sent and these grades never counted.
- **Condition play_ensenble_time with a map:** ensemble play time now carries the map as the code, so the 14 quests that ask for an ensemble on a given map (Wedding Village, Tria, Queenstown stages and others, with a party of 2) count there. Before, these quests never counted.
- **Condition hero_achieve:** an achievement reaching a grade is now checked against the row's achievement ids (codes) and grades (target), as the update already sent both. The 12 achievement grades that ask for a set of achievements (such as Master of the Arena, or A Musician Possessed: grade 1 of 3 of 25 music trophies) and the 2 quests that ask for a grade of one trophy never counted before.
- **Condition interact_object_rep with a target:** the interaction now carries the object's interact type as the target, so the one row that asks for a type (the binocular event quest 89050108, target 2 telescope) counts on any telescope. Before, it never counted.
- **Condition quest_alliance:** completing an alliance mission (quest type 9) now counts with the mission's alliance as the code, as the rows read ("complete a Maple Alliance mission", code 2: the weekly drills 93000188-93000192 and the Kritias weeklies 94000000 and on, codes 6 to 8). Before, none of the 35 quests could progress. Completing an alliance field quest (type 8) counts the same way for quest_field ("complete a Maple Alliance field mission", 93000159-93000163).
- **Guild join condition:** founding a guild, accepting an invite or an accepted application counts for guild_join (70300010, 71000032 "Join a guild").
- **Guild check-in condition:** an accepted guild check-in counts for guild_attendance (70300011 "Guild Check-In").
- **Guild recall scroll:** the guild recall scroll (item function RecallGuild, 20300054 "Summons Guild") calls the other guild members to the user, as the party scroll calls the party. The client checks the content switch, the guild and that another member is there, then casts the recall (`RecallGuild`, unnamed/unit_140d1ae30_itemfunctiondispatch.cpp). The server had no case for it: it logged the use, kept the scroll and called no one. Now the channel counts the members as the client does (`CGuildInfo::FUN_1404d3400`, commonfiles/guilddef_1.cpp:2232: online, in a field that is not a tutorial, the user left out) and refuses on a field without the cashCall recall flag. The World server then sends the offer to every online member's channel, which shows it as RecallScroll type 1 with the user's name, map and end time. The scroll is used up only when the offer went out. A member who accepts (RecallScroll answer type 1) is moved to the user while both are still in the guild and the user is still on that map. Chosen here: the offer stays open 300 seconds, as the party's (the server pack has no keep-time row for either).
- **Bank and black market call items:** the bank call passes (item function OpenBank, 20300087-20300089, 20300100, 20300528) and the black market call passes (CallBlackMarket, 20300009, 20300010, 20300021-20300023) were refused with a log line, so the client's cast ended in nothing. The client's function table (unit_140d1ae30_itemfunctiondispatch.cpp) starts its cash call for both (`CCashCallManager` @0x140b03d70 and @0x140b03980, cashcallmanager.cpp). At the cast's end `StateCashCall::SendRequest` sends the plain item use with the price, 0, as text. The server now answers the bank with StorageInventory 14 (`CStore::OnReset`, store.cpp:869), which clears the client's copy and opens the bank window (0x4e47); the window then asks for the items itself (StorageInventory Load). It answers the black market with BlackMarket 9 (`CBlackMarketMan::OnBlackMarket`, blackmarketman.cpp:143), which opens the market window (0x4e97). As the client does, a dead player gets neither, and the market is refused on a field whose cashCall market flag is off (cashCallMarketProhibit). The items are 1-slot passes (1, 7 or 30 days, or no end) and are kept (chosen here: no source says one use takes them). The medic call items (CallMedic, 20300011, 20300032, 20301983, stacks of 999) cure the death penalty, not health: the client (`CCashCallManager` @0x140b03590) starts the call only for a live player whose penalty count (PC m_n10ec, set by RevivalConfirm) is above 0, on a field whose cashCall medic flag is on (cashCallMedicProhibit off). The server checks the same (the count only, not the penalty's end time: a penalty that ran out keeps its count until the next death or revival, and the client still casts the call), uses one item and clears the penalty; the RevivalConfirm with count 0 makes the client play its cure effect (`CSceneStateGame::OnRevivalConfirm`, scenestategame.cpp:7356). The doctor's resolve_panelty condition is not counted for the medic (no source says it is). An expired item is not used at all: the client's `UseItem` (@0x140d1bd60) asks `CanUseItem`, which refuses an expired item that is not a pet and has no maid id (`CItem::FUN_140528980`, item.cpp:1717, notice 0xe1); the server now drops any item use that the client refuses this way, so an expired pass opens no window.
- **Dungeon reward pouches:** the normal adventure pouches (item function OpenDungeonItemBox, 31 KR items, e.g. 20300190-20300220, parameters `dungeon,index`) were refused with a log line. The client's function table (`FUN_140d24e70`, unit_140d1ae30_itemfunctiondispatch.cpp:1596) takes the room `MakeDungeonRoomId` names (dungeon * 1000 + index, dungeonroom.cpp:251) and does nothing when its table has no such room. It reads the record of the room's reward room (`GetRewardRoomID`: the union reward room when it has one, else the room), and when the counts taken this period (`GetBonusRewardCountNow`, 0 once the period is over) have reached the room's count plus the bought counts (`GetMaxCount`), it shows s_msg_dungeon_item_open_dungeon_count (the reward limit is reached). Otherwise its confirm box (s_msg_item_open_dungeon_item_box: using it uses up one dungeon reward, the items go to the mailbox when the bag is full) sends the plain item use. The server now checks the same, uses one pouch and one reward count of the reward room (the KR pouch rooms all count on union room 1006: 30 a week), sends the record (RoomDungeon update) and gives the items of the room's clear boxes, to the mailbox when the bag is full. Chosen here: the count is taken as a clear takes it (a period that is over starts anew); the daily count (UnionSubClears, which gives a real clear its daily clear bonus) stays as it is, as the client asks only the period count; the pouch gives the clear boxes only, not the clear's exp or meso. 16 of the 31 KR pouches name rooms the KR table does not have (20001001, 20009001, 21003001 and others), so the client does nothing for them and the server refuses them too.
- **Pet training kits:** the kits (item function PetTraining, 64000001-64000008, parameters `<v offsetExp="N" />`, 1,500 to 120,000 exp) were refused with a log line, so the training dialog never completed. The client's function table opens `UIItemPetTrainingDialog` (0x4f12, uiitempettrainingdialog.cpp), which reads offsetExp, takes a pet item whose pet data row has type 1 and whose level is below PetMaxLevel (`FUN_140e57ed0`: s_pet_training_dialog_invalid_item or s_pet_training_dialog_max_level otherwise) and previews the level and exp with `CExpTable::GetPetExp`. Its confirm (after s_msg_transfer_bind_pet_training for an unbound pet with a binding rule) sends the plain item use with the pet's uid as text (`SendTrainingRequest` @0x140e57d60). The server now checks the same, uses one kit, binds the pet and gives the exp as a fusion does (`PetFusionRules.Apply`: at the top level the exp goes to 0), sends ResponsePet 10 (exp), 11 on a level up, and then ResponsePet 13, the mode byte alone, which the dialog takes as its "complete" (`FUN_140e57cb0`; CPetManager has no case for 13). Chosen here: the summoned pet can be trained as well as one in the bag. The other pet item functions the server does not handle, AddPetEXP (10 KR items), and CashAttendEvent, SurvivalLevelExp and MultiRiding, are not in the client's item function table, so the client never sends their use.
- **Treasure map items:** the one TreasureMap item (30001025) was refused. The client appraises it in its own dialog, opened by two npc scripts (one paid, one free): it asks the cost (TreasureMap mode 2: item uid and the free flag), the server answers with ItemScript mode 2 (uid and cost; a negative cost reads "cannot appraise"), and the appraisal is a plain item use with the text "free" or none, answered with ItemScript mode 3 (success flag, item id, rank). The server now does this: the item's parameter names two individual drop boxes (`dropId` paid, `freeDropId` free), the use rolls every group of the box (the free box 330001824 has more than one), takes one map and gives every rolled item, and the answer shows the first one (the dialog shows one); without a box, with an empty roll or without room for every rolled item (checked before the map is taken) the map stays and the client gets the failure answer. KMS2 has no use of the item, and neither the client nor the data holds the cost (the client shows what the server sends), so the cost is 0, chosen here. The free flag is the client's and is not checked against the npc script, and the parameter's `groupId` is not used. No test on a client.
- **Guild quest rewards:** the guild quests (73000001 and on, `GuildQuestDaily`) give the guild exp, guild fund and guild coins of their completeReward (`guildExp`, `guildFund`, `guildCoin`, for example 120, 20000 and 8). The server gave only the player's exp: the guild rewards were a TODO. The coins are now given as the guild coin item (30000861, as the check-in gives them) with the other reward items. The exp and fund go to the World server, which adds them to the guild, and every member gets the new values (Guild 53) and the player the contribution notice (97). This exp counts for guild_exp (the line below). Chosen here: the fund stops at the guild level's fundMax, the ceiling the client's donation check keeps it under; a player without a guild gets the coins but adds nothing to a guild. A picked-up guild fund item (90000021) adds its amount to the guild fund the same way (it went to Reverse Coins before); without a guild it adds nothing.
- **Guild quest npc bonus:** a guild quest's reward gets the bonus of the guild's quest npc level, as the client's reward line shows it ("+x%", `CGuildXmlData::GetQuestRewardFactor`, questmasktable.cpp:915). `table/guildquestrewardfactor.xml` is ingested (the rows of enabled features, levels 0 to 299); the player's exp, the guild coins, the guild exp and the guild fund are each value * (1 + (factor - 1)) in float, for a factor of 1 or more. No quest npc, or a level the table lacks, gives no bonus. The `UserMeso` factor goes to the meso of a `relativeMeso` (the line below).
- **Guild quest meso:** every guild quest (the 40 daily `GuildQuest` and the 20 weekly `GuildWeeklyQuest` quests, the only quests with a `relativeMeso` in KR data) has `money="0"` and `relativeMeso`, so it gave no meso; `relativeMeso` was not ingested. The client's reward line (`FUN_1407622b0`, commonfiles/questmasktable.cpp:855) shows the meso of the reward contents row the value names (`table/rewardcontent.xml` rewardID 1003 or 1004: mesoTableID 2 or 3 of `rewardcontentmesotable.xml`, the player's level row, times mesoFactor; `RewardContentsTable::GetReward`, rewardcontentstable.cpp:213), with the guild npc's `userMesoFactor` bonus, instead of the fixed money. The quest metadata now carries `RelativeMeso` (the row id) and the server gives that meso (`RewardContentTable.RewardMeso`, `QuestMesoRules`); at level 1 a daily guild quest gives 1,000 and a weekly one 3,000. After either amount the client multiplies by the multicontrol `questRewardMeso` factor of the quest type when it is not 1; the server reads it into the constants (`QuestRewardMesoFactors`), and no pack of KR or NA has such a row, so it is 1. The quest's own meso rate (`GetMesoRate`) is 1 here, as the server sends every global rate as 1.
- **Guild exp condition:** the guild exp a member's own check-in, donation or other contribution adds counts for guild_exp (70300016, 74000029 "Obtain 100 guild experience").
- **Home bank condition:** a home bank call from the home dialog counts for home_bank (70400061, 74000003 "Use the safe inside your home once"); the inventory's premium bank button does not.
- **Home invite:** the inviter got the result 3 notice ("waiting for an answer") as soon as the invite was delivered. KMS2 sends the inviter nothing then (2 of 2 delivered invites); the next packet is the accepted or declined result, so the notice is no longer sent.
- **Home portals and channel change, checked against KMS2 with no change:** a channel change is answered by the move to the other channel (KMS2 also sends its reconnect context first, which stays off here, see Reconnect); a home warp, a visit with its password request and wrong password answers, and the invite accept have the layouts KMS2 sends; a home cube portal with no destination cube gets no answer, as in KMS2 (2 of 2).
- **Dungeon reward condition:** a dungeon clear that still had a reward count left (the clear reward is given) counts for dungeon_reward (70700002, 40001940); the achievement rows that name a dungeon stay uncounted.
- **Furnishing purchase condition:** the mesos paid for a furnishing bought as it is placed count for buy_cube (74000028 "Spend 5,000 mesos placing furnishings in your house"); meret purchases do not.
- **Attribute change condition:** each paid attribute change counts for item_remake_option on gear (70600029 "Attempt to change an item's attributes through Ophelia") and pet_remake_option on a pet, the two kinds the client registers apart.
- **Pet fusion condition:** each pet taken as a fusion fodder counts for pet_enchant (70600026 "Use 3 pets as fusion catalysts"; 90000795 names the fused pet). Exp books are not pets and do not count.
- **Pet fusion exp condition:** the exp a fusion's fodders give counts for pet_enchant_exp (70600033, 70700075 "Acquire 10,000 pet experience through fusion").
- **Pet taming condition:** the feeding that fills a wild pet's taming gauge counts for pet_taming (70600025, 70700073 "Pacify a hungry monster"; 90000792 names the pet).
- **Vibrate object condition:** each hit on a vibrate object counts for vibrate_object with the object's `materialTag` as the code (70400059, 74000024 "Hit a tree 20 times": code "wood"; the trees carry "wood", the rocks "stone"). The map data now keeps the tag (format 3), so the maps need a re-ingest (the `map-data` table).
- **Ranked dungeon clear condition:** a clear of a room with a rank table counts for dungeon_rank_clear with the room id as the code (93000174 to 93000182 "Clear the "Duel on the Deck" Fortress Rumble" and the other four Fortress Rumble rooms, 2409x005).
- **Item move condition:** a moved item counts for item_move only on the box the row names; the target is a box id, not a minimum, so a cube placed on a higher-numbered box no longer counts (93000061-93000063 use boxes 905, 906 and 907).
- **Dungeon rank condition:** a ranked clear counts for dungeon_rank in its room when its rank reaches the row's target (0 F to 5 S+, the client's DungeonRankRequire::GetRank on the room's rank table): 22210001 "Earn S+ Rank in the fortress rumble" (5), 22210045 "Earn A rank" (3), 93000173 "Spar with" the captain (0, any clear).
- **Design condition:** an item, furniture or mount designed from a template counts for item_design with its UGC kind as the code (70400058, 74000032 "Craft 1 template in the workshop": codes 1, 2 and 7; 90000560 "Use a template to design your own outfit" has no code).
- **Dungeon rookie helped condition:** a rookie (no veteran record of the room) who clears a room with a veteran counts for dungeon_help_beginner_helpee (70210090 "Complete a dungeon as a dungeon rookie with the aid of a dungeon veteran").
- **Monster skill hit condition:** a hit by a monster's or the map's skill counts for skill_damage_npc with the skill id as the code (70400009 "Get attacked by a seedling at the Maple Arcade" 49230005, 21233002 "Get trapped in the $npc:23000018$'s water prison" 99999981 and 36 other achievement rows).
- **Death by a monster skill condition:** the hit by a monster's or the map's skill that takes the player's last health counts for skill_die with the skill id as the code (11 achievements: 21233007 "Deadly Whirlwind" 70000500, a region skill of Bastei Peak; 21233011 "Spinning Laser" 50100394, 50100405, 50100406, skills of Devorak). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample of such an update: the client only puts skill_die in the group of skill_damage_npc. A death by a damage-over-time effect or a fall is not counted.
- **Boss assist bonus condition:** a dealer who is sent the field boss assist bonus notice counts for npc_assist_bonus with the boss as the code (20 achievements, one per field boss, 23000013 Doondun lv15 to 23000094 lv55). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample: the one assist bonus KMS2 sends in our sessions is for a boss no achievement names.
- **Auto fishing condition:** a catch made while auto fishing is on (the auto flag of the catch packet) counts for auto_fishing (23100181 "Sleepfishing", 10/100/1000, no code). Chosen here with no KMS2 sample: the client puts it in the fishing group, whose other conditions count catches; a count per auto fishing purchase is the other reading.
- **Epic chain conditions (tests only):** the KR epic chain's runtime conditions from lv10 to lv40 are checked against their own rows: the 34 trigger codes (60100010 AudienceWithEreb to 50001675 ClearPP, each sent by its map's set_achievement), item_move 30000119 into liftable target 600 on 02000084 (60100045, the box keyed by its block), emotion hello x3 on 02000076 (50001557), swimtime 20 on 52000133 (50001583), dungeon_clear 25004001 counted once (50001601), and the quest script move of 50001603 (scripts 104 and 200, function 1) to 52000136 portal 1. All pass; no server change was needed. ServerTableMapper.MergeQuestScriptFunctions is public so the test can feed it rows.
  - Chosen here: the rows are copied into the test (no data folder or database read); the item_move target test stays the server's >= on the target number, which the one chain row (600) meets.
- **Jump and fishing map conditions:** a jump is reported with its map as the code, so a jump quest that names a map (7 KR quests, the daily missions 70400081 and 70400103 among them) counts jumps on that map only; before, any jump counted. The target of fish and fish_big is the map of the catch and must be that map: all 27 KR quest targets and 5 trophy targets of the two are map ids, and they were read as a minimum, so a catch on any map with a higher id counted. No lv13-30 quest of the KR data (94 epic, 18 world, 375 daily missions; alliance missions start at lv50) has a condition type that is never reported or whose code cannot match. stay_cube is a time condition (the client's condition table groups it with playtime, stay_map and survive_cube, and the daily mission 70300068 asks for 60 on grass): each second on a cube counts one, in any state, with the map as the code and the cube's material as the target; before, it counted walked metres on grass, snow or glass only. The second is taken at the once-per-second lookup of the block under the player, which runs on the client's state syncs. The code of music_play_instrument_time is the instrument's category, not its id: the music_play_instrument_mastery trophies name the categories 1 to 30 (instrument ids go to 96) and that condition is reported with the category. Chosen here for both, no KMS2 sample.
- **Mission point condition:** a completed quest counts the mission points of its reward for mission_point (70900001 to 70900003 and 70900011 to 70900013, 100 to 900 points; the daily missions give 30 each).
- **Club join condition:** becoming a member of an established club counts for club_join (89050036 "Join a club"): on accepting an invite, and for the leader and each founding member when a staged club is established. Counting the founders is chosen here, as for guild_join: the client only registers the type.
- **Mini-game clear condition:** the reward a player-opened mini-game gives its winners (trigger MiniGameGiveReward of UserOpenMiniGameExtraReward) counts for useropen_minigame_clear with the field as the code (70200061 "Complete any Mini-game and claim your reward", fields 81000001 to 81000003). The players in the winner box of the EndMiniGame action, who still have their game record, count for minigame_clear with the field as the code (trophies 23400007, 23400008 and 23400039 name the fields 61000004, 61000006 and 61000010); KMS2 sends the 23400008 update as the player reaches the Crazy Runners goal, just before the winner buff (3 sessions). Chosen here: the report sits in EndMiniGame and not in the round end or the reward action, which the scripts of these fields run in the same step for the same box.
- **Mini-game round start:** start_mini_game_round sent MassiveEvent 0 (the round banner) for every player with a game record. KMS2 sends nothing there: the banner comes only from set_event_ui_round, once per round (Trap Master: one banner and one countdown per round, 21 s apart), and the fields whose scripts never call set_event_ui_round (61000004, 61000006, 61000010) show no banner at all. The client replays the banner on every MassiveEvent 0, so the fields with both actions showed each round twice. The action now sends nothing.
- **Event banners without a box:** set_event_ui_script sent its banner (MassiveEvent 2) only to the boxes of its box_ids; 949 of the 1,526 banner actions in the scripts name no box and went to nobody (two more have an empty box_ids, which failed the action). KMS2 sends those to every player of the field (Trap Master: the three opening banners, whose actions name no box). No box, an empty one and box 0 now mean the whole field; "!id" stays everyone outside the box.
- **Round banner offset:** set_event_ui_round passed its v_offset and arg3 in swapped order, so the fourth int of MassiveEvent 0 (which the client hands to the banner's setStage) was always arg3, 0 in every script, and the v_offset 120 of the ten round banners of 51000001 was dropped. No KMS2 packet of that field was seen; the value is the script's.
- **Negated box list:** the 51000001_dg tutorial (Spring Farm) writes user_detected with box_ids "!100" in 2 conditions, where every other arcade script ends a round with the negated condition name on the box; the box list parse threw on it, so the tutorial trigger did not load. A box list starting with "!" is now the negated test on the boxes after it, as the scripts' "!" means "not in the box" in event UI box lists (and the client's trigger reader turns a "!" before a condition name into its not flag, `TriggerConditionCli::Create`, `triggeractioncli.cpp:273`). A test parses the tutorial and every action and condition form of the 51000001-51000006 triggers.
- **Trophy count condition:** each grade a trophy reaches counts for trophy_point with the trophy id as the code (89040018 "Obtain 10 trophies", trophy 23100112; id range 20000000 to 29999999).
- **Decoration score condition:** a cube placed in a home reports the home's decoration score for interior_point to the owner (89050037 "Raise Decoration Score", trophy 23500024 "Reach 1,100 decoration score"): the counter is the score itself. The counters of this type keep the highest score instead of adding (chosen here). Not counted: meso_donation and donation_type (93000073 "Donate mesos", 93000071 "Donate gear"), since the server has no donation to an NPC.
- **Play time, shop and sitting trophies:** three counts no code reported are now reported. Play time (trophy 23100001) counts 1 for each full minute logged in, across maps; KMS2 sends one step every 60 seconds. Mesos paid in an NPC shop count for shop_buy (23200004), the price of each purchase; other currencies do not count. Time in an emotion counts for emotiontime by the emotion's name (23100016: Sit_Ground_A, Sit_Chair_A), read from the emotion skill id of the player's state record; it is reported once five or more seconds are due, as KMS2 sends 5 every 5 seconds.
- **Kill streak trophy:** each monster kill now reports the player's kill streak for killcount (trophy 21210014, grades 10, 50, 100, 500 and 1000 kills in a row), and the trophy keeps the longest streak, so it sends an update only when a streak passes it. No code reported this condition, and the chain kill counter was never called. The streak follows the client's Lua calcKillCount (the rule behind its kill count display, UIKillCount): a kill 5 s or more after the last one starts again at 1; a sooner kill adds 1 when the monster is level 50 or above or within 5 levels of the player, and leaves the streak as it is otherwise. The server's copy of that function had both of the other cases wrong (it kept the streak after 5 s and reset it on a level gap). KMS2 sends the streak's length the same way: in 13 sessions all 520 kills that kept a streak came under 5 s after the kill before (longest 4.97 s) and all 20 that broke one came later (shortest 5.98 s). Chosen here: every dealer of the kill counts it, as for the kill trophies, and the streak carries over a map change. Not done: the streak's bonus exp rate (calcKillCountBonusExpRate, 1% from 21 kills to 15% over 100); the KMS2 kills past a streak of 20 gave 12 exp each, too little to show 1%.
- **Meret, home and first clear trophies:** three trophy conditions that no code reported now count, each as KMS2 sends it. use_merat (trophy 23200041, 500 merets): every meret balance change that takes merets off adds the amount spent; in KMS2 sessions all 9 meret packets with a negative change were followed at once by this trophy's update, up by that amount (a 100 meret purchase moved it from 88412 to 88512), also when the merets came off the second balance of the packet. home_goto (trophy 23500019, account-wide, 1, 10 and 100 times): going to your own home adds 1, reported just before the move and only when the move is made (a refused move counts nothing, and neither does the guide's go-home step sent while already standing in the own home); KMS2 sends the update right before the migrate packet in all 6 of its updates, 4 after the go-home request and 2 after the server sent the player home with no request, and none of the 16 other moves to a home map (among them visits, the designer, the model home and an invite) had one. dungeon_first_clear (6 trophies, each listing 2 or 3 dungeon ids): the first clear of a listed dungeon adds 1, reported right after the dungeon record update that shows 1 clear, as in all 4 KMS2 updates. Chosen here: game merets count for use_merat like merets (KMS2 has no sample of a game meret purchase), and the guide's go-home step counts for home_goto as one of the moves without a request; every meret balance change that takes merets off counts as spent, so a negative GM currency command counts too and a fee that is later given back stays counted (KMS2 has no sample of either).
- **Trophy grade, guide quest and home decoration trophies:** four more conditions a level 13 to 30 player meets are now reported. They were picked by how often KMS2 sends updates for conditions this server never reported: hero_achieve_grade 25 updates in 21 sessions, quest_revise_achieve 16 in 5, interior_level 9 in 3 and interior_exp_offset 6 in 6; the other unreported conditions with more updates belong to level 50 content. hero_achieve_grade (trophies 21240001, 21100019, 23100290): each grade reached on a trophy adds 1 for the trophies whose code range or list holds that trophy, reported after the grade's trophy_point update; in KMS2 14 of 14 grades reached on a trophy in 21240001's range were followed by its update. quest_revise_achieve (22210017, 22300102): completing a quest on the trophy's code list adds 1, after quest_clear; every KMS2 update follows the completion of a listed quest. interior_exp_offset (23500023): a home check-in adds the decoration exp the home took from it, as in KMS2 where the counter rises by exactly the home's exp gain (450, 600, 950). interior_level (23500020 to 23500022): a home check-in reports the home's decoration level and the trophy keeps the highest, sent after the exp update; the grades are the levels 1 to 10. KMS2 trophies end at the home's level (2 at level 2, 4 at level 4), but KMS2 gets there in steps of 1: the one check-in that raised a home from level 1 to 3 moved the counter to 2 only, and on another account the third grade came 188 seconds after the second, by a step no session shows. Chosen here: report the level at once instead of in steps, so no grade is left unreachable and a home older than this change catches up at its next check-in; a new home reports level 1 when it is created, because KMS2 accounts hold the first grade before their first check-in and no session shows when it was given; a home name may not be empty, since an empty name would create the home again at level 1. Also chosen here: quest_revise_achieve does not remember which quests it counted, so on the account-wide 22210017 the same guide quest completed by a second character counts again (not seen in KMS2 either way); interior_exp_offset adds the whole check-in score when the home took any of it, including the check-in that reaches level 10 where the home keeps no exp, and the GM home exp and level commands report nothing; 23100290 counts every grade reached on its four listed trophies, so four grades of one trophy finish it (KMS2 shows updates for two of the listed trophies only, never such a case), and a grade on a trophy outside the Combat, Adventure and Life categories reports no hero_achieve_grade (not seen in KMS2).
- **Locked trophies:** a trophy with locking="1" in the achieve XML no longer counts (181 of the played KR client's 2101 achieve files have it, before the feature and locale test). The client shows such a trophy locked (UIAchieveDialog::IsAchieveLocked). The KMS2 evidence is thin: the one first clear of a dungeon listed by a locked dungeon_first_clear trophy (22200455) sent no update while the 4 for unlocked trophies did, and of 36,887 trophy updates over 340 trophies in its sessions 1 is for a locked trophy (21100009, a job change trophy). That one update does not show a count from play: it came during the login of a debug client session, with no job change before it, and carried a counter of 10 for a condition that asks for 1. So the rule is chosen here from the one withheld update and the client's display, and that one update stands against it. The ingest now keeps the attribute (AchievementMetadata.Locked), so this needs a new ingest (the achievement table gains a Locked column). With a played client pack given, that pack's flag stands alone for every trophy it has a row for (one that passes the feature and locale test), so a trophy the data pack locks and the played client shows open counts; the data pack's flag is used only for a trophy the played client has no row for.
