A KR ingest has 157 trophies locked from the data pack alone and 164 with the played KR client pack (which has 1,900 trophies), 22200455 and 22200457 among the added ones; no trophy the data pack locks is open in that client. Chosen here: a trophy with several rows is locked only when every row that passes the feature and locale test is; a locked trophy a character already has keeps its progress and is still listed. The GM command `player trophy <id>` (GM accounts only, as every command) now says so: for a locked trophy it answers that the trophy is locked and takes no progress, and for a trophy that has no row after the command that its condition was not met; both were a success without a line before. On success it prints the trophy's grade and counter.
- **Trophy claim with nothing due:** KMS2 gives no answer to a claim (Achieve 3) of a trophy whose reward grade has no reward due; the trophy was sent again (Achieve 2). A claim that gives a reward is answered as before (a title: UserEnv 0, then Achieve 2). The rest of this pass matched KMS2: the login list (Achieve 0, then Achieve 1 in parts of 60), no answer to the favourite toggle (Achieve 4), and the navigator's claim (Quest 0x18) answered with the quest's complete packet and then the items.
- **Metadata database older than the server:** the Game, World, Login and Web servers now stop at start with "Metadata database is older than this server" and the table names when the metadata database lacks a table or a column the code reads (one statement per table that names every mapped column and reads no row). Before, the server started and each query of such a table failed: with the trophy table of an ingest from before the Locked column that is the login of every character that has a trophy (its trophies are read then) and every count of a trophy condition. A new ingest writes the columns: the ingest recreates the database when the model's tables changed since it was written, and also when this same check finds a table or column missing while the model is unchanged (a column dropped by hand, a restored older dump); before, such a database kept its schema version, a plain ingest left it as it was and only `--drop-data` repaired it. Known limit: the check sees missing tables and columns only. A change in how a value is written inside an existing column (new move types in a JSON column, for example) is not seen by the server or the ingest; such a change needs a re-ingest, as its own bullet says.
- **Trophy list at login:** each row of the list now carries the trophy's current grade where a constant 1 was sent. The client sets up the trophy's condition and the goal of the grade before from that grade (`CAchieve::Init`), and KMS2 sends the current grade.
- **Trophy reward claim:** a claim gives the reward of one grade, as in KMS2, and the reward grade then moves over the finished grades whose reward needs no claim; the client gets one update. Before, one claim gave every due item and title.
- **Dismantle condition:** each item broken at the dismantle counts for item_break, by the amount broken (74000011 "Dismantle 1 item", 10002700 dismantles 10).
- **Item sockets on outfits:** the socket pages of the item socket dialog list items from inventory tabs 0 and 1 (gear and outfit) and the equipped gear and outfits (`GetItemsInCategory` 0 / 1 and `CollectShownItemsInLayer` 0 / 1, ItemSocketSystemPageSocketUnlock and the other pages), so an outfit with sockets (e.g. 11200084, skin type 2, socket row 9001) is one of their items. The server looked only in the gear tab and the equipped gear, so the unlock stage (0x88 mode 2) of an outfit got no answer and the page stayed waiting. Unlock, its stage, gem equip, unequip and upgrade now find the item in the gear or outfit tab or among the equipped gear and outfits (`ItemManager.GetSocketItem`), and the unlock takes its ingredient items from both tabs. A stage for an item the server cannot find answers s_itemsocketsystem_error_invalid_target, the message the client shows when it cannot find the item itself; any reply ends the page's wait.
- **Gemstone socket conditions:** a gem put into or taken out of a socket also counts for equip_gemstone_puton / equip_gemstone_putoff on equipment and skin_gemstone_puton / skin_gemstone_putoff on an outfit, told apart by the item's skin type as the client's socket code does (70600031 "Insert a gemstone into a socket" is equip_gemstone_puton).
- **Socket transfer (ItemSocketSystem 12):** the open, empty sockets of one outfit move to another outfit with the same socket count and none open; it was read and not answered. The rules are the client's (itemsocketsystemutil.cpp): the source (FUN_140627e80) and the target (FUN_140628050) both need a skin type, an equip subtype the socket system takes (`CItemData::FUN_14053e940`: 12-18, 20 and 22 on an outfit), sockets, no gem, no expiry unless the item has a socket row, and `CItemTransfer::CanTransferSocket`. That asks the item's socket row to open fewer sockets than it holds, or the item to have no socket row (`CItemSocketTable::FUN_14062d980`), and the transfer block's socket transfer flag (m_b42, the byte the server always sent as 1). The source also needs no gacha dismantle id. The source comes from the outfit tab or the equipped outfits, the target from the outfit tab only, as the page lists them. The cost is `GetSocketTransferCost`: 200 of client tag 0x58 (SkinCrystal) for each open socket. A bad pair answers s_itemsocketsystem_error_invalid_target and a short balance s_itemsocketsystem_error_lack_price. The client has no transfer code in its error list, and checks both itself first. The answer is mode 13 with both socket blocks and the target's transfer block. The target's flag is cleared, so it cannot pass the sockets on (chosen here: the source keeps its flag; KMS2 sends the flag as 0 on many fully socketed outfits, which the socket row already keeps from a transfer). The flag is kept in the item row; a row saved before reads it as 1. Mode 17 is the GM debug transfer and is not run.
  - The subtype the rule asks is the client's (`CItemData::FUN_14053e940` reads the client's item data), which for equipment is the two id digits after the first (GearScoreRules.SubType, as the gear score uses). The server pack gives most equipment subtype 0 or 16: the Northern Fur Earrings 11200084 have 0 there and 12 in the client, so the client offered them and the server refused the pair with invalid_target, which the client shows as "The selected target is not in your inventory.". The ingest still keeps the server pack's subtype: taking the client's for every item would also move the emotes and the fishing and music items out of the tabs the server picks from that subtype.
- **Transfer flags at item creation:** a new item now gets the client's socket transfer flag (m_b42) from its socket row, as `CItemTransfer::InitFromItem` sets it: m_b42 = `CItemSocketTable::FUN_14062d980` of the item's own socketDataId row at the item's grade, else grade 0. An outfit with its own 9001-9008 row (12220268 included) starts able to pass its sockets on. The client reads no drop row there (drop items are made by the server). Chosen here: when the sockets come from a drop row's socketDataID, the same rule is asked of that row, so an outfit dropped with rows 1002 or 1006 (sockets already open) starts unable to pass them on. Before, every item started with the flag set. A socket row's openType is ingested: with openType 1 the client opens no socket at creation (`GetInitialSocketCount` reads fixOpenCount only for openType 0), and the server now does the same, also in the transfer check. The socket system (feature 0x2e, ItemSocketSystem) and the transfer page (0xa7, ItemSocketTransfer01) are ingested from the feature table; with either off a transfer is refused, and with 0x2e off no outfit can give its sockets. KR has both on and no openType 1 row. The no-dismantle flag (m_b3F, the transfer block's byte before m_b42) is now sent, saved and read back: a drop row's disableBreak sets it, and an extraction result gets it with no socket transfer, as the client's preview shows. The dismantle follows `CItemBreakManager::CanBreakItem`: a gacha dismantle item passes; any other needs enableBreak and no m_b3F. This needs a re-ingest (openType and the two features).
- **Pet capture conditions:** a pet captured with a trap now counts for pet_catch_id with the pet's item id as the code (the Kritias fauna quests from 94000006, "capture $item:61000153$"), and for pet_catch_grade with its rarity (70600034 "Capture a rare pet", code 2). The rarity is still the server's own pick (taming rank / 25 + 1). It also counts for pet_catch_category with the pet's `petproperty` `collectGroupId` (70600035 "Capture $petCollectGroup:2$ pets"); the pet ingest now keeps that group, so this one needs a re-ingest (before it, the group is 0 and no row counts).
- **Alliance missions (daily and weekly alliance quests):** the client starts a daily alliance quest (type 9, `repeatable` 2), or a weekly one without a `groupID`, only when it is in the list of Quest 0x1F (daily) or 0x20 (weekly) (`CQuestManager::vfunc_1`, client/ms2/questmanager_2.cpp:555; the reader at :1145), and the server never sent either list, so the alliance quest window offered none of them. The server now sends both lists at login, again at the daily reset, and again when the quest a method names in `reloadOnAccept` or `reloadOnComplete` is started or completed. The lists come from `table/famepickmethod.xml`, read as `CFameTable::LoadPickMethod` (commonfiles/famegradedata.cpp:571) reads it: the `<environment>` of the server's locale, or else the one without a locale, and of it the methods whose feature is on. A method of type `RankAllianceCombination` picks each rank's `pickCountByRank` from its alliances together, an alliance at most its `maxCount`; any other type picks each rank's count for each alliance. Only quests the character can start are picked, and the missions of the list's repeat already started in its period (done or not) are picked first and use up the counts, so a level up or a new gear score within the period does not add missions past the counts. The quest ingest now keeps a quest's `alliance`, `rank` and `groupID`, and `table/famedef.xml` gives the officer NPC kind of each alliance. When a list's period ends (the day; the week from the `usePeriod` weekday), the held missions of that list without a `groupID` that are not completed are deleted (the client's `RemoveQuestsByTypeAndState` keeps the ones with a group), and the list is sent with its drop flag set so the client removes them too (`RemoveQuestsByTypeAndState`); at login such held missions are deleted before the quest list is sent. A started mission must be in the list (Quest error 3 otherwise; the GM bypass skips it). The reset runs on the session's own queue, and takes the period that starts within two hours of it as the new one, so a reset timer that fires a moment early, or an hour early after a clock change, still starts the new day. A completed quest is no longer deleted by Quest 7 (expired) or Quest 2 (abandon): Quest 7 drops only a quest in progress that has an end time (a `usePeriod` weekday or minutes, or an `eventTag`; `FUN_14074b090`), and Abandon refuses a completed quest, so the completed row keeps a mission from being started and rewarded twice in a period. Chosen here, as the client does not show how the original server picked: each list is a shuffle of all the missions of its repeat, seeded by the character, the start of the period and the method, so it stays the same through the day or week and across logins with no stored state. A data ingest is required: the Release server does not start on data ingested before this change (the fame table has no row), and the quest fields it reads (`alliance`, `rank`, `groupID`, the `usePeriod` minutes) are new; no migration.
- **Navigator and level-guide reward claims (Quest 0x18):** the reward button of the daily mission page and the level guide (`UIDailyMissionDialog::SendQuest`, client/ms2/uidailymissiondialog.cpp:321; `UILevelUpGuideDialog`, uilevelupguidedialog.cpp:306 and :380) sends Quest 0x18 for a quest the client holds as complete. The server completed it when it held the quest and its conditions were met, and otherwise sent nothing, so the button did nothing and no line was logged. A refused claim is now logged with its reason (no quest data, not held, completed, not in the played client, conditions unmet, inventory full), and the client gets the quest error (Quest 0, `ShowQuestErrorMessage`, questmanager_2.cpp:1187). A `level` condition (no codes, no party count) is now counted when the quest starts if the player is already at or above its level, as the level-up counts it; before, a navigator mission started at level 10 or above stayed at 0 until the next level-up.
  - Chosen here: the refusal answer is error 2 (`s_quest_error_consume_fail`), as the client names no claim error; an inventory-full claim gets only the inventory error Complete already sends, and a claim of a completed quest (a second click) gets no answer.
  - Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Alliance mission accept:** the alliance window's accept (NpcTalk 8 with mode 0, `UIAllianceQuestDialog` onAccept) started the quest at once, and the window kept the row on "Accept" until it was opened again: the window fills its rows only when it opens, and a quest start does not refresh it. The client's own answer to NpcTalk 8 is the NpcTalk 8 reply (`UITalkManager::OnTalkPacket` mode 8: talk type, quest id, script state, page, button, read as mode 2), which loads the quest's talk and opens the talk scene (`ReloadScript`); the talk's accept button sends Quest 2 as any quest talk does, and the window is opened again when the talk ends. The server now answers with the quest script's first start state (100 to 199) and starts the quest on that Quest 2. A quest without a start script is still started at once. Complete (mode 2) is unchanged: the quest's completion closes the window.
  - Chosen here: the start state is the script's first state from 100 to 199 whatever the held quest's state, as the window offers only missions that can start.
- **Alliance fame points:** the alliance mission window's reputation (`GetFamePoints`, the grade line and the points) never moved, because the server kept no fame points and never sent them; the window showed 0 after a mission whose reward said +700. The client fills them only from UserEnv 10 (all, a count and per alliance a short id and int points) and 11 (one alliance), `CSceneStateGame::OnUserEnv`; the unused UserEnv writer sent mode 10 with the shop's reputation numbering, which is not the client's alliance id. Completing a quest whose complete reward sets `useMainFamePoint` now adds to the quest's alliance the points the client shows as the reward: Lua `calcQuestRewardFamePoint(rank, grade, useMainFamePoint, alliance)` (alliances 1 to 5: 2000/1500/1000/800/700 for S to D, doubled with a flag of 2; otherwise 230/200/140/100/90, ten times with a flag of 2), and sends UserEnv 11 before the quest's completion; login sends UserEnv 10. The points are kept per character in the new `FamePoints` column of `character-unlock` (`AddFamePoints`), and `useMainFamePoint` is now ingested, so this needs a re-ingest and the migration; until the re-ingest no quest gives fame.
  - Chosen here: no upper limit (kept at int.MaxValue); the client's grade table is not used to cap; quests without an alliance give none.
- **Alliance fame in every locale:** completing a rank C mission of a main alliance (93000001) moved the reputation by 100 while the reward line said +800. The server's `calcQuestRewardFamePoint` gave the large table only in KR and CN, as the upstream server script does; the client's own script (`luapack`) has no locale test, so the client shows 2000/1500/1000/800/700 for alliances 1 to 5 in every region. The server now uses the client's script, so the points match the reward line in NA too.
  - Chosen here: the client's script, not the upstream server script, is followed; no re-ingest or migration is needed, and points already given are left as they are.
- **Fame log (fame board history):** the fame board's history stayed empty, because the server kept no fame log and never sent one. The client fills it only from UserEnv 12 (all: a count, then per entry an int log id and a long time in epoch seconds) and 13 (one entry, with the notice `s_fame_point_update`), `CSceneStateGame::OnUserEnv`; the board lists the entries whose `table/famelog.xml` row has the shown alliance, with the text of the log id and the date of the time (`UIFamePointBoardDialog`). In KMS2 a character's list holds no log id twice, every log is of a grade its alliance has reached (never above the alliance's current grade), and UserEnv 13 follows UserEnv 11 after 2 of 19 fame gains, each time with a log of the alliance that gained and of its grade at that time. The ingest now reads the log rows (`id`, `alliance`, `grade`) and the grade rows of `table/fame.xml` (`alliance`, `grade`, `requirePoint`) with the client's feature and locale filter into the fame table; the character keeps its logs (migration `AddFameLogs`, a json column of the character's unlock row), the list is sent at login after UserEnv 10, and a fame gain from a quest may add one log.
  - The client keys its list by the time (`uifamepointboarddialog.cpp:163`), so two logs with one time show as one line. A new log's time is the clock's or, when that is not above every held time, the highest held time + 1.
  - Chosen here: one fame gain in ten adds a log (the KMS2 share; what decides it there is not known); the log is a random one of the alliance's rows at its grade after the gain (the client's `GetGradeByPoint`) that the character does not hold yet, none when all are held; the time is the server's clock. Needs a re-ingest and the migration; characters start with an empty list.
- **Completion tickets when the client's region is not LOCALE:** using 25 tickets on a rank C mission (cost 20) got Quest error 7, the box that says the tickets could not be used. The client offers the tickets when the quest's `property` has `AvailableCompletionTicket` and its own feature `FameCompletionTicket` is on, read in the column of the client's own region (KR unless the client is started with `-locale`). The ingest also tested that feature, but with the server's `LOCALE`, and the feature is off in the NA column, so under `LOCALE=NA` no alliance quest took tickets while a KR-region client offered them. The ingest now sets the quest's ticket flag from its `property` alone; the cost stays the Lua `calcFameCompletionTicketCount` of the rank, the same in the client's script. The server now reads the region in the client's hello (`ResponseVersion`: int version, short feature level, short region; the region was read as one byte and dropped) and logs a warning at each connection whose region is not `LOCALE`, since the client then gates features, item data and texts by another region than the ingested data. Needs a re-ingest.
  - Chosen here: the feature test is left to the client, which asks it before it sends the request; a connection with another region is still accepted, with the warning only.
- **Talk choices in the played client's order:** a choice the player picks in an NPC or quest talk reaches the server as an index (NpcTalk mode 2, `CSelectableDistractorCinematicComponent::vfunc_1`, client/ms2/cinematiccomponent.cpp:721) into the choices of the client's own script content in the order it shows them, and the server answers with the goto of its choice at that index. The played client's scripts can list them in another order than the data pack: in the KR client's `script/npc/npcscript_final.xml` the Colosseum MC (11004288) script 20 lists Continue first and Start Over second, the data pack the other way round, so Continue started a new game and Start Over continued. With `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` set, the ingest reads that pack's `script/npc/npcscript*.xml` and `script/quest/questscript*.xml` as the client does (`LoadTalkScripts`, commonfiles/talkscript.cpp: one element per id among those that pass the feature and locale test, a `randomPick` script without content not kept) and drops a choice of an npc or quest `<script>` whose goto names ids of which none is a kept script of that npc or quest (`CCinematicContent::vfunc_3`/`vfunc_4`, commonfiles/scriptcontent.cpp:301), as the client does after loading; each content of a script the pack has then takes its choices in that order. The log has one line with the count of contents changed. Not set, nothing changes. Chosen here: of several available elements with one id the first is taken (the client's feature selection between them is not followed); a content the pack lacks keeps the data pack's choices. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Dungeon rooms with limited entry:** a room the played client's own room table marks `limitEnter` (`DungeonRoom::Load`; `GetLimitType` 2) can be entered only while its reward record has a count left, as the client's `DungeonRecordCli::HasCountLeft` tests it. Otherwise the room window shows its cooldown icon, and the server answers the create with RoomDungeon error 4 and the room id; the client then shows that record's expire date (`DungeonRecordManagerCli::OnError` case 4). The server's NA room table has no `limitEnter`, and its `cooldownType` is set on 101 rooms as the reward reset, so the flag is read only from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` (6 rooms in the KMS2 dev client); without it no room is limited. Chosen here: the record checked is the reward room's (`unionRewardID` when set), only the creator's record is tested for a party, a missing record has a count, and the client's extra-reward-count event (`IsExtraExhausted`) is not modelled. The record's own `CooldownTimestamp` is not what the client shows; the client's expire is `UnionCooldownTimestamp`.
- **Extra reward tickets:** a room the played client's room table gives an `extraRewardTicketTableID` (34 rooms in the KMS2 dev client, none in the NA data) can buy one more reward count with the ticket items of its coupon row (`table/dungeonrewardcoupon.xml`, read from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`). RoomDungeon 8 charges the room's `extraRewardTicketBaseCount` times the coupon's rate at the count bought so far (`DungeonRoom::GetExtraRewardTicketCost`; the last rate past the list), by item tag, and adds one to the record's bonus count (`ExtraSubClears`, the client's `GetBonusCountNow`), which raises the weekly reward count until the record's expire; the reply is the record update and the client's added-count notice. Refused with the client's texts: no ticket or a cost of 0 (error 5), the coupon's `maxExtraCount` reached (error 7), too few tickets (error 6). A room create with the use-ticket flag (the client's confirm box for a `limitEnter` room without a count left) buys one count the same way and then creates the room. The bonus count now resets with the weekly period and no longer with the daily one, since the client expires it with the record's main expire. Chosen here: the party leader pays for a ticket entry; the cost, the maximum and the count are the reward room's (`unionRewardID` when set; the client's ticket-entry box prices the entered room, and the data's union pairs have the same base count); a coupon without `maxExtraCount` stops only at 255 (the record's byte); a later row of a room or coupon id replaces an earlier one; a purchase in an expired period starts the next period; a count bought for a ticket entry stays if the room cannot be created afterwards.
- **Dungeon rank score:** a clear of a room with a rank table now fills the two scores the client's room window shows from the record (`DungeonRecord.WriteTo`): `LifetimeRecord` (the client's m_60) and `CurrentRecord` with its expiry (m_68, a timed value). From the client: the room's rank table is its `rankTableID`, else its group type (`DungeonRoomData::GetRankTableID`: normal 1, raid 2, chaos raid 3, reverse raid 4, fame challenge 5, world boss 6, others none), the score cap is that table's `maxScore` (`DungeonRankRequire`, dungeonconfig.xml), a season rank room is a chaos raid whose `limitPlayerLevel` is at least the constant `DungeonSeasonRankMinLevel` (`IsSeasonRankRoom`; 60 in KR, 99 in NA, JP, TH and TW), and the season is the first `table/dungeonseason.xml` row of the locale and features whose period holds the time (`DungeonSeason::GetCurrentSeason`; now ingested as a table, so this needs a re-ingest). Inferred, not found in the client (the server fills these values): the run score is the sum of the run's mission scores at the clear, capped at maxScore; `LifetimeRecord` keeps the best score, -1 until the first clear with a rank table; in a season rank room `CurrentRecord` keeps the best score of the season week (0 once its expiry has passed), and its expiry is the next midnight (the server's daily reset hour) of the season's `dayOfWeeks`, clamped to the season's end. `dayOfWeeks` is read with Sunday 1, the client's `CMsTime::GetDayOfWeek` convention for `limitDayOfWeeks`; every row has 6, which is Friday so read, and two of the season starts in the table fall on a Friday, but the client code that reads the field was not found, so the day is not proven. The expiry goes in the record's `CooldownTimestamp` (the long the client reads as m_68's expiry; the DB column keeps its name). The room cooldown that was written there before is no longer written: nothing on the server read it, and the client's room expire is `UnionCooldownTimestamp`. The score counts on every clear, also one without a reward count left. Not tried live.
- **Dungeon helper reward:** a veteran who clears a room with a rookie now gets the helper reward, instead of the old TODO. The checks: the room has a `dungeonHelperRequireClearCount`, and the player's record of the room is a veteran's (RoomDungeon 17) with at least that many clears, as the client's `DungeonRecordManagerCli::CanClaimHelperReward` (dungeonrecordcli.cpp:509) tests it. Another player of the clear must be a rookie, which the client's expert notice (`s_room_dungeon_record_notify_change_expert`) gives as the rule. The reward takes one count of room 1003's record while it has one left (`DungeonRoomRules.HasCountLeft`; the helper window shows that room's weekly count, `UIDungeonHelper::GetHelperRewardCountText`), and adds the result dialog's helper line (`DungeonBonusFlag.Helper`). It also gives the items of the room's `findHelperRewardDropBoxIds` and sends the record update with the client's reward notice (`GiveDungeonHelperReward`). The boxes are read from the pack named by `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` when it is set (box 330002188 on 23 rooms in the KMS2 dev client), else from the server's rows, which set none in the NA data. Without a box, the count and the dialog line are still given, with no items, and the items need the box in the server's drop tables. Takes effect with the next ingest. Chosen here: a rookie is a player whose record of the room is not a veteran's (a player with no record counts too); only the players still in the field at the clear count. The veteran state and clears are taken before this clear counts. The helper keeps the normal clear reward. With no count left there is no helper reward and no notice. Room 1003's sub count advances with the main count, and an expired period starts the next one, as a clear does. The boxes are dropped both ways (individual and global, at the room's level), as the room's unlimited boxes are.
- **Dungeon helper bonus (FindDungeonHelperBonus):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the bonus) and the event list sends it: the event's id and the bonus. The client adds up the running events' bonuses and shows the event mark on the room window's helper line while the sum is above 0 (`uidungeonroomdialog.cpp:841`); it does nothing else with it. KR has 9 rows, all ended (the last 2021-09-22), all with bonus 1.
  - Chosen here: the helper reward's boxes are given once more per bonus (`EventSaleRules.HelperRewardTimes`, at most 10 times), still for one count of room 1003's record. The client gives no rule; the KR rows' notes describe the events as one more helper coin, or twice the coins.
- **Leaving a room goes back where the player came from:** entering a field saves its `enterReturnId` as the return field only for an open field or a saveField instance, and now never in a dungeon lobby or room. A room can sit on an open map (Zakum's room 23040003 is field 02000426, the Dark Stream exit is Malevolent Manor's map): its field carried the map's own `enterReturnId`, so leaving by the Game Menu, or Exit on the result box, put the player in that map as an open field. Solo and party take the same path.
- **Relog inside a dungeon:** a login on the map of a running room the character entered (the client closed in the room, so the character was saved on the room's map) goes back into that room's lobby or field as a dungeon arrival, and gets the room clock, RoomStageDungeon and the mission list as on the first entry. It made a new open field of the map before. A room that is gone, or a logout by the menu (which saves the return field), enters the map as before. Chosen here: of two running rooms with the character's record on the same map, the newest. A channel move does not rejoin: the room lives on its own channel.
- **Party entry checks every member:** a party's room is refused when any member's enter state for it is 10 or above (level, achievement, VIP, gear score, cleared dungeon, buff, recommended weapon), as the client's `CPartyManager::CanEnterDungeonRoom` tests it. The room window shows each member's state in red, but its party button sends the request anyway, so the server checked only the leader before. The reply is FieldEntrance error with the limit and the room id (`CSceneStateGame::OnFieldEntrance` result 2), which names no member. Chosen here: the first blocking member in join order gives the limit.
- **United weekly reward once per step:** a clear gives the united weekly reward of a step (`UnitedWeeklyRewards`, clear count to reward id) only when it raised the union record's count to that step. A clear past the daily or weekly cap leaves the count where it is, and gave the reward of the count's step again at each clear. Tests with made-up ids.
- **Union clears reset by the record's own period:** the weekly reset now zeroes a record's week counts (its clears and bought extra counts) only when the record's own period is over (`UnionCooldownTime`, the day its room's `cooldownValue` names, which the client counts down to), not at the world's Thursday reset. A record still in its period keeps them, as the client still counts them. The offline resets in the database do the same: the daily one zeroes only the day's clears (bought extra counts last the record's period, as online), and the weekly one zeroes the clears and bought extra counts of the records whose period is over. Tests with made-up ids.
- **Items:**
  - The option block ends with the int the client reads (itemoptiondata_1.cpp:260).
  - The enchant history is in the client's layout (itemenchantinfo.cpp:28). Its bool is always sent true: it is not "tradeable" but a flag that, while false, makes the client refuse to destroy, trade, list or repack the item whatever its transfer flags say (itemsocketsystemutil.cpp:2316, 2440, 2468; itemrepackingscrolldata.cpp:154), and the server has no state that locks an item this way.
  - A gem writes its id as an int (itemgem.cpp:1010).
  - Enchant sub-type 15 writes the uid and the whole item.
  - The limit break preview has no meso field.
  - Adding a pet item writes no string before the item.
- **Dismantle with a full inventory:** the confirm checks for room before it breaks anything. Every reward of the still valid staged items is counted at its max, in stacks of its slotMax (mesos, medals and furnishings take no slot); when they do not fit, the client gets result 3 of ItemDismantle mode 4, its s_err_inventory notice ("Your inventory is full."), and the staged items stay. Before, the items were broken and a reward that did not fit was lost. The room check judges each stack against the inventory on its own, so a stack it lets through that does not fit is sent by mail, as dungeon and fishing rewards are.
- **Dismantle confirm:** a confirm with nothing valid left in the staging (a second confirm, or items that left the bag since they were staged) is answered with the error and gives nothing; it sent an empty result. The item_break trophy updates follow the result, as KMS2 sends them.
- **Trade with a full inventory:** when both players have confirmed, the server checks that each bag takes what it would receive, counting each item in turn as the add places it (its trade window slot when that slot is free, else the stacks it can join, else an open slot). If a bag is short, the finish is refused and nothing moves: the player whose bag is full gets `s_err_inventory`, the other player gets trade error 8 (`s_trade_error_slotcount`, "The other player's inventory is full."), both offers stay locked and either player may confirm again. The client shows trade error 8 as a notice and leaves the trade window open (`CTradeManagerEx::OnTradeRequestFailed` @0x14185c2a0; `UITradeDialogEx::FUN_141b297e0` resets only the meso input, for errors 13, 14, 17, 18 and 19); only the trade end (sub-type 6) closes it. Before this, the trade finished and what did not fit was mailed; the mail now remains only as a safety net.
- **Trade hand-over as KMS2 sends it:** when a trade finishes, the giver's window loses each offered item (Trade 9, own side); the player who receives an item gets it as an own offer (Trade 8) and its removal (Trade 9) before the bag's add; the mesos come after the items, and a player who gets no mesos gets no Meso packet; Trade 6 is last. A cancelled trade sends Trade 9 for each own item before it goes back to the bag. Before, the mesos came first and no Trade 9 was sent.
- **Trade sub-types and order:** KMS2 sent the requests 0, 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 13 (60 in all; 9 and 14 never) and the answers 0, 2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13 (98; 1 and 14 never), each of the server's writers in the size KMS2 sends. Two orders differed and follow KMS2 now: when one side changes its offer after the other locked, the unlock (Trade 12) comes before the change, and a cancelled trade gives the mesos back (Meso) before the items.
- **Trade offers racing the end of a trade:** an offer (item, removal or mesos) tested the trade's state before it took the trade's lock, so an offer racing a cancel or the last confirm could put an item or mesos into a trade that had already handed everything back, and they were lost. The state and the own lock are tested again inside the lock, and a trade that ends before it started no longer runs its time-out. Each refusal (not in a trade, locked, too many mesos, an item that cannot be traded, a count over the stack) is tested to move nothing.
- **Locked items stay in the bag:** a locked item, and one whose unlock wait is not over, is not offered in a trade and not dropped or destroyed: the client asks `CItemLockManager::CanUseItem` before it sends either (`InvenSlot::TryAddToTrade`, and the drop and destroy check `InvenSlot::vfunc_26`, invenslot.cpp), and the server took both from a changed client (the shop sale and the black market already refused them). A drop or destroy (RequestItemInventory 4 and 5) is also not taken while the player is dead, for the pet that is out and for the score that is being played, as that client check has it; these are dropped without an answer, because the client shows its own text and sends nothing. A drop with a count of 0 or below dropped the whole stack; it is ignored now (the client sends 1 to the stack's count). The last part of that client check is the gemstone test: an item with a gemstone in one of its sockets is not dropped or destroyed (the client shows its own text and sends nothing), and the server drops such a request without an answer too.
- **Drop and destroy by the client's rule:** RequestItemInventory 4 (a count) comes only for an item that may land on the field. The client asks `CItemTransfer::CanDestroy` (trade bit, no binding, not locked), the gemstone test and the enchant state (`CanDestroyItem`, itemsocketsystemutil.cpp; `InvenSlot::vfunc_26`). For any other item it shows the destroy box, and that sends 5 (`UIMessageBox::HandleDestroyItemButton`). The server dropped an item on the field only when it also had the server-side split flag, and otherwise threw the whole stack away, also for mode 4 with a count. It made no difference between 4 and 5. Now mode 4 drops the chosen count of an unbound item with the trade bit that has not expired; anything else is destroyed. Mode 5 always destroys the whole item.
- **Gems in traded and listed items:** the client offers an item in a trade, or lists it on the black market, only when every gem in its sockets may go with it (`CItemSocket` FUN_140622790 and FUN_1406227f0). Such a gem is not bound, not locked, and of transfer type 0, the only type the client's own rule gives the trade bit; empty slots and gems with no data are skipped. The black market also refuses a written custom music score (`CanListOnBlackMarket`). The server asked neither and now does: a trade offer is dropped, and a listing gets s_blackmarket_error_disable_registitem (the client's own gemstone code for it is not known).
- **Storage (RequestItemStorage):** KMS2 sent the requests 0, 1, 2, 3, 8, 12 and 15 (48 in all) and the answers in the order the server sends them (open: 11, 13, 3, 4 and the item batches 5; deposit: the bag's change, then 0; withdrawal: 1, then the bag's add; mesos in: Meso, then 3; mesos out: 3, then Meso; sort: 11, then 8). The open request (12) of a storage the server still held open got no answer, so a window opened again after the client left it without its close request (15) stayed empty; the held storage is sent again. A meso deposit that would pass the storage's limit is answered with its own text (`s_store_err_deposit_max_money`, 20) instead of the invalid amount text (19), and the limit test no longer overflows. A withdrawal the bag does not take after the item left the storage (no field, no such tab) puts the item back into the storage; before, the item was held by neither until the next login. Store items that do not move between characters: the item xml's `property moveDisable` (feature ItemStoreMoveDisable01, on in KR: 2,133 of 35,463 items) was not read. The item's last block on the wire is the character that put it into the storage (id and name; it was always empty): the client lets only that character take the item out (`s_item_err_moveDisableitem_store_out`), and offers to destroy it only when that character is no longer one of the account's (storeslot.cpp:478). A deposit of such an item now records the character, a withdrawal by another character is answered with that text, and the record is cleared when the item is back in a bag. Copies stored by two characters, or with and without a record, stay on their own stacks; a merge would have kept one record for all of them. The checks for an item bound to a character (stats, lock, equip, attribute change) read this block and so never fired; they read the item's transfer binding now. Expand and destroy, which no KMS2 session has: the expand request (6) was answered with the whole storage again; it is answered with the expansion alone (answer 13, the added slots as the open sends them), which the client adds to the open window, after the merets are paid, and is refused with `s_store_err_expand_max` once the added slots reach StoreExpandMaxSlotCount. A deposit or withdrawal of mesos above the per-request limits of the constants is answered with the invalid amount text. The destroy request (10, the item's uid) comes from the box the client offers for a stored item this character cannot take out (`StoreSlot::vfunc_26`, storeslot.cpp); it is answered with the storage's removal (1) as before, and is not taken from a dead player, as that check has it.
- **Timed items at their expiry:** the client handles an item's end by itself and reads no packet for it: from the second of the expiry on (`CItem::IsExpired`, expiry <= now) it shows the item as expired in the tooltip, refuses to equip or use it, a worn one loses its look, stats and set count at that moment (`CEquipment` tick, equipment.cpp:2950), and the bag's sort offers to remove expired items. The server keeps the item, as the client does. It counted an item as expired one second later than the client; it uses the same second now. The stats and set effects of a worn item (gear, outfit, badge) that ran out were only dropped at the next equip change or login; the totals are now rebuilt within a second of the expiry while the player is online. The summoned pet's bag takes no item once the pet's item is past its time, as the client does not ask then. No KMS2 packet of an expiry notice or expiry mail was seen, and none is sent.
- **Unequip order:** KMS2 sends ItemPutOff and then the bag's add when an item is taken off (ItemEquip 1); the server sent the add first. An item that makes room for a new one still enters the bag first (bag removal of the new item, bag add of the old one, ItemPutOff, ItemPutOn), as KMS2 does.
- **Equip bag space:** an equip is refused when the items it takes off do not fit the bag. Each item that comes off counts once and the first takes the new item's bag slot, so a two-handed weapon over a two-handed weapon needs no free slot and one over a weapon and a shield needs one. Before, a worn two-handed weapon counted once per hand slot (two free slots asked) and the refusal sent nothing. The client's put-on check (`FUN_14140dea0`, client/ms2/invenslot.cpp) does not look at the bag, so the server answers.
  - Chosen here (the client does not settle it): the refusal is the bag's own full notice, ItemInventory error `s_err_inventory`, as for any item that does not fit.
- **Black market order:** KMS2 answers a listing (3 requests) with Meso (the deposit), the item leaving the bag, then BlackMarket 2, and a purchase (6 requests) with Meso, BlackMarket 5, then the mail notice (Mail 14). The server took the item before the deposit and sent the purchase answer last, after the notice and after the seller's mail. Both follow the KMS2 order now; a deposit whose item cannot be taken is given back, and a seller mail that fails after the purchase went through is logged instead of answered with an error after the success. The price preview (BlackMarket 8) keeps the NPC sell price: the client's listing page (`BlackMarketPageItemRegister`, uiblackmarketpage.cpp:1468) shows it as the item's shop price and shows its "shop price unknown" text for 0. KMS2 sent 0 in its 3 answers, all for rarity 1 items with a list sell price of 1 meso; three cheap items do not show a rule, so the price stays.
- **Black market buy and take-back as one step each:** a buy looked the listing up, took the mesos and then wrote the new count, so two buyers on the last of a listing were both paid out of one stack, and a take-back racing a buy mailed the whole stack back while the buyer kept its part. The buy is now one transaction that lowers the row's count only while the row still holds that many at the price the buyer saw, moves or splits the stack and writes the buyer's mail; the mesos are taken after it succeeded. The take-back deletes the row first and reads the item after it, so the seller gets what is left. The world's search copies are no longer written back to the item table when the world stops (a copy from before a partial buy put the old amount back).
  - Chosen here (KMS2 does not show a lost race): the buyer that comes second gets `s_err_lack_itemcount` when the listing is gone and `s_blackmarket_error_purchase_count` when fewer are left than asked; a price changed since the look counts as the second. A take-back of a listing that is gone closes the window's request with the error it already had. A world that cannot be told of a finished buy or take-back is logged, not answered: the item and the mesos have moved.
  - Listed, not changed: the client reads two more answers the server never sends, BlackMarket 6 (stored, nothing shown) and 9 (a dialog; its trigger is not known), and no rule for a deposit kept on take-back was found, so the deposit rule stays. The black market and the meso market were not used in the KMS2 sessions looked at for this pass, so their sub-types were checked against the client's readers only.
- **Black market row with nothing left:** a buyer's first look at a listing is not under the lock, so it can fall between the two writes of another buyer's purchase of the last of it and find the row with a count of 0. That look now counts as sold out (error 23, as for a missing row) instead of going on to the amount check (error 41). Found with a two-buyer test repeated 200 times in one process: the test db has no transactions, so the row with 0 is visible there; the purchase itself was already one step.
- **Badge unequip order:** KMS2 sends BadgeEquip 1 and then the bag's add when a badge is taken off (client `OnBadgeEquip`, scenestategame.cpp:11006); the server sent the add first. Equip (bag remove, BadgeEquip 0) was already in the KMS2 order (20 requests in 2 sessions).
- **Gear score to the world, Guild 14:** the gear score is sent to the world server when it changes, so the guild, club and party lists of other players show it (it was never sent: the lists showed 0). A guild member's gear score change is sent as KMS2 sends it, Guild 14 with the member's name and the score (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413beb10` case 14, guildmanager.cpp:318, stores it in the member), instead of the whole member. The client hands the stored score to each row of the guild window's member list as `itemLevel` and adds it to the total of the overview page (`UIGuildDialog::UpdateGuildInfo`, uiguilddialog.cpp:1761); whether a row shows it is up to the window's Flash file.
- **Guild member field change (Guild 31):** KMS2 sends a member's field change alone, the member's name and the map id; the client (`CGuildManager::OnPacketMemberDataChanged` case 31, guildmanager.cpp) stores the map and updates the member's row of an open guild window (Guild 32 with the whole member data does the same; Guild 14 stores the gear score without touching the row). The world sends one notice for all that changed in a sync step, so a field change that came with an hp change was sent as Guild 32; the fields the guild does not list no longer choose the packet. The row the client hands its window carries the gear score (`itemLevel`) and the location.
- **Gear score formula:** the gear score is the client's own computation, which is also what KMS2 sends for other players (`GearScoreRules`). Per worn item (`CItem::UpdateItemLevel`): the base level from the item's gear score, its rank (the item data's `rank` when set, else the item's rarity) and its subtype's share (1 for weapons, 0.2 accessories, 0.21 cap, 0.32 clothes, 0.3 pants, 0.06 gloves and shoes, 0.62 overall), with the client's table from gear score 70; the enchant's part (a rate per enchant level, up to 0.65 below gear score 70 and only for data rank 4 or more, up to 2.0 from 70); and the limit break's part (the client script's `calcUnlimitedEnchantItemLevel`). The sum (`PC::vfunc_126`): the left hand's shield or spellbook adds nothing, one dagger or throwing star counts divided by the constant `BothHandGearScorePenaltyDiv` (4), two count by the script's `calcOHItemAdditionalLevel` (equal levels once, the mean when the lower is 70% of the higher or more, else the lower plus low * (high - low) / (2 * high)); both script parts are fitted to the script's outputs. The subtype is the two digits of the item id after the first (15100304: 51), which is the subtype the client's item data has for every gear item with one (18,177 items); the server's item rows carry another version's number (16 for that bow, the gloves' share, which gave 2,528 for the client's 42,147). A missing constant row falls back to the client's default 4 with one warning. KMS2's changes for single items match (for example 230, 170, 31, 93, 6 and 1779, one dagger 17 and two 70). Before, the server used the script package's formula with no subtype share and no data rank: 1,630 where the client showed 42,147.
- **Guild 18 and 32 on a join:** the members who were already in the guild get the join notice (18) and then the new member's data (32), as KMS2 sends them; 32 was not sent. The client redraws the member count and the member's row on 18 when the row's type byte is 3 (`CGuildManager::OnPacketMemberJoined`, guildmanager.cpp:1926), which it is. KMS2 puts a member id of its own in the row's first long; the character id is sent here (chosen here, the client only stores it). On an accepted invite the members get the invite answer (Guild 6) before 18 and 32, the KMS2 order; it came after them. The client ignores an accepted answer (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413be150`, guildmanager.cpp:1636), so nothing changes on screen, and the new member no longer gets a 6 with its own name.
- **Guild 35, a member's trophy grade:** when a guild member earns a trophy grade, every member of the guild, the earner too, gets Guild 35 as KMS2 sends it: the name, the trophy, the grade earned and a short that is 3 when that grade is the trophy's last and 2 otherwise (`CGuildManager::OnPacketMemberAchievement`, guildmanager.cpp:2303: 2 prints the "grade reached" line, anything else the "completed" line). It was never sent, and the unused writer for the completed line wrote 0.
- **Guild 27, the emblem notice:** when the emblem changes, KMS2 sends every member Guild 27 with the changer's name alone as plain text and then the emblem; the client puts the name into its own line (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413c15b0`, guildmanager.cpp:2185). We sent the finished line as the text, so the client printed its line with our line in the place of the name. The text is now the name.
- **Guild fund ceiling on a check-in:** the fund a check-in adds stops at the fund ceiling of the guild's level (`fundMax`), as a donation's and a quest's does; it was added without the ceiling. A donation reads the level's row under the guild's lock, so one that races a level change is counted against one row. The other Guild answers were compared with KMS2 by sub-type and layout and are the same.
- **Guild create, the founder's answers:** the founder gets them in KMS2's order: 77 (the guild name over the head), 1 (created), the meso, 0 (the guild), then 18 with an empty requestor and no notice and 32 for the founder's own row. The client's reader of 18 updates the row it has and redraws the guild window (`CGuildManager::OnPacketMemberJoined`, guildmanager.cpp:1926). We sent 77, 0, 1 and neither 18 nor 32. A contribution's answers were already in KMS2's order for the other members (50, 51, 53, 36; the contributor also gets 56).
- **Guild storage stays empty:** the client's storage holds only what the server puts in with Guild 110 (`CGuildManager::FUN_1413c3e50`, guildmanager.cpp:2590); it has no request that puts an item in, and KMS2 was not seen to send 110, a gift result (109) or a log (112). With no known source for the storage items the storage is not built: 109 stays the refusal and 112 the empty list described below.
- **Lock items for option change:** the item that locks options is chosen the way the client counts it (GetLockItemId and GetLockItemLevel): the lock tag of the item's kind (armor, weapon, accessory, then pet), and for an equip the equip's rarity (OptionBlock02 is on); a pet takes a pet lock item of any rarity. Before, the server kept its own lists of item ids, which missed one accessory lock item and four pet lock items that the client offers. The first match in the misc tab is taken, of the items the character can use (next bullet). Pet lock is off in the played client (no PetRemakeBlock01), so the pet case only applies where that feature is on. The lock tags come from the played client's item data when the ingest is given one (MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML): the client matches its own tag, and the server pack tags some scrolls for another kind (30061170 is an armor lock in the played client and an accessory lock in the NA pack, so the server took it for accessories and refused it for armor). Chosen here: only the four LockItemOption tags are taken over; an item whose available environments disagree on the tag keeps the server pack's. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Pet attribute change fee:** the pet page pays the crystal fragments of a change with the ones the player owns and 1,000 meso for each one lacking (PetRemakeOptionBoard::FillIngredientList; KMS2 sends the meso update, then the bag updates); only the other two materials must be owned. The server refused the change when fragments lacked. It now takes the owned fragments and the meso (meso first, nothing unless all can be paid). A gear change is paid the same way (UIItemRemakeOptionDialog::CanPayRemakePrice turns lacking fragments into the same meso fee). The fragment count adds the client's 1e-5 before the cast (CItemOptionTable::CalcPetRemakeCost); the client also lowers it by a running pet crystal sale, which the server does not apply.
- **Attribute change refusals:** every refusal answers with the error packet of the dialog that asked, so its notice shows. The item and pet dialogs (OnRemakeError) read a byte and an int code and have strings for 1 (not in the bag), 2 (cannot be changed), 3/4 (wrong item), 5 (materials lacking), 6/7 (change failed), 9 (cannot lock) and 10; the scroll dialog reads the same shape on its own packet with codes 1-8, 10-17. Before, a scroll change whose roll failed answered on the item dialog's packet, which the scroll dialog does not read, so nothing showed; it now sends the scroll dialog's code 12 (failed to modify). On the item dialog a lock without a lock item sent the unnamed code 255 and now sends 9, and a failed roll sends 6 instead of 255. The scroll error code is written as an int explicitly. The client itself refuses, without asking the server, an item it cannot find, an item CanRemakeOption rejects (rarity under 4, kind, level, the remake-disable property, no options), a price it cannot pay, and a lock with one option or without a lock item. KMS2 sessions hold no refused change, so the codes are the client's. Not checked by the server: the item's remake-disable property (not ingested)
- **Lock items the client counts:** with lock scrolls 30061170 and 30000889 (both rarity 4) in the bag, an option change of a rarity-4 accessory took 30061170, which the client does not count for it. The client counts a lock item by its own item data's tag, the item's rarity and `PC::CanUseItem` (`Unk_Inventory::FUN_140a5f7b0`, inventory.cpp:835). In the played client's item data 30061170 is an armor lock (`LockItemOptionArmor`), in the NA pack an accessory lock; the ingest takes the played client's tag only when `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML` is set, so the server that took it ran without it. Setting it and running the ingest again makes the server take 30000889 for accessories. The server now also asks what `CanUseItem` asks of a lock item: not expired (as before), a premium-club item only with an active premium club, not bound to another character.
  - Chosen here: no hand list of scroll tags is kept in the code; the played client's pack stays the only source of its tags, as for the other played-client tables.
- **Outfit dismantle reward:** an outfit with no entry in the break ingredient table now gives what the script's calcMeratSkinItemBreakReward gives, the rule the client also uses to list it as dismantlable. Skin types 1, 2 and 5 give lucky bag coupons (40100027), types 3 and 4 the free ones (40100032), and the count grows with the grade. Type 99 and outfits with no skin type give nothing, and the client does not let those be staged. In the NA branch the script gives nothing for any outfit. Before, these outfits gave the same onyx as other gear. Each opened socket on such an outfit also gives back calcMeratSkinItemBreakRefundPercent of its unlock ingredient (75% of 690 SkinCrystal, 517 per socket, at least 1), as the client adds it to the reward list. The SkinCrystal tag pays out as item 40100027, from the tag rows of the client's table/itemdata.xml, so it adds to the lucky bag coupons of skin types 1, 2 and 5. The NA and JP branches refund 0%.
- **Gear dismantle reward:** gear with no entry in the break ingredient table gave 700 to 1,000 onyx crystals times its rarity. KMS2 lists far less, by the item's sell price: onyx crystals (40100023) = the sell price at rarity 3 divided by 600, 280, 50 or 37.5 for rarity 1 to 4, rounded down, at least 1, the same number as min and max; a one-handed weapon (slot name OH) gives half. The chaos onyx row (40100024) is always listed, with 0 to 0 below rarity 4 (the result lists it with 0 too), 3 at rarity 4 and 1 to 2 for a one-handed weapon (`DismantleRules.Onyx`, `ChaosOnyx`). The amount paid now reaches the listed max (it was rolled below it). Not known, left as before or as rarity 4: rarity 5 and above, the part of the enchant level, and items KMS2 sessions show with other rewards (a pet gave a pet item and 5 onyx).
- **Ability window:** learn (mode 2) and reset (mode 1) are handled, and so is the GM learn-all request (mode 3, sent by the client's `LearnAllAbility` chat command) for GM accounts only (see GM-only packets below). Chosen here: learn-all learns every table ability not learned yet without testing level, points or the open quest, sends the load packet with the whole list and counts no trophy. A learn counts the `character_ability_learn` trophies with the ability's category as target (trophies 23100216-23100220), and a reset counts `character_ability_reset` (23100221). Chosen here: the counts add up, so a relearn after a reset counts again. The abilities are the client's `table/characterability.xml`, a new metadata table: it needs a re-ingest, and the Release server stops at start without it. The learned ids are kept in the character's Experience JSON and the reset time in its Cooldown JSON (no migration). Bonus points are sent as 0: the client data gives them no source. A reset goes in KMS2's order: the meso, the load packet with an empty list and the new reset time, then the reset trophy. The client's mode 1 reader is empty and KMS2 never sends it, so the server no longer does either.
- **Skill tree (Job 9 save, Job 11 auto distribute):** both send the whole tree (id, level, learned per skill) after the client's own check (`UISkillBookTreeDialog::RequestJob`, `SkillPointSimulator::Validate`). The server now repeats that check on the tree it would store and takes it whole or not at all: per rank, the points spent (a base skill's levels above 1, any other learned skill's level) are within the points from the level (`JobSystem::GetLevelSkillPoint`: none below 10, the base rank one a level from 10 up to `SkillPointLimitLevel1`, the awakening rank one a level from 60 up to `SkillPointLimitLevel2`) plus the extra points of that rank; each learned skill is within its max level; and its level's `upgrade` element is met: the character level, the listed skills learned at their levels in the sent tree, and the listed quests completed. A refused tree is not stored, and the answer (9 or 11) carries the stored one: the client's Job error mode has only job-advance texts, and it re-reads the whole tree from 9 and 11. Before, both took any level up to a skill's max. Not settled from the client, the server's own reading: the client marks a quest as needed when it is rewarded, the server takes a completed quest (the data lists several quest ids per level, the client's code one); the `money` of an upgrade element is not charged, as the client's check has none; skills the client gets outside the tree (its extra flag) have no server counterpart; the bonus value after the extra points is sent as 0, so it adds nothing. The upgrade elements are a new part of the skill metadata and need a re-ingest; until then no requirement is checked, only the points and the max level. Constants ingested before the two limits were added read as 0, and then the points are not checked either.
- **Job confirm (Job 2):** the confirm box of a job script sends Job 2 for every job condition row, and only 8 rows of the KR data name a job to change to (the others are doctors and movers). A row without one is now ignored; it threw. A row with one is checked again against the character (the row's job and level; Job error s_err_job_bad_job or s_err_job_not_enough_level), as the box can be confirmed by a character the script would not offer it to. Chosen here: the two error codes, no KMS2 sample.
- **Function cube:** the command numbers are the client's, and a gathering success writes the count 1 the client adds to its tally.
- **Requests read without a reply yet** (each logs what it read; the reply needs state the server does not keep):
  - Item merge choice, revert and material select; manufacture through the enchant scroll; exchange scroll target and fill.
  - Change attributes: keep current (drops the pending result), stage item, open dialog.
  - Revival: coupon revive and summon revive.
  - Meret Market: UGC sale details, the by-id lists (at most 20 ids) and the random sale requests.
  - System shop: every shop kind; closing one clears the open shop.
- **Pets:** a summoned pet comes back after a field change and after a channel or game server change (the pet item's uid rides in the migration request, so Game and World must be of the same build). A pet is not summoned in a map whose `usePet` is 0 or by a character below the pet item's level, as the client refuses; in a no-pet map the pet is kept for the next map that allows one.
- **Pet bag:** an item skill cast with the uid of an item in the summoned pet's bag uses that item up (the stack shrinks, the last piece leaves the bag). The client looks for a potion in the pet's bag before the player's (`CPetManager::UseItemById`, `CPetInventory::UseItemSkill`) and casts with the uid it found; the server looked only in the player's bag and refused the cast. Taking an item out of the pet's bag is refused with s_err_inventory when the player's bag has no room for all of it; before, the item left the pet's bag and was lost.
- **Pet bag load:** KMS2 answers the bag request of a summoned pet (3 answers) with PetInventory 6, then PetInventory 4 only when the bag holds items. The client clears its slots on 6 and only adds on 4 (`CSceneStateGame::OnPetInventory`, scenestategame.cpp:2615), so the list alone, as the server sent it, left the items of the pet shown before in the window. The server sends the reset, then the list for a bag with items. Putting in, taking out and moving (10 requests) already equal KMS2.
- **Lapenshard upgrade:** KMS2 (1 upgrade, a bag lapenshard fed with pieces of its own stack) takes the fodder first, then the meso, the ingredients and the lapenshard itself, adds the new item and answers Lapenshard 5 with uid 0. The server took the ingredients first and the meso third, and answered with the request's uid, the uid of an item that is gone. It follows the KMS2 order and sends uid 0; ingredients are checked before anything is taken. The client's upgrade dialog is not in the source tree, so no client line for the uid. Equip, unequip and the preview answer (15 requests) already equal KMS2.
- **Closet:** KMS2 ends every closet equip (4 of 4, also when every item of the tab is already worn) with a Notice of flags 5 (chat line and alert) carrying the closet's success string, which no client code names (the server picks it). The server sent no notice, and a "do not have" box for each item of the tab that was already worn. It skips worn items and sends the notice; for an item that is neither worn nor in the bag it sends the closet's "item not exist" string instead, which is taken from the client's string table and not seen in KMS2. A cleared tab (2 KMS2 answers) is sent with type 0 whatever type it was made with; the server kept the type. Create, key and rename already equal KMS2. Equip order: per item KMS2 sends ItemPutOn before the bag's remove (17 items in 2 requests), where a normal equip sends the remove first; the closet equip now follows that for an item that takes no worn item's place, the only case KMS2 shows. An item that displaces a worn one keeps the normal order, because the displaced item goes into the bag slot the remove frees.
- **Limit break fee:** the newest KMS2 build with a limit break preview (1 request) sends five ingredients and no meso: the four catalysts and crystal fragments (client tag 0x60), a thousandth of the meso fee (6228 for 6,228,500), and the client's fee text (uiunlimitedenchantdialog.cpp:263) is 1000 meso for each fragment the player lacks. The server sent four ingredients, so the client showed a fee of 0 while the server took the whole meso fee. It now sends the fragments, takes the fragments the player owns and 1000 meso for each one lacking; that charge is read from the client's text, since no upgrade was seen on that build. On the older build (6 upgrades) KMS2 takes the meso before the materials; the server took the materials first and now checks everything before it takes anything. The onyx amounts at limit break 40-49 are KMS2's 258 and 16770 (the server asked 16777).
- **Meso market window:** KMS2 answers the window's load (2 requests) with the limits, the counts, the character's black market listings (BlackMarket 1) and the own list. The server did not send the black market listings, and its limits differed: KMS2 sends tax 0.05, range 0.2, 5 listings, 5 a day, 10 purchases a month, 2 days, page 100, price 50 to 3000 (the server: tax 0.1, 30 a month, page 50, price 100 to 1000). The average price in KMS2 is the live one (116 and 136); the server keeps its fixed 200. The client reads the limits in CMesoMarketMan::OnMesoMarketLimits (mesomarketproductcli.cpp:352).
- **Meso market register, buy and average:** the register request carries the meso amount and the total price (client `MesoMarketProductCli`), and the amount is one of the window's amounts (`MesoMarketProductUnit0` to `9` times `MesoMarketBasePrice`). The server took only the base amount, compared the total price with the price per base amount, and took the base amount of meso whatever was listed. Now the listed amount is taken, and the checks run in the client's order with its codes: amount not offered 15, not enough meso 5, own listings full 8, daily limit 13, price per base amount outside the range 16. The range is the client's: the average times (1.0001 minus or plus the range rate), cut to whole numbers and kept within the minimum and maximum price. A buy of a listing that is gone is answered with the buy result and code 17, on which the client drops the row; it was the error packet, which left the row. The average price sent with the limits is the mean price per base amount of the last 100 sales (200 with no sales), kept within the price bounds; it was always 200. KMS2 only shows the window's load, so these rest on the client; the average's rule is ours, and the seller's payment by mail (price less the 5% fee) is unchanged.
- **Meso market, later fixes:** a register refused for its price (code 16) is now preceded by the limits with the average of now, since the client tests and names the range from the average it got when the window opened. The average is the mean of the last 100 sales' prices per base price, each sale counting once whatever its amount, and stays 200 until 10 are sold (own rule): one sale moves it by 2% at most, where before the first sales, or one large one, moved it by up to 20%. A buy of a listing past its end answers code 17 like a sold one, on which the client drops the row (it kept it on code 12); the listing stays for its owner to take back. A sale is one save, the listing's move to the sold table with both mails, so a failed mail no longer leaves a paid sale without the meso or the price. The search's end-time filter was checked and is right: the end is stored in the host's local time and compared with it.
- **Meso market races:** a sale deletes the listing's row first and writes the sold row and both mails after it, in one transaction, so of two buyers on one listing, or a buyer and the owner taking it back, the one that comes second has written nothing: it gets the sold-out answer (code 17) and keeps its tokens, or the take-back is refused and no mesos come back. Each refusal of a register, a buy and a take-back is tested to leave the mesos, the tokens, the listing and the mails as they were.
- **Pet exp from hunting:** when the owner gets a monster's kill exp, the summoned pet (a pet row with a type other than 0, below the top pet level) gets exp too and the owner is sent ResponsePet 10, then 11 on a level-up. In KMS2 a level 1 pet gets level * 12 / 100 (rounded down) from a class 1 monster of level 23 to 50 and level * 48 / 100 from a class 3 one (levels 40 and 42); the rule is not in the client. Chosen here: classes below 3 count as class 1, above 3 as class 3, and the pet's level does not change the amount.
- **Pet level in the item tooltip:** the item's pet block carries the level (name, exp, evolve points, level, has-items; the client stores it with `SetPetLevel`, item.cpp), so nothing is missing in the packet. The tooltip (`ASTooltip`, astooltip.cpp) adds its level row (s_word_petlevel_sizeup) only for a pet whose row in table/petproperty.xml has type 1 and whose item has no main description text: an item with that text shows the text instead, and a pet of type 0 (a row without the attribute) shows no level at all. The level also scales the constant stat rows and fills the exp gauge. Read from the client source; not compared on a client per pet.
- **Pet auto flag at login:** KMS2 sends ResponsePet 15 with one bool between the in-game rank and the field enter request of every login; the server sends it there with 1, the value the client starts with (`CPetManager`), since the flag is not stored here.
- **Pet summon answer:** KMS2 answers a summon with the pet's field entry first, then the pet item's binding (first summon), the owner's pet state, the collection entry when the summon made one, and a notice with s_pet_summon_on; the client prints the dismissal line itself (CPetManager::OnPetUnsummoned) but has no summon line of its own. The server sent the binding and the collection entry before the field entry and no notice, so calling a pet printed nothing; it now sends KMS2's order and the notice (not on a field change). Pet fusion and evolve points take the meso before the fodders, as KMS2 sends.
- **Pet warp sound:** KMS2 sends the owner ResponsePet 2 (72 times in the sessions) at the moment the summoned pet is put next to them after falling behind, with the pet's NpcControl entry at the new position; the client plays the pet summon sound for its own pet (`CPetManager::OnPetSummonSound`). The server moved the pet without it; it now sends it to the owner with every such warp.
- **Summoned pet level:** the level in a summoned pet's field entry (`CSceneStateGame::OnFieldAddPet`, the npc level the client keeps for the pet) is the pet item's level in KMS2 (the same pet enters with 3 and later with 29). The server sent the level of the pet's npc row; it now sends the pet's own level for a summoned pet and the npc row's level for a wild one. The client's name tag for npcs and pets shows the name and title only (`UINameTag::Entity`; "lv" is in its debug tag alone), so a level on the tag is not expected; a level-up writes the pet item's own Level, the field read here.
- **Item binding packets (ItemInventory 14 and BindItem 0x103):** when an item the client already holds binds to the character, the server sent ItemInventory mode 14 wherever the item was, and the client applies mode 14 only to an item in the bag (client/ms2/scenestategame.cpp:2960), so it did nothing for an item that had left the bag (an equip binds the item after taking it out of the bag). Now an item in the bag still gets mode 14 (it also refreshes the bag), and an item that is not in the bag gets BindItem: the uid and the item stream, which the client reads over the item it finds in the bag or, failing that, among the equipped items (`CSceneStateGame::OnBindItem` @0x14173b420, client/ms2/scenestategame.cpp:8542). A pet with transfer type 7 now binds when it is first summoned, and that bind goes out as BindItem although the pet item is in the bag: KMS2 sends BindItem for the pet item right after the pet's field entry and before the owner's pet state (3 summons of unbound pets, items 61120070 and 61100136), and the stream differs from the bag's only by the binding (character id and name).
- **A bound item shows no trade count:** once a BindOnEquip item is bound, the tooltip reads "Untradable, Unsellable" and "Bound to {name}" with no trades-left line, as the client builds it (astooltip.cpp, from SetItemSellPrice): `CItemTransfer` 0x140635590 answers 0 for a bound item, and the count line shows only for a tradable item with a count left. The item row keeps its RemainTrades (1 for the Cat Mask 11050048), which nothing reads while the item is bound. Not changed here.
- **Item transfer as KMS2 sends it:** checked against 70,168 decoded item records of the KMS2 sessions (builds 2502-2549). A black-market-only item (transfer type 6) is always 4 (trade) on the wire, whatever its rarity; the server sent 0 for one at or past its trade rarity (TradeMaxRarity), so the black market refused 20 ids that KMS2 lists (1,306 records). Binding no longer sets the trades left to 0: KMS2 keeps them, and about 400 bound bind-on-equip, bind-on-use and pet records still carry 1. They do nothing while the item is bound, because every client check tests the binding first. The other mismatches are data that changed between builds (18 bind-on-loot ids sent unbound as 0, and a few tradable and untradeable ids in builds 2506-2522). The ingest is unchanged. Repackaging (ItemRepack) already follows `CItemRePackingScrollTable::Check` code for code, and `CItemTransfer::IncrementTransferCount`: one trade and one repackage are added, and types 3 and 5 are the only ones repackaged, which the client's own rule always gives the limited-trade bit.
- **Black-market-only items hand to hand:** the 4 on the wire is what the client reads; its trade-slot check (`CItemTransfer` 0x140635590) and its drop check (`CanDestroy`) test only the rule bits and the binding, never the item's transfer type, so the client offers such an item in a trade and sends mode 4 to drop it. The server decides from the item data instead: a black-market-only item goes in a trade or lands on the field only below its trade rarity (TradeMaxRarity) or with trades left, as before the wire change; otherwise the trade add is ignored and a mode-4 drop is refused: the item stays in the bag and the client is answered with ItemInventory error 42, which it shows as `s_item_err_drop` (`CSceneStateGame::OnItemInventory`). Only the destroy box (mode 5) destroys it. The black market still lists it.
  - Chosen here: BindItem for the item that is not in the bag. KMS2 packets show no bind after an equip, with either packet.
  - Not sent here: KMS2 also sends BindItem for an item that is created bound, before the packet that adds it (514 of its 517 BindItem packets). The client does not hold that uid yet, so its handler returns without reading; the add that follows carries the binding, as it does here.
- **NPC AI summon:** an AI summon node spawns npcs. Its npcId is a 1-based index into the summoner's aiInfo `summonNpcIDs`: the nodes use 1 to 48 and never 0, and in the 473 npc rows whose AI file was found the highest npcId is never past the list. The list is read from the npc xml at ingest (the file parser does not read it), so the npc table needs a re-ingest; rows ingested before summon nothing. A run spawns `npcCount`, while at most `npcCountMax` of the summoner's summons of the node's `group` are alive. A summon stands at `summonPos` (a map position) or at the summoner plus `summonPosOffset` (no node gives both), scattered on the ground within `summonRadius` X and Y, and snapped to the navmesh. It faces `summonRot`; one next to the summoner without a rotation faces as the summoner does. `delayTick` (milliseconds) delays the spawn, and `lifeTime` (under 1000 seconds, from 1000 milliseconds; see AI summon lifeTime below) removes the summon. A summon of a node with `master="None"` stays when the summoner dies or leaves; every other summon is removed with it. Server choices, as no data settles them: the offset is not turned with the summoner's facing, and the Z of `summonRadius` is not used. Not built: `option` (masterHP, hitDamage, linkHP; what they link is not known), the difference between no `master` and `master="Slave"`, and `summonTargetOffset` (2 nodes, both 0).
- **Boss AI files in the _Normal folder:** some boss npc rows name an AI file in `BossDungeon/<boss>/` that the server data ships only in the copy folder `BossDungeon/<boss>_Normal/` (each file both folders have is the same in both). Such a missing name now falls back to the same path in the _Normal folder, with a warning in the log; before, the npc ran the default AI. This fixes 8 npcs: 23000114 (the boss of room 23021003, `SnowKing/AI_SnowKing_Chaos.xml`), 23200067 (`Madria/AI_BigWingChimera_Chaos.xml`) and 21500830-21500839 (six `Pinkbean/Chaos/AI_StatueHuman0*.xml`). The SnowKing file used is the one npc 23502011 runs; its Battle, Reserved and BattleEnd are those of the normal boss's AI_SnowKing.xml, and only one preset's buff id differs (50005008 for 50170031, two additional effects with the same values). The other npc rows with a missing AI file (319 name a file found in no folder) keep the default AI. Live try: not run (tests only).
- **Boss AI file named with NA:** npc 23200077 names `BossDungeon/Papulatus_Chaos/AI_Papulatus_ChaosNA.xml`, which the server data does not ship. A missing name ending in `NA.xml` now falls back to the name without `NA` when that file exists, with a warning in the log; before, the npc ran the default AI. With the npc's own skill list `AI_Papulatus_Chaos.xml` holds every skill and level KMS2 sends for this npc (13 skills), each inside the file's health bounds; the folder's other file, `AI_Papulatus_ChaosNormalJP.xml`, does not fit (one skill missing). Live try: not run (tests only).
- **NPC AI slaves:** a summoner's slaves are its live summons that leave with it (every summon but `master="None"`). `RemoveSlaves` (19 nodes in 13 files; the files' comments: remove every monster this npc summoned as its slaves, even mid-action) removes them. Its one `isKeepBattle="true"` node (a boss clearing its slaves just before a skill break) also keeps the npc in battle, as `isKeepBattle` does on the skill and standby nodes. The slave count condition comes in two forms, which the file parser reads into one class: `SlaveCount count="N"` (26 conditions, 3 with `useSummonGroup="1" summonGroup="201"`, which counts that group only) passes when exactly N slaves live.
