The client uses the flag only to draw the button and reads nothing back for it. A summoned pet here follows its owner and does not fight, so the flag has nothing to switch; it is read and dropped. The server's answer 15 (it sets the client's flag) is sent only at login.
- Quest 15 (sky fortress mission attack; the body is a field id): the client sends it from the world map only while an alliance quest of that field is in progress and the day's attack count is not used up. What the attack does on the server (the client counts the attacks itself and reads no direct answer) is not known. Read and logged.
- Quest 17 (epic quest restart; no body): the game menu sends it only while an epic quest restart game event runs, the character has not restarted yet and stands in a field the event allows. The server's event table has no such event, so the client shows its "event ended" text and sends nothing. Which quests a restart resets is not known from the client. Logged if it arrives.
- Quest 33 (complete an alliance quest with completion tickets; the body is the quest id) is handled. The client sends it from the alliance quest window for a quest in progress whose basic `property` has `AvailableCompletionTicket` while the feature `FameCompletionTicket` is on (`UIAllianceQuestDialog::CanUseCompletionTicket`, unit_140bf60f0_uialliancequestdialog.cpp:597). The quest is completed without its conditions and with its rewards, and the server takes as many items of tag `FameCompletionTicket` as the Lua `calcFameCompletionTicketCount` gives for the quest's rank (S 90, A 60, B 50, C 20, D 10, other 0; the alliance argument is not used). The tickets are taken only when the completion went through. A quest without the property, a cost of 0, too few tickets or a completion refused (a full bag) get Quest error 7, the client's text for a ticket that could not be used; the client reads no other answer. The property is a new part of the quest table, so it reads false until the data ingest of this build is run.
- Quest 34 (accept all alliance quests) and Quest 35 (complete all) are handled. Neither has a body: the alliance is the talk NPC's, whose kind is the alliance's `officerNpcKind` in `table/famedef.xml` (`CFameTable::GetAllianceByNpc`, commonfiles/famegradedata.cpp:830). 34 starts each mission of that alliance in the lists sent (Alliance missions, under Fixes) that can be started, then Quest 0x22 names the alliance (the client shows `s_quest_alliance_accept_all_<alliance>`); 35 completes each held alliance quest of that alliance, daily or weekly, whose conditions are done, then Quest 0x23. With none started or completed, or no alliance for the talk NPC, the answer is Quest error 3 or 2. The client's accept-all also takes a weekly quest with a `groupID` but no `unrequireGroup` (`CanAcceptAllQuests`, unit_140bf60f0_uialliancequestdialog.cpp:612); the server does not start those (8 quests in the data). Quest 19 (re-enter a quest's hidden field) is handled: a started quest whose data has `useReEntryHiddenMap` moves the player to its first progress map. The flag is a new part of the quest table, so it reads false until the data ingest of this build is run. Quest 28 (find a player for a quest) is handled: the found player is tested against the quest's `user_find` condition (job, level, gender, title, ride, shown equipment) and the condition goes up. The effect named in the condition's code (`effectId`, `effectLevel`) is not given: the client does not show who gets it.
- RequestItemEnchant 9 (refund of enchant experience): sent from a message box that the client opens only for an item whose enchant record has experience and a version byte other than 1. The server writes that byte and always writes 1, so the box never opens. Which materials the refund gives back is not shown by the client, so the request is read and logged.
- RequestItemEnchant 13 and 14 (enchant simulation): sent by a simulation window that nothing in the client opens, and the client reads no answer under either number. They need a GM account and are then only logged.
- RequestItemEnchant: The GM debug buttons 15 to 18 are handled for a GM account. 15 (force enchant): the enchant of the item in the window succeeds at once and nothing is consumed. 16 (fill ingredients): the materials of that enchant are put in the bag; the first button tops the bag up to what the enchant needs, the second gives 10 of each (the server's reading of the two button names). 17 and 18: the item's stored fail charges are filled to a full charge rate or emptied. 19 has a sender in the client that nothing calls, so it is not counted; it is logged for a GM account.
- Party 32 to 34 (dungeon match): see the matchmaker under Fixes; the match-found box has no confirm wait or decline, and the World side is tested only through the queue (it needs channel clients).
- Divorce is handled. It is driven by the npc scripts of the divorce lawyers (script conditions weddingState, marriageDate and coolingOff; script function attribute divorce) and three wedding sub-types. Filing alone (function individual): married for more than 30 days and holding the fee of 1,000,000 meso, which is taken; the marriage goes to state 3 or 4 (filed by the first or the second partner of the wedding packet) for a cooling off of 7 days from the filing time, which the wedding packet carries as its first time. The other partner can agree (individualAgree): the couple is divorced (state 5) and the filer gets the fee back with a mail. The filer can take it back (individualcancel) until the cooling off ends: married again, no fee back. When the cooling off ends the couple is divorced without a request. A mutual divorce (function mutual) asks the partner, who must be in the same field, with sub-type 8; the answer is sub-type 9 (13 yes, 14 no) and yes divorces the couple at once, without fee. Sub-type 7, from the wedding window of the partner who did not file, moves that partner to the divorce field. State 5 lasts the propose cooltime of 2 days, then the marriage row is deleted. A proposal is refused while either side is in a divorce or in the cooltime. Each step sends the mails of the mail table (40000001 to 40000005, 40000007, 40000011). The ends without a request are checked when a partner enters a field, talks to such an npc or is part of a proposal, not by a timer. Needs the migration AddMarriageDivorceTime. Chosen here, not read from the client: the fee comes back as meso attached to the mail; the date in the filing mails is written by the server as yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm; a mutual ask to a partner who is not in the field is answered with the same-field text; a refused mutual ask shows the asker nothing (the client has no text for it). Each change of the stored state (file, agree, take back, end of the cooling off, mutual) is one update that tests the state it starts from, so two requests at the same time store one change, take one fee and send the mails once. The fee is saved in the transaction of the filing. The Wedding 7 move is refused when dead and from a dungeon or wedding hall room. A mutual ask waits 30 s (the server's own time), one at a time, and its answer counts only while the asker is still in the field.
- Wedding 37 (the item of a wedding skill) is handled: the client sends the id of a skill of type item (table/weddingskill.xml) whose item it does not hold; the server gives the row's item (itemID, grade, count) when the couple is married (state 2) and the wedding grade reaches requireGrade. The grade is the client's WeddingTable::GetGrade over table/weddingexp.xml (the grade before the first row above the marriage exp, else the top grade). Server choices, not from the client: only state 2, no once-only record (the client only checks that the item is not held), a refusal sends nothing (no result text fits) and a full bag gets the inventory alert. Both tables are read at ingest, so a re-ingest is needed.
- Wedding 34 (the couple message) is handled: the message is saved and shown to both partners.
- Wedding 0x0F (reservation done) after a coupon reservation: the chat line names the hall's meret price although the coupon paid and no merets were taken. The client builds that line itself (`WeddingManager::OnReserveOk`, weddingmanager.cpp:856): 0x0F carries only the ticket, and the price is the hall's `merat` from the client's own wedding table. Only the change notice 0x16 carries the cost in the packet (`OnMode16`, :890), which is why a change of the time alone says 0. The server cannot change the 0x0F line.
- BonusGame 3 (close) is handled as nothing to do: `UIRouletteDialog` sends it when the last spin has shown its result. The game ended at the spin (sub-type 2 clears the game id), and the client then ends the talk itself with NpcTalk 0.
- KeyTable 7 (leave the skill set mode, `CKeyTable::SendKeyTableMode7`) is handled as nothing to do: the client sends its keys with KeyTable 2 just before, which the server stores. KeyTable 6 has no writer in the client.
- Attendance gift requirement: the gift event now carries its requirement kind and value (the byte and int after the play seconds; 1 a user value is set, 2 an item, 3 a user value is not set, 0 none), and Attendance 0 (claim) and 4 (early participation) give nothing for an event whose requirement the player does not meet, as the client lists it: kind 1 needs the player's unexpired user value of that type (id 0) to read true, kind 3 needs it absent.
  - Chosen here: a kind 2 event (started by an item) is never attended, because the server has no item that starts an attendance. A claim or early participation refused for the requirement gets no reply.
- Attendance 6 (the started item events) is sent at login after the guide records, as KMS2 sends it: an int count, then an int event id and a long time for each attendance event the player started with an item. The client keeps them in a map (`CMyCharacter::FUN_140d9f1c0`, client/ms2/notifydebugstate.cpp:147). The gift dialog reads it to show "item required" for a kind 2 event not yet started (uiattendgiftdialog.cpp:557), and the meret attendance dialog reads it for its locks (uicashattenddialog.cpp:477). KMS2 sends one entry (an event id that is not in the KR data, with a time years before), so the list is the account's own record. The time is the event's end for that player: a kind 2 (item) event with an entry shows and uses it as its end instead of the event's own (`GameEventAttendGiftCli::GetEndTime`, gameeventcli.cpp:564), which fits the old time KMS2 sends for an event long over.
- **Attendance gift play time and claim:** the gift has no claim button. The countdown popup (`UIDTAttendPopup`, client/ms2/uidtattendpopup.cpp) counts the event's play seconds down each second and, at 0, sends Attendance 0 (claim) by itself, again every 5 seconds until the day's completion time (user value 101) arrives; a click only opens the calendar, which has no claim either. The played seconds are user value 106 plus the time since the play start of TimeSync 3 (`GameEventAttendGiftCli`, gameeventcli.cpp:530; `CMainSystem`, mainsystem.cpp:366, which counts nothing from a start on an earlier day). The claim compared the time with the character's last save, which moves on every save, so it was refused without a word and the countdown stayed at 00:01. Now the session keeps the play start it sends in TimeSync 3, the claim counts the same seconds as the client (`AttendanceRules.PlaySeconds`), and a save stores 106 as the day's seconds with this login's added, until the next midnight, as KMS2 holds it (a login loads e.g. "15621", and the value grows by the session's seconds). At the daily reset TimeSync 3 is sent again with the reset time, as KMS2 sends it. The long-term calendar uses no play timer (EventReward 27 on load, 25 and 26 on a click).
- **Play-time reward values (DTReward):** the four values of a live play-time reward are sent once a second as KMS2 sends them: the time now (700), the total seconds (703), then the row's seconds (701), and the seconds grow by the time since the last step (the server added 1 per step whatever the time, and sent the row's seconds before the total). On the step that reaches the row's end the next row's index (702) and the restarted seconds are sent in place of the row's seconds, then the mail notice; the row's seconds at their end are not sent, as in KMS2. The client counts between the steps itself, the row's seconds plus the time since 700 (`UIDTEventDialog`, `uidteventdialog.cpp`). Chosen here: the values last until the last second of the server's local day (most KMS2 values end there, a few at a later day's last second; the server used 24 hours from the first step); the first step of a session counts one second, so time away is not play time; the restarted seconds are the claimed row's start, 0 in the KMS2 claims seen.
- **UserConditionEvent at login:** the server now sends UserConditionEvent (0x00BD) once at every login, after the guild, club and buddy lists and before the home blocks, as KMS2 does: mode 0 and id 0. The client's reader (`UISystemMailNotifyDialog`, `uisystemmailnotifydialog.cpp:324`) stores the id of mode 0 as the row last noticed; mode 1 names a user condition event row whose mail notice it shows, which the server never sends (KMS2 shows mode 0 only).
  - Chosen here: at the daily reset 106 is also sent as 0 until the next midnight (KMS2 shows only the TimeSync there), so the client holds no seconds of the old day; 106 is not sent at logout, the next login loads it.
  - Chosen here: the list is always empty, because no item starts an attendance event here (see the bullet above).
- **Ended attendance event replay, opt-in:** every AttendGift event in the KR server pack has ended (52 rows, the latest ending 2024-01-02), and the ingest drops ended events, so the client never shows the attendance calendar. With `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT=1` (or `true`) the ingest keeps one ended AttendGift event of the ingested locale and moves it to start on the ingest day (the ingest machine's local date, kept without a time zone like the other events' times, which the server compares with its local clock), at its original time of day, with its original length. One log line names the event and both date ranges. Unset, empty, `0` or `false` leaves the ingest as before; any other value stops the ingest. Takes effect with the next ingest, and the event ends on its moved end date (a later ingest moves it again).
  - Chosen here: an environment switch, default off. The event is the one with the latest end; on the same end, the longer one, then the higher id (in KR, 99000056 "Advent Calendar Rewards", 5 days, rather than 99000060, a 2-day compensation gift ending on the same minute). Only one event, not all 52. Its rewards, mail text, requirement and pay settings are kept as they are. An event with a reward item the client cannot rank is passed over (the log names it): the client reads an AttendGift reward as an ItemGift, whose rank comes from its own item data (GetItemRank of the item id, not the rank sent) and asserts at login when that is 0, which it is for an item missing from its item data or without a property rank. In KR, 99000056's four items are not in the item data, so the replay takes 99000060.
- **Long-term attendance calendar (LongTermAttendGift):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<info title maxattendance merat eventTag>`, value2 `<reward count item01 item02>` rows, items as "id,rank,count") and GameEvent writes what `LongTermAttendGiftContext::Read` reads: the tag, the end time, int 0, bool true, the reward rows (day, byte 0, both items; an empty slot as item 0 rank 1 count 1, as KMS2 sends it), the start and end times, the title, the meret price and maxattendance. The player's state is in GameEventUserValue 2700 (claimed days as "5,11,20"), 2701 (attend points), 2702 (days attended) and 2703 (when the last point was given, unix seconds; KMS2 sends it, the client does not read it), all ending with the event. EventReward 27 (sent at event load and dialog open) gives the day's point and replies 0x1b after updating 2701 and 2703; 26 + byte attends one missed day with a point (0) or for the meret price (1) and replies 0x1a after updating 2701 and 2702; 25 + day claims an attended, unclaimed day's row and replies 0x19. Missed days are the client's: min(80, calendar days since the start, the first counted) less the days attended. Every KR row has ended, so `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT` replays the latest ended one too (above).
  - Chosen here: one attend point per local calendar day, given on EventReward 27 and at the daily reset to an online player, with no cap on saved points (KMS2 shows a dozen saved up); missed days also stop at maxattendance; the meret attend charges Meret (the dialog's wallet check reads the meret balance and its lack prompt opens the meret shop, as AttendGift's pay does); a claim gives both items of the row (to the bag, else by mail), creates them before it gives any, so a bad item id claims nothing, and marks the day once an item has landed: when the first item goes to neither bag nor mail the day stays open and there is no reply, and when only the second fails the day is marked and the error is logged (the first cannot be taken twice); the values are per character like the other event values, where KMS2 keys them on the account. One switch for both replays: both are the attendance calendars, every row of both has ended, and the switch means "replay an ended attendance event". The long-term rows end to the second ("yyyy-MM-dd-HH-mm-ss"), which the ingest's minute form does not read, so such rows count as ended; the replay alone reads both forms for the ended rows (632 KR rows end to the second, some in 2099, so a parse fix for all would put those events live). As with AttendGift, a long-term event whose reward rows name an item the client's item data lacks (an empty slot aside) is passed over: its reward is read as a plain ItemEntity with the rank sent, so it does not assert, but the item would have no icon or name. In KR the replay takes 90000063. The type is read only with the switch on (off, the ingest stores no LongTermAttendGift event, as before), and a row whose value1 or value2 is not XML is skipped like any unreadable row. A later ingest during the run moves the event again; the stored values then follow the moved end when the character loads, so the claimed days stay claimed and the days attended count on (no missed day opens until the moved run has as many days), rather than all four running out at the first end with the event still live.
- **Stamp card (StampEvent):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<tag event>` and `<stamp quest special x y>` rows, value2 `<reward special count item01 item02>` rows, value3 the complete reward, value4 the key; items as "id,rank,count") and GameEvent writes what `StampEventContext::Read` reads: the tag, the end time, the stamp count, the key, bool true, the stamps (quest, special flag, x, y), the reward rows (count, special flag, both items; an empty slot as item 0 rank 1 count 1) and the complete reward. The dialog (client/ms2/uistampeventdialog.cpp) shows a stamp as done when its quest is rewarded and reads the claims from GameEventUserValue 1200 (1 once the complete reward is claimed), 1201 (the counts of the claimed reward rows as "4,7") and 1202 (the same for the special rows), all ending with the event. EventReward 0 + int count + byte special claims the row of that count and kind when as many stamps of the kind are done and the row is unclaimed; 1 claims the complete reward when every stamp is done. Each updates the user value first, then replies with the same sub-type, an int item count and an int id and short rank per item given, which the client shows in its reward notice (`CSceneStateGame::OnEventReward`). The client has no refusal reply, so a refused claim is only logged. Every KR row has ended (18 rows, the latest ending 2023-11-03), so the card is live only through `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT`, which replays the latest ended one too (above); the type is read only with the switch on, and a row whose value1 or value2 is not XML or that has no stamp is skipped.
  - Chosen here: the server starts a live card's quests at login and completes each when its conditions are met. They are account event missions (`eventMission event="stamp"`, auto start, remote complete) with an event tag that no QuestTag event carries, and the dialog has no action to accept or complete one, so nothing else would ever start or reward them; the card itself counts as their event. Several live cards: the claims go to the one without a key, the last of them in send order as the client keeps the last card sent, else the first (the client shows a keyed card only on a user value the server data does not name; KMS2 only shows key 0). A claim gives the row's items like the long-term calendar's (both to the bag, else by mail; all are made before any is given; the row is marked once the first has landed). The claim values 1200 to 1202 are the account's, like the quests: they are stored per account (table `account-event-value`), so a row is given once per account, also after the character that claimed is deleted; two characters of one account online together each hold their own copy until one saves, the only way left to a second claim. A stamp quest whose completion failed (full counters at start, no condition, a reward that did not fit) is tried again at every field entry and before a claim. The run of a card (and of a long-term calendar) is kept as its end time in `server-info` (`EventRun:<id>`) and tested when the world server starts: an end moved while the kept end is not over is the same run, and the stored values follow the new end even when a character logs in after the old one; an event that is live again after its kept end is a new run, which starts without the event's user values, the account's claims and the card's quests. Only the card's own stamp quests are deleted: the quests of its stamps that are stamp event missions (`eventMission event="stamp"`) and that no other card in the event list has as a stamp; a quest two cards share stays completed for both. An event that is absent before its kept end (taken out of the event list, or the replay switched off) and comes back before that end is the same run: a login in between does not read its values and does not delete them, and a stored value is deleted at login only once its own end is over. A new run needs all servers restarted, not the world server alone: a session that stayed online through a world-only restart still holds the old claims and the deleted quests, writes the claims back and fails its quest save. Databases that ran this card before the claims moved to the account keep their completed stamp quests, but the claims stored per character are deleted at login without being copied, so on such a database every reward row can be claimed once more per account; clear it by hand (delete the card's quests, or let the event end) if that matters. Edges: the test runs only at world start, so an end moved by an ingest counts as the same run only if the world is started once before the old end; values without a kept end (stored before this rule) count as the current run. One switch for the three replays; the replay passes over a card with a reward item the client's item data cannot rank or (with `MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML`) a stamp quest the played client's quest table lacks, since such a stamp could never be filled. In KR the replay takes 490010 (ended 2019-12-31; 20 stamps, 5 of them special, 9 reward rows): the five later cards are passed over, the latest, 99000041, because its complete reward 90090007 is not in the item data (it could not be given).
- **Level-up package (LevelUpPackage):** the ingest reads the event (value1 the quest scroll item, value2 the package's quest ids) and the event list sends it: the event's id, the item id and the quest list as text. Without it the client's menu entry answers that the event is not valid; with it the entry opens the package window once the scroll has started the quests (the QuestScroll item use and the window's reward button, Quest 27, were already handled). KR has one row, running until 2100.
- **Replay of the ended sale and helper bonus events:** `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT=1` also keeps the latest ended SaleUgcDesign, SaleHousing, FindDungeonHelperBonus, SaleRemake, SaleBeautyShop, SAHotTime, DungeonBonusReward and CollectItemGroup event that the ingest stores, moved to the ingest day like the attendance events, one log line each. Unset, none of the eight is stored (all their KR rows have ended). A CollectItemGroup with an item the played client lacks is passed over.
- **Event arcades (ArcadeOpen):** the ingest reads the event (value1 `<ms2><info arcadeId rewardCountPerDay/><reward itemID count grade/>...</ms2>`, `gameevent.cpp:6657`) and the event list sends what `ArcadeOpenContext::Read` reads: the event id, then the arcade id. The client keeps one event per arcade (`CGameEventValueArcadeOpen::Add`) and its lobby lists an event arcade (arcadegame.xml feature Event, the Three Two One arcades 3-5) only while such an event runs; the server now lets the lobby's enter (PlayArcade 10) into an event arcade only then, the lowest event id when two name it. Chosen here: the client never receives the reward rows or rewardCountPerDay, so the server gives the rows at each end of a game of the arcade, while the player has had fewer than rewardCountPerDay that day (GameEventUserValue 2400, not a KMS2 value, ending at midnight), each to the bag or by mail, and lists them in the Result's item list. KR has 3 rows, all ended (900500 arcade 3, 900501 arcade 4, 900502 arcade 5); `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT=1` replays the latest ended one of each arcade, so all three arcades open at once (an event with a reward the client lacks is passed over).
- **Tree watering (TreeWatering):** the ingest reads the event's three values (`GameEventTreeWatering::ReadTreeWateringData`, `gameevent.cpp:5664`: the steps and emotion days; the water and emotion rewards with their daily limits; the field, tree name tag, water pot and animations, and a nif per step) and the event list sends what `TreeWateringContext::Read` reads, its strings one-byte. With the pot held near the tree the client's action key waters it (state 0xa0) while the day's waterings (user value 1911) are under the limit, and on an emotion day after them cheers it (state 0xa1) while the emotions (1912) are under theirs; at the end of the cast it sends 0x00AA with 0 or 1. The server checks the event, the field, the pot (else 0x0118 mode 2 result 1) and the counts (mode 2 result 2, mode 3 result 3), then gives the reward to the bag or by mail, raises the count and replies with the count and limit (result 0). Chosen here: the pot is held, not used up (the client only asks that it is held); the counts end at midnight; the lowest event id when two run. The server's map entities carry no name tag, so the client's 400-unit tree range is not checked again. The client draws no tree: the step nifs are read by nothing, and the tree is a map prop named by its tag (02000064_tw_triatown02: TreeWateringEvent201804 and 201811 visible, 201906 invisible at -6225,4575,2400). KR has 30 rows (900101-900130), all ended; `MS2_REPLAY_ATTEND_GIFT=1` replays the latest one whose pot and rewards the client has, 900120 (map 2000064, tree 201906, pot 30001431, 10 waterings a day for 30001432; 2 steps, no emotion day), with its steps moved with it; 900121-900130 name items the KR client lacks and are not replayed. Cheering runs in the tests only: the replayed row has no emotion day.
- **Event days and hours on the local clock:** an event's `dayOfWeek` and `partTime` are tested on the server's local day and time of day, the clock its start and end are compared on; they were tested on UTC.
- **Event types read but not sent:** TransferItemBound (20 KR rows, all ended) is one flag the client stores and never reads. BurningLevelup (10 KR rows, all ended; a level range, an exp bonus, an effect, reward items and a level potion option, shown by the character list and the level-up package window) is not ingested or sent: its entry is a context with several lists, and it needs a per-character burning state the server does not keep.
- Sub-types the client sends that are dropped or only read, not in the table above, because the client and the data do not settle them:
  - Shop 13 (the new-products page, for a shop whose info sets DisplayNew): the reply is a Shop 13 start and records of `CNpcShopItemNew` built from `shop_specialRandom_new.xml`, which is not ingested.
  - Job 7 (learn a skill from an item; `UIMessageBox::HandleLearnSkillButton`, box tag `learn_skill`): nothing in the client opens that box, so the server would open it; which items open it and what learning stores is not known.
  - Beauty 4 (an item taken off in the beauty shop preview, `Customize::Equipment::SendRemoved`, the item uid): whether the item goes back to the bag or is deleted is not known. The body is read and dropped.
  - Attendance 2 (the gift window loaded) and 3 (the meret attendance box): the reply is not known; the client reads Attendance 5, 6, 7 and 9.
  - MeretMarket 201 (the chat command SaleMarket, client GM level 50 or more; it has no body and the client reads no reply, so what it does is not known), BlackMarket 6 (no body; the reply is not known).
- MeretMarket 107 (sent each time the market dialog opens; it was taken for a cart load) is answered with the client's sale settings, MeretMarket 107 read by `CMeratMarketMan::OnSettleSettings`: two achievement grades and a product buy count, which the client only puts into the buy refusals 0xbd, 0xbe and 0xbf (the server sends none, so 0), then the days from a sale until its profit can be collected. The client counts a sale as collectable after these days and assumes 7 without the answer; the server waits `UGCShopProfitDelayDays` (10), so the dialog offered a collect the server refused without a word for days 7 to 10. It now gets 10.
- MeretMarket 103 (the ad list, which the market dialog asks for when it opens) is answered with an empty list, mode 103 and a zero count. The client's request sets flags that only the answer clears, and the answer makes the dialog refresh its limited tab for an empty list (`CMeratMarketMan::OnAdProducts`, client/ms2/meratmarketman.cpp:2129, `RefreshLimitedTab`); the server did not answer. KMS2 answers each request this way: 724 requests in 599 sessions, 723 answered, every answer the empty list. Not done: KMS2 also sends the empty list without a request (101 times in 59 sessions, mid session); what triggers it is not known. The ad data itself is not ingested.
- NpcTalk 8 (alliance quest window, accept or complete one quest; quest id, then 0 to accept or 2 to complete, `UIAllianceQuestDialog` onAccept, unit_140bf60f0_uialliancequestdialog.cpp:288) is handled while a talk is open: only quests of type 9 (alliance quest) are taken. 0 starts the quest with its usual requirements (Quest error 3 if it cannot be started); 2 completes a started quest whose conditions are done and gives its rewards (Quest error 2 otherwise). The window lists the quests from the client's own quest table; the server does not check that the quest belongs to the talk NPC's alliance or the fame grade it asks for. A daily alliance quest, or a weekly one without a group, starts only when it is in the lists sent (Alliance missions, under Fixes); a done one is offered again by the quest repeat rule (Quests started again, under Fixes).
- Wedding 26 (enter the ceremony) and 30 (the answer to a question of the hall) are handled. The couple gets the hall ticket (sub-type 24) on field entry while the ceremony time is at most 30 minutes away, the window in which the client offers the way in, and only while the couple is engaged: after the vow (married) and in a divorce state the hall is held and neither the couple nor a guest gets its ticket. Each marriage has its own room of the hall map; the couple, the guest list and, for a public hall, anyone may enter. The hall trigger asks the couple (sub-types 30 and 31), and its vow step stores the engaged couple as married with the marriage time, which the wedding packet and the character info now carry. At the vow each of the couple gets the mail 40000010 with the hall's completion gift (weddingCompleteItem: the night row for a ceremony in the night map, the day row otherwise); a first reservation paid in merets mails 40000008 with the hall's reservation items (weddingItem, the list of the client's reservation page); a change mails no gifts. A reservation costs the hall price, or the coupon the sender picked; a change costs only the meret difference to the new hall (0 for a change of the time alone) and uses no coupon. The mails 40000012 and 40000014 (merets or a coupon back for a hall that could not hold the ceremony) are not sent: no table says what that event is, and the hall row does not store how it was paid. Wedding 27 (find a character for the guest dialog) and 28 (send invitations) are handled: the guests are stored on the hall, the sender pays the invitation meso for each (1,000), each guest gets the invitation mail (type 112) and, on field entry inside the window, the hall's ticket; the sender gets sub-type 29 and the invited list in sub-type 1. Refused as the client does: more than 70 invitations, a guest invited before, too little meso, and (without a text) from 10 minutes before the ceremony. The invitation mail's title and text are the client's own two string keys (`s_wedding_mail_title_receiver`, `s_wedding_mail_contents_receiver`): the mail list takes sender, title and text from the server for this type, reads a key from its string table, and gives them with the wedding data (groom, bride, date, hall name) to its script; what the script draws of them is not in the client code. Unproven: the order of the two ids of an invited pair in sub-type 1 (written account, character). The session looks once a minute (on its own event queue, no new thread) for a hall whose window opened since, for the couple and for a guest, and sends that ticket once; a guest who is invited inside the window gets it at that look. A changed reservation keeps its guests and, when the time or the hall changed, mails each of them again with the client's change keys (`s_wedding_mail_change_title_receiver`, `s_wedding_mail_change_contents_receiver`); a cancelled reservation mails each guest with the cancel keys and the attribute `cancelMail` the client reads from the mail's wedding data. A hall that ends by its time mails nobody. The trigger moves the guests in a box to the portals of the list. The groom is the partner who reserved the hall (the groom of the invitation mail and the megaphone link), the bride the other: the hall steps for one of them now run. The reserver is the hall row's owner, the partner who sent the reservation. A row whose owner is neither of the couple (only a row changed outside the game) is read with the second partner as reserver, so that one is the groom; a ceremony without one of the couple as reserver names no groom and no bride, and these steps then do nothing while the ceremony's other steps run. wedding_move_user Groom or Bride moves that one, when in the box, to a portal of the list; wedding_set_user_emotion sends that one Wedding 0x27 (result 2, `OnMode27Accept`, weddingmanager.cpp:1155: the weddingemotion row, then keep-item 0 and loop 0, so a held item is put down and the row plays once), and the client puts its own player in the emotion state and shows it to the others; wedding_set_user_look_at and _rotation send that one trigger Ui 0xb (look at the other's position) and Ui 10 (the rotation), each with its immediate flag; wedding_user_to_patrol walks that one along the patrol, as move_user_path does (the patrol index of the scripts names nothing more). Not done: the broken-wedding step, for which the client shows no state to change. Both partners must be on the same channel. The npc test for the couple (weddingHallEntryType) needs a new ingest of the server tables; until then it is not tested.
- Wedding 23 (drop all hall tickets) is sent on every field entry after the marriage and the hall, before the tickets that still hold, as KMS2 sends it. The client keeps a ticket until it is told to drop it, so without this a ticket of a hall that ended or was cancelled stayed in the hall list until the client was restarted. Not done: Wedding 25 (drop one ticket by its id), which KMS2 sends to a player in the field; the next field entry clears the ticket instead.
- RoomDungeon 21 (reset the weekly clear count of the united reward, after a confirm box; the button shows only with client feature 185): the client's refusal texts say the reset is once a week for the whole account and only at the weekly limit. The server keeps no account-wide mark of a used reset, and the client does not show whether the reset has a price. No count is reset; the answer is the client's text for the reset being switched off (RoomDungeon error 33). It stays counted. 17 (expert mode) and 24 (favourite mark), counted here before, are handled. The four lobby buttons of the dungeon search window (4, 12, 13, 14) belong to the match queue and are not counted.
- RequestCube 5: the account that owns a plot sends it with the action key in the talk scene of that plot's sign (the plot number, then 0); any other account sends the purchase (2) from the same place. The only answer the client reads under sub-type 5 takes the owner off the plot, and the server already sends that packet for a sale (6, from the sale window). Whether 5 is a second way to sell or something else is not settled from the client, and acting on a guess could remove a home, so it is read and logged.
- RequestCube 30: the dialog of a function cube of sub-kind 7 (home summon) sends the cube's id string and a typed text of two or more letters. The client reads no answer under sub-type 30, and nothing in it shows which packet the named player would get, so it is read and logged. The dialog opens for a function cube with `UseUiFunction="Summon"` (the client's sub-kind 7, commonfiles/objectdata.cpp:170); no function cube in the data has it, so with this data no client sends 30 (the same holds for the home password, `Password`, sub-kind 6).
- RequestCube 19: a debug index stepped with two keys, sent only while a debug switch of the client is on. Nothing in the client turns that switch on (it is only cleared), so a normal client never sends it. It needs a GM account and is then only logged.
- RequestCube 56, 57 and 58 (the build budget of a home and its build helpers) are handled. 56 sets the budget in meso and merat: the owner's wallet pays a rise and gets a fall back, and a fall that would not fit into the wallet is refused. 57 and 58 add and remove a helper by character name (the helper's account is stored; at most 20, a limit of the server). A helper in the owner's home, while the owner is there, may place, remove, rotate and replace cubes; a cube a helper places is bought from the budget at its shop price, and a cube a helper removes goes to the owner's storage. The client gets sub-types 0x35 (owner and balance, for a helper), 0x38 to 0x3a (the answers) and 0x3b (the helper list). Known limits: the client offers a helper only the cube categories marked "delegator" in `cubeCategory.xml`, which the server's data does not have, so the server does not check the category; a helper can build only while the owner is in the home, and not on an outdoor plot or in the owner's planner modes; removing a cube gives nothing back to the budget; sub-types 0x37 and 0x3c are not sent; place, remove, rotate and replace of one plot run under one lock, the other code that reads or fills a plot's cubes (field load, plot save and reset) does not take it.
- RequestCube Sub-type 13 has a case but the client never sends it, so it is not counted. 68 (the market's blueprint preview) builds only in the model home; if the model home already holds cubes they are removed and the client is asked to send the preview again (sub-type 0x45 of the cube packet, which the client answers with a new 68 once its own cube list is empty; that the retail server used 0x45 this way is read from the client's reader, not seen). In a planner the server refuses a cube that the player neither owns nor finds in the shop, so such a blueprint previews in part. The client gives no rule for this case: for 68 it sends only the blueprint's id, does no owned or shop test of its own, and draws the cubes the server sends back, so the server's refusal is kept. 70 (the bill of a used blueprint item) is handled.
- RequestCube 49 (send everyone out, the home option window's button, `UIHomeOptionDialog::SendRequestCubeMode49`, no body) is handled: in the owner's own home every player of another account is sent back to the map they came from. From anyone else, or outside a home, it is dropped. The client shows its own notice, so there is no answer.
- PlayerHost 6 (the billboard's window) ends with the board's id, as the client reads it (`BillBoardInfo::Read`); 0 was sent. The same id is the long before the owner's name in the board's interact object (the owner's character id was sent there): the client compares the two to tell whether the open window belongs to the board it is asked about (`interactobject.cpp:1670`), so both carry the board's id. The staff's remove request, ItemBillboard 8 (`uiitembillboarddialog.cpp:571`), sends the id back; the client sends it only at GM level 50 and up. That request is handled for a GM account: the board with the id on the sender's field is removed; an id of 0, an unknown id or a sender without the GM permission is dropped. KMS2's window carries 25 more bytes after the id that this client does not read; they are left out.
  - Chosen here: the board's id is a counter of the running Game server that starts at 1; a board lasts no longer than its field.
- Achieve 5 (GM debug buttons of the trophy window) is handled for a GM account. The client names the four buttons up, down, up30 and up99; the server reads them as: finish the grade in progress, go one grade back (the reward grade stays, so finishing the grade again gives no second reward), and set the counter to 30 % or 99 % of the grade's goal. That reading comes from the names only.
- Trophies that do not count: movement trophies (run, swim, climb, glide, ride, fall) are reported one unit for each block moved and the rest of a block is dropped; KMS2 sends steps of five or more and its unit is not proven. These trophy ids are counted in KMS2 and have no row in the ingested data, so they never count: 21100018, 21100028, 21100037, 21100041, 21233016, 22200342, 22200343, 22300025, 22501045, 22501046, 22501047, 23100012, 92000142.
- Mapleopoly 5 (the GM debug dice) is handled for a GM account: one roll with the two dice the GM picked, at the cost of a normal roll.
- Buddy: with "refuse friend requests" on, the request is declined by the server and no longer shown to the refusing client, which used to send a second decline for the entry that was already gone.
- Buddy 19 (the count that ends the list at login): the blocked entries and the requests the player sent are not counted, as in KMS2. Requests received stay counted (chosen here: no KMS2 list held one). The client has no reader for 19.
- Buddy block list: the check the party, guild and club invites use looked a blocked player up by the block entry's id and not by the character's id, so it never found one. A friend request to a player the sender blocked made a request entry next to the block; it is refused now with code 5 (the code is chosen here). Blocking a player by name a second time made a second block entry; it is dropped (the client's report window also blocks only a player it has not blocked).
- SkillMacro 1 with no tab (every skill macro tab removed): the save is refused, because the client also sends an empty save when the window closes before the load answer came. A player who removes all tabs gets the stored ones back at the next login.

Revival 1 (the meret revive in place, which was only logged and not counted above) and ChatSticker 1 (expired sticker groups) are handled now. So are Quest 18 (the quest portal opens again when the quest talk ends) and Buddy 20 (the "refuse friend requests" option: a request is declined on receipt while the option is on; the server asks the client for the option at login and also learns it when the options are saved), Club 12 (the leader hands the club to a member) and HomeAction 1 to 3 (the home owner kills, sends out or revives a visitor from the player menu; only sub-type 3 was counted above). RequestRide 6 (the "ride alone" game option: the field is told and nobody can board the mount while it is on) and FunctionCube 5 and 7 (use a cube; closing a cube's dialog hands the cube back to other players) are handled too, EventReward 8 as well, and the client sends no Prestige sub-type other than the two that were already handled. The two HomeAction entries left are the PvP control dialog (14) and the OX quiz (10). The revive block of a buff (`disableRevivalHere`) is a new metadata property: it is empty until the metadata is ingested again. RequestItemInventory 8 and 9 (sort, expand) and Trade 14 (busy answer) were already handled and are no longer counted. The meret revive charges the price the button shows, or one free revive coupon when the box is ticked and a coupon is held; the revive debuff is added only if the server's constants name it.

Packets the client reads that the server never sends, although a player can ask for them. The request is dropped (no handler) unless a line says otherwise:
- MoveEventField 0x0085 (the world map's event field portal): RequestMoveEventField (field id) is handled. A field counts as an event field while a world boss of the time event table is active there with a live channel; the player is then moved to it in the current channel, otherwise the answer is byte 0 (s_err_timeevent_move_field). The continent group that the client tests itself (Constants::AreSameGroup: the same continent code, or both in WarpOpenContinent) is tested again from the map data and the constants table, and a field outside it is answered with byte 1 (s_err_timeevent_same_continent). Own choices: only world boss fields count, the battle state is not checked again, and the World server's answer about the running events is kept 5 seconds per session so that a repeated request makes no new World call. Not confirmed: the client's portal list of a world map icon is filled by no recovered client code, so the button may never show; no live try.
- InteractNpc 0x0059 (object id, talkable byte) is handled. The client offers an NPC's interact (the cannons and support NPCs of raids such as Crimson Balrog and Turka, whose data has an `<interact>` row) only while its talkable byte is set (`InteractNpcAction::FindInteractNpc`, client/ms2/actionkeyobject.cpp); the server wrote it as 0 in FieldAddNpc, so no NPC could be used. The ingest now reads the row (`UseSkill,<skill>,<level>`, the only function in the KR data; casting and cool time), FieldAddNpc sends the byte as set for such an NPC outside its cool time, and 0x32 (sent after the cast) is answered when the NPC is alive, within the InteractionDistance constant and not cooling down: the NPC casts the skill, and 0x0059 clears the byte for the cool time and sets it again at its end, for every player in the field. A friendly NPC's skills now take the mobs as hostile targets and the players as friendly ones (the server took the players as hostile targets for every NPC). Chosen here: the cool time is the NPC's, not one per player; the casting time is not checked; the `interact_npc` condition is not counted. Takes effect with the next ingest.
- **Npc region skills without a hit target:** an npc attack placed its region (splash) skills only at targets hit inside the attack's own range, so a shot that explodes far away never left its region. The skill data marks such a splash `nonTargetActive` (`SkillLevelData` reads it), and an attack that fires along a magic path with a distance and a velocity now lands it (`NpcSplashRules`): in KMS2 packets a raid cannon's shot (path distance 2,500, velocity 1,200) is sent with the direction to a monster and no target id, and its region is added on that monster's cell, 2,430 to 2,560 from the cannon and 450 below it, about two seconds after the cast, where it damages, pushes and slows the monsters. Chosen here: the aim is the npc's own target, else the nearest other actor of the attack's target kind within the path's distance plus one block; the delay is the ground distance at the path's velocity; a target farther than that is no aim and the npc itself never is; a shot without an aim ends at the path's distance and end height; the splashes of the same attack that need a target stay with the targets hit in its range. A `nonTargetActive` splash of an attack without such a path is placed only at hit targets, as before: nothing in the client or in KMS2 packets shows a region for it when nothing is hit. Attacks with a friendly range and no `includeCaster` reach other npcs only, as in the client (`CBaseCollision::Detect` skips the owner unless the range includes the caster).
- FittingDoll 0x007B (the fitting doll in a home): a placed mannequin cube keeps its items as item rows owned by the cube uid (group 13). The plot owner dresses it (0x42 modes 2 put on, 7 take off, 10 return all), within the client's own checks: skin vs equipment doll, gender, slot, and overlapping slots taken off to the inventory. Removing the cube returns the items to the inventory, or by mail when it is full. The DB rows are the authority: the same home open on two channels reloads them before each change, and a row leaves the doll only while it is still on that cube (one conditional write), so an item cannot be given twice. An item bound to another character is refused (result 0x12, s_fittingdoll_Invalid_binding, "belongs to another character"; the client tests no binding itself): one bound to the character dressing the doll is put on, and another character of the account cannot take it off (0x12). A recall or a removed cube gives such an item to the character it is bound to (its row, in its inventory at its next login). Mannequins work only in the indoor home: a forfeited or expired outdoor plot drops its cubes without removing them one by one, which would leave the items on a cube that is gone; a mannequin placed on an outdoor plot is plain furniture. A put on is refused (InvalidDoll) when the cube no longer has its row on the plot, tested in the same transaction as the write; the item row itself is not tested, since the inventory is saved lazily. Known gap: an older instance of the home on another channel that saves its plot deletes the cube rows it does not hold, a mannequin placed and dressed elsewhere among them; its item rows then stay on the cube uid with no cube (a sweep that mails such rows back is not done). Not done: transform (mode 3) and the bound item before a recall (mode 9).
- MaidCraftItem 0x00A9 (a maid's crafting): modes 0, 1, 3, 4, 6, 10 and 11 are sent, see Maid crafting; FieldMaid 0x009D and UserMaid 0x009E are sent.
- RecallUser 0x008C (call another player to your place): the client has no request that starts it, so the GM command `recalluser <name>` sends the invite to a player on any channel (for another channel the World server passes the invite to that channel and the result back to the caller's; the GM check is the command's, on the caller's channel). The result values follow the client's string keys (ids 2299 to 2305): the caller gets kind 5 value 1 (s_recall_user_notify_confirm_recall) when the invite goes out, kind 5 value 0 (s_recall_user_notify_recall) after the player was moved, and kind 7 for every failure: 6 not found, 4 already being recalled, 2 the move failed, any other value the player declined. Kind 6 (s_recall_user_notify_recalled) names the one who recalls, so it goes to the invited player with the caller's name when the move starts. Kind 5 values 2 and 3 are not sent. A player who is offline is answered as not found. The invited player gets the confirm box and the kind 5 text with the caller's name; the caller gets only the result. When the caller stands in a home, the invited player is moved into that home (the home field's owner goes with the move, on another channel too); the recall scroll and the warp command still enter no one else's home.
- Gamble 0x009C, CardReverseGame 0x00DA and BirthdayCard 0x0111 (event windows): the server's event table has no data for these events. TreeWatering 0x0118 runs (Tree watering). PlayArcade 0x00D8 runs for the Spring Farm, Boom Boom Ocean and Three Two One arcades (trigger actions, Run in part: arcade_*); the lobby enters an event arcade while an ArcadeOpen event names it (Event arcades). BirthdayCard: the NPC talk type 0x58 opens the window, which asks with one byte 0 and shows the card only when the answer's birthday (mode 1: character id, birthday) is in the current month; accounts store no birthday.
- SmartRecommendBilling 0x00A0 and NoticeDialog 0x00CF (shop recommendation and notice windows): the server has no content for them.
- PcBangBonus 0x00A5 and SetPcBang 0x00B5 (PC cafe benefits): accounts carry no PC cafe state.
- Pvp 0x00B6 and TeamPvp 0x009A: the rank duel and the free-for-all arena are built (see Rank duel and Individual arena); the team and guild PvP packets are not sent, and a field's PK mode works without them.
- SpectateMushtopia 0x011A, Survival 0x0119 and SurvivalEvent 0x0116 (Mushking Royale): no match server. The server writes Survival and SurvivalEvent for its medal data, and this client has no reader for either.
- WaitingTicketUpdate 0x00B4 (the login queue): the server has no login queue, so the client never waits.
- DungeonMatch 0x00EF (dungeon match queue): no match queue window; the Party 32 to 34 matchmaker does not use it.
- MeretUse 0x0117 (the event meret balance after a purchase): the server keeps no event meret balance; purchases update the normal meret packet.
- StateFishingData 0x00FA (another player's fishing state): sent to the others in the field when a player starts and stops fishing. A player who enters the field later is told which players are already fishing.
- RelocateWorld 0x0072 (the world transfer request): no handler; the server runs one world, so there is no world list and no transfer address to send.
- Cash 0x006A/0x00AD (the cash shop window, menu id 0x4ebc): the server has no cash products and no cash balance; its shop is the Meret Market, which has its own packets. The window's requests got no answer. The three it sends when it opens are answered now, as KMS2 answers them (`CCashMarketMan::OnCash`, client/ms2/cashinventorybag.cpp): 1 with code 1 and a balance of 0, 0 with an empty product list, 5 (and the page buttons' 5) with code 1, the page asked for and no bought products. The login sends mode 9 with 1 after ServerEnter, as KMS2 does at every login; it is the client's own default, and 0 would make the menu button show a notice and not open the window. The requests that need a listed product (2 buy, 3 gift, 4, 6, 7) are logged.
- DynamicBanword 0x0124 (the client sends its ban word list version when it enters the game): the handler only logs the request. The server's ban words are the client's own static list, so there is no newer list to send.
- ChangeAttributes 0x00C8 kind 8 (a GM-level client asks for the cost again): it has a writer and nothing sends it. ChangeAttributes answers with kinds 1, 2, 4 (the cost of the staged item), 5 (error) and 6 (the pending change when the window opens). ItemExchange 0x0102 kind 0 is sent when a target item is put into the exchange scroll window and lists the scroll's ingredients (the same list for every target); kind 2 is the result and kind 3 answers the GM fill buttons.
- DebugMode 0x0087: the client reads it in three debug windows: the NPC control window (kind 5 state text, 6 skill list, 7 open the AI debug window, 8 AI line), the debug guide window (kind 7 command lines) and the dialog center window (kind 10 trigger debug line). The server sends kinds 5 and 6 of the NPC control window and kind 7 of the debug guide window, to GM accounts only, as answers to SetDebugMode 0x50 (see GM-only packets). The AI debug kinds 7 and 8 and the trigger kind 10 are not sent. Kind 10 (string trigger file path, int line; asked for with SetDebugMode mode 9: path, bool on or off, uidialogcenterdialog.cpp:285, 411) needs the 1-based line of the client's own trigger XML file for each step; the server keeps only a merged and rewritten copy of that XML without the original lines, so it has no correct line to send.

The checker follows the client's item reader (CItem::ReadFromStream, commonfiles/item.cpp:2655). It picks the item's optional blocks from the item tables, which it reads at run time. Where a packet does not carry the item id, the checker reads a plain item.

- Dungeon mission types the server does not count yet: LimitUserCount, and the client-only event types (KillNpc, UseSkill, UseInteractObject, HealingPotion, UseEp, UseSp, DamageCount). No dungeonmission row uses any of them; LimitUserCount reads only value1 (a user count, default 1) and its packet only the score, so neither the data nor the client says when it scores. The others are not in the server's type enum. A penalty Trigger mission (2 rows) is filled by dungeon_mission_complete like the others; the client does not show whether the script meant one penalty step. The server sends no mission rank: the client works out the rank from the mission scores and DungeonConfig itself.
- Dungeon season rewards (see Dungeon season reward mail): open are the `dayOfWeeks` day (see Dungeon rank score), the reset hour (the current KR season starts at 08:00, the server resets at midnight), whether the mail goes at the week's end or at the season's end, and the cap of the total. The per-room `seasonRankRewardID` rank rewards (DungeonRankRewardBase ranks 1-5 with an item and a system mail, the client's packets 0x13 and 0x14) are not done: no thresholds for the ranks are known. Also open: the record's `ClearTimestamp` is written as the client's extra-reward-count expiry (m_extraCount, with `ExtraClears` as its value), while the server uses it as the room's first-clear mark (`GetEnterLimit`, DungeonClear); `ExtraClears` is never raised, so the client always sees 0, and the meaning of that expiry for the extra-reward-count event is not clear, so it is left as it is.
- Trigger actions and conditions that only log and return a fixed result, and the ones from this list that run now (dungeon_disable_ranking and the Run line). The numbers are shipped trigger files using each one. The fixed result stays wherever a real result would block a shipped script.
  - dungeon_disable_ranking (2, the Colosseum continue-play): the room's run is left off the Colosseum board (web ranking, Colosseum line).
  - Run: wedding_move_user for guests (the players in the box who are not the couple each go to a random portal of the list in the hall, as the hall scripts note), shadow_expedition_open_boss_gauge and close_boss_gauge (the score board 0xE1 as the boss gauge, at 0 points), and give_reward_content (every player in the field gets the reward content: exp, meso and item tables).
  - Modes the server does not run, with what is missing: npc_to_patrol_in_box (96 files, the Maple Survival boat maps; needs a survival match). dark_stream_* runs in part (the next line). A wedding move to another map and wedding_broken are not done (the groom and bride steps run: the groom is the hall's reserver, see Wedding 26 above). shadow_expedition_points stays false above 0: nothing in the scripts, the client or the tables says what raises the gauge points. create_field_game and field_game_* (17 files: hide and seek, Maple Survival, guild vs game, water gun battle) are whole game modes. set_pvp_zone and pvp_zone_ended run for zone types 1, 2 and 3 (the Red Arena, the free-for-all, Treasure Island; see Individual arena); type 4 (guild) and the other pvp_zone_* actions are not built. field_war_end (4 files): the field war table gives the war ids, fields and reward, but the maps start from a user value a field war schedule sets, which the server has not, and the client has only the invite popup (0x11F). guild_vs_game_*, show_event_result, set_user_value_from_user_count, set_user_value_from_guild_vs_game_score and user_value_to_number_mesh (5 files, guild vs guild, 66200001_gd) need the guild vs game. arcade_spring_farm_*, arcade_boom_boom_ocean_* and arcade_three_two_one{,2,3}_* run (the Run in part line below); the event arcades 3-5 (feature Event, the Three Two One games) open with the ArcadeOpen game events (Event arcades), which in KR have all ended and run only through the replay.
  - Run in part: dark_stream_* (59 files, fields 02000350 and 02000498). start_game opens the score dialog (DarkStream 0xC5 command 0), a spawn of spawn_monster gives its score once all its monsters are dead (command 1), and clear_round of the round start_game named sends the result (command 2: score, best score before the run, exp, meso, habi, then the reward items); the scripts have no fail or dungeon clear, so the run ends there and the player leaves with the result dialog (RequestMoveField 1) or the portal the script opens. Missing: what damage_penalty takes from the score (the client shows a score below 0, nothing says when it is taken; it is kept, not applied); the result gives each player in the field the row of table/darkstream.xml (ingested as DarkStreamTable, rows by round, a locale's row replacing the plain one) for the rounds cleared, or the row below when there is none: the meso, the habi, and the items (id,rank,count, the order of ItemEntity; to the bag, else by mail), sent in the result as ItemEntity::Read reads them (int id, short rank, int count, four bools sent as false). Inferred, not given: the exp is the row's expRate times the Dark Stream room's rewardExp (dungeonroom.xml), the rule of the rooms' own rewardExpRate; the client reads rewardExp and rewardExpRate but uses neither, the exp table has no Dark Stream row, and the client's exp type list has no Dark Stream type, so no level-based rule is given. How several items are listed in one row is not shown (each shipped row has one item; the reader takes further triples after a comma or semicolon). Habi is HaviFruit (the client icon list puts it after lu/Rue, as Maple2 puts HaviFruit after Rue). How a run ends before the last round (death or leaving) is not in the scripts; leaving ends it with no result. The best score is kept per character (table dark-stream-record: the score and the time it was set; the migration AddDarkStreamRecord, removed with the character): the result sends the best before the run (0 before a first run), and the run's score replaces it only when it is higher (an equal score keeps the older record and its time). The web ranking (irrq.aspx) answers the Dark Stream boards from it, built like the trophy boards: 0x21 is the header, a count and rows of rank, character id, name, profile image, two ints, the score and the time it was set, as GameRanking::DarkStream reads them (the best 200, or with a search name the row of that character); 0x1f is the character's rank on that board (0 without a score). The request's ranking id is the board's job tab: the ranking dialog adds a tab per job code under the board, 0 for every job. Not settled by the client: the two ints of a row are read but used nowhere (sent as 0); how equal scores rank is chosen here (the earlier record first). No season is kept, so the boards of the season before (0x22, and 0x20 for the own rank) are empty and rank 0.
  - Run in part: arcade_spring_farm_* (the Spring Farm arcade, arcade id 1). The lobby asks with PlayArcade mode 10 (enter) and mode 9 (scores). Enter moves the player to the game's field (table/arcadegame.xml, ingested as ArcadeGameTable); an unknown game, an event arcade (isEventArcade) no running ArcadeOpen event names, a game without a field, a dead player, a player in battle (as the client's lobby refuses it) or one already in the field is refused. Each player gets a field of their own: the arcade fields 51000001-51000006 are solo instance fields (table/Server/instancefield.xml Type 1), and an instance field entered without an owner or a room is a new field each time (FieldEntryRules.NewFieldPerEntry). start_game opens the score dialog (0xD8 Open, the open type is the arcade id) and the life bar (LifeInit, chosen as 1); each interact object use and each spawn cleared adds its score once (Score); an object that keeps its state when used (Ui, Telescope) scores once per object in the game, while the shipped scored objects are all Mesh (interact type 19000022, reset after 3 s) and score on each use; clear_round of the open round adds the time score (the whole seconds left times the rate, the remain value; for the open round only); a player's death takes a life, and 0 lives ends the game at once; end_game ends it once. The end pays only the game's players, the characters in the field at start_game, and sends each the Result: score, best before the game, exp, meso. Chosen here: the exp is the arcade exp curve (CommonExpTable arcade) times the row's expRate; the reward row is the one of table/arcadereward.xml (ArcadeRewardTable, a locale's row replacing the plain one; a second node with the same arcadeId adds its rows to the first one's, though the shipped file has one node per arcade, ids 1-5) with the most rounds at or below the rounds cleared, none below the first row; no habi is given, and items only by an event arcade's event (Event arcades). The best is kept per character, arcade and Arcade season (table arcade-record, the migration AddArcadeRecord, removed with the character); a higher score replaces it, an equal or lower one keeps it; without an Arcade season no best is kept. The scores list (mode 9) sends the bests of the current Arcade season (region = the season id, 0 and no rows when there is none). After each Result the server sends HideAndSeek 0x12B mode 3 with the player's object id: for the player's own object the client closes the life bar (dialog 0x4efc, the one mode 4 opens as UIArcadeLifeBar) and shows the hud again.
  - Run in part: arcade_boom_boom_ocean_* (arcade id 2, entered from the lobby like Spring Farm). start_game opens the score dialog and the life bar with life_count (20); set_skill_score keeps the score of each skill id; a hit by a non-player skill with a score, or a buff granted with one (each grant, a refresh too), adds it; the score does not go below 0. Chosen here: only a hit that lands scores (not evaded, not on an invincible player: damage type Normal, Critical or Block); a negative skill score also takes a life, and so does a death, but a hit that takes a life and kills takes only that one; the time score of a round is the whole seconds survived, at most the round's duration, times the round's rate, given once at clear_round of the open round, so a game that ends inside a round (0 lives, the fail box's end_game) pays nothing for it (the client has no say: the score is the server's). 0 lives ends the game and moves the game's players to portal 44 (the fail box's portal, as the scripts' fail end does).
  - Run in part: arcade_three_two_one_*, arcade_three_two_one2_* and arcade_three_two_one3_* (Three Two One, the event arcades 3, 4 and 5 of the fields 51000004-06, entered from the lobby while their ArcadeOpen event runs). start_game opens the score dialog with init_score and the life bar with life_count; start_round (round, ui_duration, the order of the script attributes) shows the three buttons for ui_duration seconds (PlayArcade 7, the client's couple dance window); each player picks with PlayArcade 11 (an int direction: the buttons send 2, 1, 3); result_round hides the buttons and sets the user value ThreeTwoOneResult the script reads, 1 when every game player in the field picked and none picked the round's direction, else 0; result_round2 of a win adds 1000 and the streak (PlayArcade 8 type 0), of a loss takes a life and resets the streak. Chosen here: the win score 1000 and no pick = a loss (the scripts give neither); the rounds cleared of the reward row are the rounds won, since every script's clear_round passes round 1 and runs after a loss too; the score starts at init_score (10000), so a game with no win still ends at 10000 and keeps that as the best. 0 lives ends the game and moves the players to portal 44, as above.
  - give_guild_exp (13): type 2 would give the guild exp of a GuildGainExp table row, as the client's winMiniGameGuildExp suggests; neither pack has a GuildGainExp table, so the amount and the mapping are not known.
  - set_mini_game_area_for_hack and unset_mini_game_area_for_hack (13): anti-hack areas. The client's check for them is not known.
  - give_exp (1) and start_tutorial (1): one use each, and the script does not settle the rule.
  - announce: no shipped script uses it.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) boss boards: types 0x47 (71), 0x48 (72), 0x51 (81) and 0x5b (91) are answered from the boss clears the game keeps. The ranking dialog's boss boards (raid 7, chaos raid 8, reverse raid 9, lapenta 0xf and turka 0x11) add one tab per dungeon room of that group type with a BossRanking, the request's ranking id is that BossRanking, and every tab has three modes that ask for the same types on all five boards (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey): the clears per character (0x48, the own rank 0x47), the first clears (0x51) and the fastest clears (0x5b). An earlier note here said 71 and 72 are raid-only types the client never asks for; that was wrong. The first change of a room to Clear keeps one row (table boss-clear, the migration AddBossClear) when the room has a BossRanking and its group type is one of those five: the BossRanking, the room, the leader, the clear time in seconds (the time the play-time missions are scored with) and the date; its members (table boss-clear-member) are the players of the room's party in the field, led by the party leader when the leader is one of them, else by the first of them. When none of the room's party is in the field (the party broke up or was left during the run), or the room was entered without a party, the room's first user is kept alone when in the field; with nobody to keep, the game logs it at Debug. A second Clear of the same room (the state change repeating, a reload) keeps nothing. Colosseum rooms also have a BossRanking, but their board (0x10) asks for other types, so they are kept on their own (Colosseum line below); fame challenge rooms are kept for their own board (next line). A member goes with its character (a deleted character drops out of the rows and counts); the leader has no key to the character, and a row whose leader's character is gone shows its first member, as the client does; a clear with no member left is not shown. 0x51 and 0x5b rows are read as GameRankingItem::BossKillRecord reads them: rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the leader's character id, an int the client does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the clear time in seconds (shown as minutes and seconds), the date, and the members (character id, name, profile image, level and job code; the client keeps the first 10). 0x48 rows are rank, character id, name, profile image and three ints of which the client shows the second (the clears); the first goes out as the BossRanking and the third as 0, neither is used. Not settled by the client, chosen here: 0x51 is the earliest clears first, 0x5b the fastest first and then the earlier one, equal ones by the row id; 0x48 is the most clears first, equal counts by the earlier last clear, then the character id; the best 200 rows, and with a search name (0x48 only, as the client asks) the row of that character. No season is kept. Rooms ingested before this change have no BossRanking: re-ingest the dungeon rooms.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) fame challenge board: types 0x8d (141), 0x8e (142), 0x97 (151), 0xa1 (161) and 0xa2 (162) are answered from the same kept clears (the enum names FortressRumble* and their placeholder writers are replaced; their "S rank" and "early victory" reading was not the client's). The ranking dialog's board 0xc adds one tab per fameChallenge room with a BossRanking and asks with it, in three modes (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey, the dialog's tab labels): the clears per character (0x8e, the own rank 0x8d), the first clears (0x97, no own rank) and the fastest clears (0xa2, the own rank 0xa1). A clear of a fameChallenge room with a BossRanking is now kept as the boss clears are. 0x8e rows are GameRankingItem::BossRumbleCount, the same as 0x48 rows. 0x97 and 0xa2 rows are GameRankingItem::BossRumbleRecord, one character each (the shipped fame challenge rooms take one player, and the dialog's tooltip shows character info, not a party): rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the character id, name, profile image, level, job code, an int the client does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the clear time in seconds and the date. Not settled by the client, chosen here: a character has one row on 0x97 (its first clear, the earliest first) and on 0xa2 (its fastest clear, then the earlier one), so its own rank on 0xa2 is its place there; 0x8e is ordered as 0x48; the best 200 rows. The seasons of these rooms (seasonRankRewardID) are not kept. No re-ingest beyond the one the boss boards need.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) world boss board: types 0x6f (111), 0x70 (112), 0x79 (121), 0x83 (131) and 0x84 (132) are answered from the same kept clears; they were not in GameRankingType and went unanswered. The ranking dialog's board 0xb adds one tab per worldBossDungeon room with a BossRanking (the shipped one is 24090004, the Crimson Balrog field 02000410) and asks with it, in three modes (CGameRankingCli::BuildKey, the dialog's tab labels "Greatest Contribution", "Earliest Clear" and "Damage Dealt"): the clears per character (0x70, the own rank 0x6f), the first clears (0x79, no own rank) and the clears with the most damage (0x84, the own rank 0x83). A clear of a worldBossDungeon room with a BossRanking is now kept as the boss clears are (the room's first Clear: the field's 15-minute survival), and the row also keeps the party's damage (the migration AddBossClearDamage, a Damage column on boss-clear, 0 for the other rooms): the sum of the damage the room's players dealt to its bosses (npcs of class 3 and up, the server's IsBoss) before the room had its result. 0x70 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageCount, the same as 0x48 rows. 0x79 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageDate: rank, a long the client does not use (sent as the row id), the leader's character id, an int it does not use (sent as the BossRanking), the row's point as a long, the date and the members as on 0x51. 0x84 rows are GameRankingItem::BossDamageAmount: rank, a long not used (the row id), the leader's character id, a long not used (0), an int not used (the BossRanking), three longs of which the client shows the third as the point, a fourth long not used (0), an int shown as a rank point when not 0, the date and the members (the client shows the leader's name and image). Not settled by the client, chosen here: the point of 0x79 and 0x84 is the party's damage; the 0x84 rank point is sent as 0 (nothing on our side gives one); 0x79 is ordered as 0x51, 0x84 the most damage first, then the earlier clear, then the row id, and the own rank 0x83 is the place of the character's best clear there; 0x70 is ordered as 0x48, with a search name the row of that character (0x70 only, as the client asks); the best 200 rows. Not tried live.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) Colosseum board: types 0xcb (203), 0xcc (204), 0xc9 (201) and 0xca (202) are answered from the best Colosseum run per character and season (table colosseum-record, the migration AddColosseumRecord, removed with the character). The ranking dialog's board 0x10 has a tab for the current Colosseum season and up to three seasons before it (seasondata_bosscolosseum, weekly), each with a tab for every job and one per job code, shown only when a colosseum room has a BossRanking; the current season's tab asks for 0xcb (0xc9 for the own rank) with no season, the others for 0xcc (0xca) with the season id, and the request's ranking id is the job tab (0 for every job). The current season is the one whose start and end hold the time. The first end of a colosseum room with a BossRanking keeps the run of its first user, when in the field and it cleared a round: the rounds of dungeon_clear_round and the room's seconds (the time the play-time missions are scored with), in the season that holds the time (none outside every season); dungeon_disable_ranking (the continued game) keeps nothing. It replaces the character's best of that season with more rounds, or the same rounds in fewer seconds (an equal run keeps the older record). Rows are read as GameRanking::BossColosseum reads them: rank, character id, name, profile image, two ints and a long the client does not show (sent as 0), the rounds (shown as the points), the seconds (shown as minutes and seconds), an int not shown (0) and two time strings not shown (the time the run was set). Not settled by the client, chosen here: most rounds first, then fewer seconds, then the earlier record, then the character id; the best 200 rows, and with a search name the row of that character. A second run in the same room (the script goes back to its start after each end) is not kept: the room's seconds would count both runs.
- House commendation and web ranking (irrq.aspx) house board: the home info dialog's recommend button sends RequestCube 0x19 (CommendHome), which the server left unanswered. The server now keeps the commendation (table home-commend, the migration AddHomeCommend; the rows go with the home and with the commending account) when the player stands in a home that is not its own and has not commended today: the client's rule for the button (`IsNotRecentDayChange`: no commendation yet, one more than a day ago or one on an earlier calendar day of the server's time zone) is checked again. The refusals are ResponseCube 0x19 with a ShowBuildNotice code: 76 outside a home, 78 for the own home, 81 for a second one that day, 77 when the row is not saved. The voter gets 0x19 with its account and the commendation time (the client keeps it and turns the button off), the field gets the home's scores (ResponseCube 0x1c, the dialog's "current / total") and HomeCommand 1 for the owner (the client finds the owner's PC by object id and refreshes its MyInfo dialog), sent to the field the owner is in, on any channel: the new scores go to the other channels through the World (FieldPlotRequest architect_score), which set the loaded home's and the online owner's copies; the commend_home condition is counted. HomeCommand 0 at login now carries the account's last commendation time instead of 0, so the button is off after a relog the same day. A home's architect scores (Home, the field entry) are its commendations of the current month and of all time; at the monthly reset each channel reads them again for its loaded homes and online owners, so the month's score starts from 0. The ranking dialog's board 6 asks for 0x3f (0x3d for the own rank) for the current month without a date and 0x40 (0x3e) with the yyyymm month for the months before (`CGameRankingCli::BuildKey`, FUN_140b3df90); these four types were not answered. Rows are read as GameRanking::UgcMapCommendation reads them: rank, the owner's character id (portrait), name and profile image, the commendations (shown as the points), an int the client does not use (0), the owner's account id and the home's name (the outdoor plot's name, else the indoor one). The own rank is the asking account's home. Not settled by the client, chosen here: most commendations first, then the home that reached its count first, then the account id; the owner's character is its last played one, a home whose account has no character is left out; the best 200 rows, and with a search name the row of that character's home.
- Web ranking (irrq.aspx) requests of type 0: the ranking dialog sends a request whose key type is 0 when the board it shows has no tab. `UIGameRankingDialog::BuildRankingKey` and `BuildMyRankKey` return before `CGameRankingCli::BuildKey` without a tab, and the season boards (Dark Stream 3, Colosseum 0x10 and the others with a SeasonRankType) only get tabs from a season of the client's own Table/SeasonData_<type>.xml whose start and end hold the server time (`CSeasonDataTable::Refresh`, `GetUpcoming`). The client has no answer reader for type 0, so nothing the server sends can fill such a board: the fix is a season in the played client's table. The server used to answer these requests with a 400, which made the client send them twice more, 500 ms apart; it now sends the empty answer it gives a board with nothing to serve, and logs at Information that the played client's season table has no season at the server time. Reading the request body is now one place (GameRankingRequest), with tests built from the client's writer.

## Two local changes a server operator must know

1. **All servers bind to loopback (127.0.0.1) by default.** To serve other machines, set `MS2_BIND_IP` to the address to listen on (`0.0.0.0` for all interfaces, as upstream); the server has no hardening for the open internet beyond upstream's.
2. **Test hooks.** Two environment variables turn on test hooks. Neither does anything unless it is set.
   - `TST_CMD_FILE`: once a second, the game server reads a file named after this prefix and the character id, and runs each line as a chat command **as the logged-in player**. Never set it on a server that others can reach.
   - `TST_PKT_DIR`: the server appends every packet that clients send, including chat, to a log file per character in this directory.

**LOCALE must be the played client's locale.** The server takes script branches and locale rows from LOCALE, the client from its own region; with a KR client and LOCALE=NA, for example, the client lets an outfit with no break row be dismantled (its KR script gives lucky bag coupons) while the server's NA branch previews and pays nothing. The ingest prints a warning when MS2_PLAYED_CLIENT_XML is set and its pack's region (its one table/<region>/ folder) is not LOCALE.

## Errata in commit subjects

Published commits are not rewritten, so wrong numbers in their subjects are listed here.

- `6fea6fcf` and `fce39da6` say "Guild 0x0054". The opcode is `RecvOp.Guild = 0x004B`.
- `e319062d` says "RequestPet 0x00C2". The opcode is `RecvOp.RequestPet = 0x0078`.
